# 07. Glossary and Adopted Decisions

## Terms
- **OCR** — recognizing text from images.
- **Tasks** (ADR 162; the release cut `pageOcr` and `rawPageOcr` — ADR 252) —
  the four model tasks a profile declares by string id; the same ids name the
  profile files (`roles.<task>`), the enum (`ConnectionRole::BlockRecognition,
  Decision, Layout, Fix`), the settings and the QML. The list of the four ids
  lives in `core/ConnectionRole.h` and nowhere else (ADR 215).
  **blockRecognition** — single-block transcription (plain: the type prompt +
  the crop, no candidate — the previous text is not sent, ADR 163 superseding
  ADR 152's recheck framing); **decision** — the fast yes/no gate
  (`/v1/systemone`, ADR 145/147; since ADR 254 the visual diff
  `systemone_visual_diff_v1` is its only protocol); **layout** — the manual
  page-markup pass (ADR 149); **fix** — a universal model confirms / corrects /
  refuses the candidate (OK/FIX/REVIEW, ADR 163). Per-task settings live in
  `tasks/<task>/{modelPath, mmprojPath, draftPath, requestProfileId,
  modelName}` — blockRecognition's launch files keep the legacy `check`
  prefix; `check/` keeps only the behavior key `showConfidence` and
  `decision/matchThreshold` stays (the `autoCheck`/`autoFix` switches are
  gone, ADR 178). Every task has its own request-profile store (user files
  `requestBlock.json`, `requestDecision.json`, `requestLayout.json`,
  `requestFix.json`; legacy `request.json` / `requestValidate.json` are
  renamed on load; QML singletons `RequestProfilesBlock` …
  `RequestProfilesFix`). Model ↔ task assignment, downloads and management
  live in the «Модели» window. The legacy vocabulary survives where renaming
  would cost user data or churn: `CheckController`/`CheckRequest`/
  `CheckResult`, the queue's `Phase::Recheck`; the registry index renames
  the legacy `"check"` tag on load and drops every tag that is not a
  canonical role name — an entry left without a role waits for
  re-activation in Settings → Models (ADR 146); the unknown-name fallback
  to BlockRecognition lives in `connectionRoleFromName`, the settings/QML
  dispatch.
- **OCR model (adapter)** — the per-model recipe (`models/OcrModel.h`): how to
  talk to a specific OCR LLM — prompt variants, the default output parser, and
  (via the base class) the OpenAI-style chat-completions request body and
  response parsing. Created by `OcrModel::create(modelId, role)` from the
  task's model id (default recipe `mineru`); the adapters of the removed
  models are gone with them (ADR 251) — the surviving recipes are the model
  profiles in `resources/profiles/models/`.
- **ConnectionConfig** — the **transport** settings struct (base URL, API key,
  connection timeout, response timeout), formerly `ProviderConfig`. It is
  separate from the model settings: when a recognition starts,
  `RecognitionController` assembles an `OcrRequest`
  (model + prompt + request-body parameters) from `SettingsStore` and a
  `ConnectionConfig` (connection) for the transport.
- **LlamaClient** — the thin HTTP transport (`core/LlamaClient.h`): joins the
  `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint URL (`endpointUrl`), pre-flights the TCP
  connect against the connection timeout, POSTs JSON with an optional bearer
  token and a whole-request response timeout, supports `abort()`, and
  surfaces the server's error message. The former provider layer was reduced
  to exactly this (ADR 58, ADR 148).
- **OcrRequest** — the per-request payload: `image`, `prompt`, `modelId`, and
  the ordered request-body parameters (`RequestParameter`). Defined in
  `core/OcrRequest.h`.
- **DRY sampling parameters** — llama.cpp's "Don't Repeat Yourself" sampler
  settings (`dry_multiplier`, `dry_base`, `dry_allowed_length`,
  `dry_penalty_last_n`), part of the **request profile** and sent in the request
  body. Tuned originally for the Unlimited-OCR model (now in the archive,
  ADR 251).
- **Output parser** — the strategy for parsing a model's response into a
  structured result (`raw`, the layout markup parser `mineru-layout`; default
  `auto`), selected in Settings → Output. The parsers that existed only for
  the removed models live in `archive/parsers/` (ADR 251).
- **BlockStyle** — mapping from model block labels (`title`, `image`, `chart`,
  `equation`, `table`, `ref_text`, captions, …) to Markdown rendering styles
  (`BlockStyle.h`).
- **bbox** — bounding box coordinates of a recognized fragment for overlay on
  the preview (produced by the model's own parser).
- **Block status** — the per-box verification state, shown as the dot to the
  left of the box (ADR 164, two layers): every block carries `box.text` (the
  OCR result) and `box.correctedText` (the fix model's correction); the dot
  reflects the **effective** layer (the correction when present, else the
  text) and answers one question — is the text you are looking at verified?
  A markup block without text shows no dot; **gray** = recognized, no verdict
  on that layer (including «исправлено» — a stored correction without its own
  verdict yet); **green** = the decision model confirmed it (Ok);
  **red** = rejected (Mismatch) or unreadable/damaged (Review). The amber
  re-recognized state is removed (`BoxCheckStatus::ReRecognized` deleted
  without renumbering; superseded ADR 153's two-variant semantics). The
  confidence badge follows the same layer. The box hue follows the block
  kind, not the status: text/table/formula/image each have their own fill +
  frame color.
- **Image/chart block** — a box labeled `image` or `chart`;
  editable on the preview (move / resize / delete) and exported as a cropped
  image.
- **Markdown preview** — client-side render of the current page's Markdown via
  Qt WebEngine + marked + KaTeX, toggled in the right text pane.
- **RAG** — Retrieval-Augmented Generation; here — indexing scans into a vector DB.
- **Document / page model** — `DocumentModel` holds pages; `PageListModel` feeds
  the thumbnail strip (recognized/edited/duplicate/current flags, no boxes).
- **Recognized marker** — per-page indicator showing whether a page has been
  OCR'd yet; edited pages and pages with duplicate boxes carry additional
  markers.
- **Connection mode** — `External` (connect to an existing OpenAI-compatible
  server; the app launches nothing) or `Managed` (the app starts and owns a
  local `llama-server`). Chosen by the first-run wizard; existing profiles
  default to `External`.
- **ResolvedConnection** — the ready-to-use connection returned by
  `RuntimeController::ensureConnectionReady()`: `baseUrl` + `apiKey` +
  `modelId` + the two timeouts (ADR 148). In `External` it mirrors settings;
  in `Managed` it is computed from the started server (loopback URL +
  `--alias`).
- **RuntimeController** — the managed-runtime facade (QML singleton `Runtime`,
  created once in `main.cpp`, ADR 36): owns connection resolution, server
  lifecycle, self-test, memory estimation; QML must not instantiate it.
- **AppBusyState** — the app-wide exclusive busy state: `Idle`,
  `StartingRuntime`, `Recognizing`, `StoppingRuntime`, `Downloading`,
  `Installing`.
- **RuntimeState** — the managed-server state machine: `NotConfigured → Stopped
  → Starting → Ready → Stopping → Stopped`, with `Failed`;
  drives `canRecognize` with `configValid`.
- **ensureConnectionReady()** — the single async entry point that recognition
  uses; External resolves immediately, Managed starts the server, waits for
  `/health`, queries `/v1/models` and verifies the alias. Callback-based
  (review 3.3): concurrent callers register a callback and share one in-flight
  resolve (dedup, ADR 37).
- **runSelfTest() / runSelfTestQml()** — an independent end-to-end check
  (start → health → `/v1/models` → one OCR request with a built-in test image),
  used by the wizard's Launch step.
- **alias** — the `--alias <name>` given to the managed `llama-server`; it
  becomes the `modelId` clients use (`/v1/models` must report it).
- **Managed / External (runtime)** — see **Connection mode**. “Managed
  runtime/model” also marks files inside the app's `models/` dir (removable),
  vs **external** user-provided GGUF files (never deleted, only listed).
- **Model preset** — a downloadable entry `repo + revision + model/mmproj/mtp
  files + sha256` expanded from a model profile's quantization list
  (`ModelPresetCatalog::expand`, one preset per quant; e.g. MinerU Q8_0),
  merged with the user's `catalog.json` by `id` (ADR 42/43). The preset
  installs the files of its `profileId` — the profile owns the role, the
  launch and the parser (ADR 58).
- **Request profile** — the ordered set of request-body parameters sent to
  llama.cpp next to `model`/`messages` (DRY params, temperature, max_tokens,
  stream, …). Built-ins are expanded from the model profiles serving the task
  (`ModelProfiles::requestWithMaxOutput`); per-task user copies live in
  `<AppData>/LLocr/profiles/requestBlock.json`, `requestDecision.json`,
  `requestLayout.json`, `requestFix.json`, merged by `id` (ADR 56); the
  **active profile follows the task's model choice** (blockRecognition:
  `model/recipeId`; decision/layout/fix: `tasks/<task>/requestProfileId`) —
  unknown ids fall back to the first built-in profile (ADR 59). One
  `RequestProfileStore` per task (QML singletons `RequestProfilesBlock` …
  `RequestProfilesFix`) owns them.
- **Launch profile** — a named llama-server command-line preset (ctx-size,
  n-gpu-layers, flags, …) tagged with `os`/`backend`. Built-in presets ship in
  `:/profiles/serverLaunch.json`; per-preset user copies live in
  `<AppData>/LLocr/profiles/serverLaunch.json` (full copies, so user deletions
  stick). The selection (`launch/profileId`) auto-follows the installed
  runtime backend (ADR 57); `LaunchProfileStore` (QML singleton
  `LaunchProfiles`) owns it. `--model/--mmproj/--alias/--host/--port` are
  never part of a profile.
- **ModelRegistry** — persists installed models in `<modelsDir>/index.json`;
  distinguishes managed vs external, recovers by rescan, guards removal.
- **GGUF** — llama.cpp's model format; vision models consist of a main
  `.gguf` (+ optional `mmproj` projector; multi-part `model-00001-of-N` files
  are supported).

## Adopted decisions (ADR-lite)
| #    | Decision                                                     | Reason                                                       |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 1    | Qt6/C++/QML stack                                            | Existing skill, covers all GUI requirements                  |
| 2    | CMake instead of qmake                                       | Standard for cross-platform C++                              |
| 3    | RAG as a separate Python service                             | Python's ecosystem is stronger for ML/RAG                    |
| 4    | Build the OpenAI-compatible provider first                   | Supported by almost all local runners                        |
| 5    | Internal format — Markdown, export via Pandoc                | Cheap support for many formats                               |
| 6    | RAG deferred, only interface design sketched               | Working OCR first. Backend RAG interface stub not implemented yet — see Stage 4.
| 7    | PDF via Qt PDF (`QPdfDocument`), not MuPDF/Poppler           | No extra native dependency; bundled with Qt6; adequate at ~150 DPI. |
| 8    | Recognition is cancellable (`abort()`)                       | Users must be able to Stop long multi-page runs.             |
| 9    | Navigation stays live during recognition                     | Users browse other pages while a batch run proceeds.         |
| 10   | Export written directly (TXT/MD/HTML); Markdown-as-source + Pandoc for DOCX/PDF | Ship useful export now; converge on the single-source pipeline. |
| 11   | Thumbnails carry no bounding boxes                           | The strip only signals recognized/edited/current; boxes belong on the center preview only. |
| 12   | OpenAI-like(llama.cpp-like)-compatible only                  | One clear connection type; connection/model/parser/request-body params live in `SettingsStore` (Settings dialog + `QSettings`). Historically the prompt was preset-driven; since ADR 58 it belongs to the **OCR model adapter**. |
| 13   | Window geometry persisted (`WindowSettings` + `SettingsStore` `ui/*`) | Persist window pos/size/visibility across sessions. |
| 14   | Unit tests live in `tests/` (Qt Test), gated by `LLOCR_BUILD_TESTS` | Early coverage for parsers; more to come. |
| 15   | JSON model-profile classes (`ModelProfile` / `ProfileRepository`) removed | Stage 2 replaced them with the **Settings dialog**; the leftover classes were dead code and were deleted. |
| 16   | i18n via Qt Linguist (`qsTr`/`tr` + `.ts`/`.qm`), language selectable in Settings (System/English/Русский), default **System**, runtime retranslate | Standard Qt i18n; persisted via `SettingsStore` (`ui/language`); embedded `.qm` loaded at startup and on change. |
| 17   | The **layout-token parser** consumes the `<\|det\|>…<\|/det\|>` block stream and JSON-unescapes the content (`\n` → newline, `\\` → `\`, …); the legacy bare `label [x1,y1,x2,y2] text` stays a fallback | The model streams one block per token with JSON-escaped text; the legacy form is kept for older responses. No `<PAGE>` splitting — one page is sent per request. |
| 18   | The parser also strips model control tokens from content — `<\|end_of_sentence\|>` (ASCII and full-width-pipe `｜ U+FF5C` + `▁ U+2581` variants) and any stray `<\|…\|>` wrappers | The model appends an EOS marker after the last block; it must not leak into the recognized text. |
| 19   | The `table` block style is recognized and rendered as a **GFM pipe table** (`\| a \| b \|`) built from the model's `<table>` HTML. `colspan`/`rowspan` are laid into a dense grid, but — since GFM has no native spanning — a spanned value is written **once**, into the top-left cell it covers, and the remaining covered cells are left empty (not repeated). A row made of a single wide cell (e.g. the model's `End-to-end Model (v1.x)` band) is treated as a **full-width section header**: it starts at the first column and is not offset by an active `rowspan` from above. Cell math is converted via `convertMath`, and `|` / newlines inside cells are escaped. `table_caption`/`table_footnote` render italic like figure captions. The **«Keep tables as HTML»** setting (`output/tablesAsHtml`, default off) bypasses the flattening entirely: the model's `<table>` block is passed through verbatim, since most Markdown editors accept inline HTML and this preserves `colspan`/`rowspan` | The model emits table content as inline HTML (`<table><tr><td …`) inside the `table` det token; raw HTML must not leak into the markdown, so it is parsed into a pipe table. Repeating a spanned value across every covered cell produced unreadable output (a full-width band repeated 8×, and `rowspan` cells duplicated onto a following section-header row); pandoc/turndown likewise write the value once and leave the rest blank. |
| 20   | The `ref_text` det-token (bibliography/reference list) parses like `text` and renders as a plain paragraph | The model labels reference entries (`[1] …`, `[2] …`) as `ref_text`; they are ordinary text runs (no heading/italic markers), keep their `ref_text` label, and get no extra Markdown styling. |
| 21   | **DRY sampling parameters** (`dry_multiplier`, `dry_base`, `dry_allowed_length`, `dry_penalty_last_n`) exposed in **Settings → Model** and sent in the request body | They materially affect recognition quality with llama.cpp for the Unlimited-OCR model; users can tune them without recompiling. |
| 22   | **Markdown preview** rendered client-side via **Qt WebEngine + bundled marked + KaTeX** (`resources/preview/`) | LaTeX/tables/images render locally with no network access; `image://ocr/crop/*` refs are converted to `data:` URIs before rendering. |
| 23   | Pages can be **deleted** and **drag-reordered** in the thumbnail strip; `PageEditStore` and `PageListModel` remap indices afterwards | Multi-page documents need page management; edits must follow their page across reorder/removal. |
| 24   | **Image/chart block editing** (move / resize / delete) directly on the preview; `rebuildPageText()` regenerates the page Markdown from the boxes | Editing regions is more convenient than re-running OCR; markdown image refs embed the box index, so removal forces a rebuild to keep `image://ocr/crop/<N>` indices consistent. |
| 25   | Detected **duplicate** bounding boxes are collapsed and flagged (`OcrPage::hasDuplicates`, `PageListModel` duplicate role → red marker) | The model can emit the same region twice; the parser dedups it and the UI surfaces it. |
| 26   | Two connection modes `External` / `Managed`; the transport stays decoupled from `QProcess` (historically `OpenAiProvider`, since ADR 58 `LlamaClient` + `OcrModel`) | Don't break the existing external-server scenario; process management is a separate responsibility. |
| 27   | The managed server is **only llama.cpp** (`llama-server`), minimum build **b4000** | A single predictable CLI; Ollama/LM Studio have their own managers; below b4000 the CLI drifts too much. |
| 28   | Runtime installs come from **GitHub Releases** (`ggml-org/llama.cpp`) and are unpacked by our own code (vendored `miniz`), verifying `sha256` — the release-body table, with the API per-asset `digest` field as the primary source since ADR 236 | Reproducibility, no external package manager; file size is *not* an integrity proof. |
| 29   | Models download **directly from Hugging Face**, with the **commit SHA pinned** and `sha256` verified against `lfs.oid` | No Python dependency; `main` is mutable between browsing and downloading. |
| 30   | No-orphan: **strong** on Windows (Job Object + `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_CLOSE`) and Linux (`prctl(PR_SET_PDEATHSIG, SIGTERM)` via `ProcessGuard`), **best-effort** on macOS (`owner.json` + next-start detection) | macOS has no PDEATHSIG analog; honest formulation over a false promise (§5.4). |
| 31   | First-run wizard driven purely by `runtime/setupVersion`, **no network health probes**; pre-existing profiles are treated as configured (`setupVersion=1`), a clean profile gives `setupVersion=0` | A network probe is unreliable (VPN, powered-off server) and must not decide setup state; the version allows the wizard to evolve. |
| 32   | In `Managed`, `baseUrl` and `modelId` are **computed** from the running server (loopback + `--alias`); the `model/name` setting is never overwritten | The running process is the single source of truth; `External` settings must be preserved for switching back. |
| 33   | ⛔ No automatic attach to a process listening on the configured port | `/health==200` proves neither process identity nor the model; a foreign process cannot be owned — a fixed busy port is a hard error (§5 task 4). |
| 34   | ZIP **and** `.tar.gz` archives are supported; dispatch by extension in `ArchiveExtractor::extractArchive` | llama.cpp publishes `.zip` on Windows but `.tar.gz` on macOS and Linux (`llama-b<build>-bin-macos-<arch>.tar.gz`, `llama-b<build>-bin-ubuntu-*.tar.gz`); on macOS there is no ZIP asset at all. macOS tarballs contain symlinked `.dylib`s that the extractor recreates as relative links (ADR amended after the 2026 asset-format change). |
| 35   | ⛔ No automatic removal of `com.apple.quarantine` | Never bypass OS protection for downloaded executables; `QNetworkAccessManager` downloads are usually not quarantined anyway (§7.8). |
| 36   | `RuntimeController` is a singleton instance created in `main.cpp` before the QML engine loads; QML cannot instantiate it | Deterministic lifetime; no duplicate instances. |
| 37   | `RecognitionController` obtains the connection only through `ensureConnectionReady()` (External resolves immediately; Managed starts/waits later) | It must not know about modes, processes, or health checks. |
| 38   | The managed server binds **loopback only** (`127.0.0.1`/`::1`); other hosts require `runtime/allowNonLoopback=true` (advanced) with a clear warning | An unauthenticated server must not be reachable from the network; client `baseUrl` stays `127.0.0.1` even when bound elsewhere. |
| 39   | Runtime installation is **transactional**: staging → verify → probe → atomic rename → settings commit (ADR 28/34) | Eliminates partially-installed runtimes; any failure removes `staging/<uuid>` and leaves settings untouched. |
| 40   | `.part` downloads always live in the **target directory**, never in a shared `downloads/` | A rename across filesystems is not atomic — models are often kept on an external drive (§3.1). |
| 41   | Capability detection: build-allowlist + parsed `--help` refinement + one auto-retry without an offending flag on `unknown argument`; cached as `capabilities-<sha1(path+mtime)>.json` | llama.cpp's CLI drifts between builds (e.g. `--flash-attn` boolean → `on|off|auto`); blind argv building breaks compatibility. |
| 42   | Preset catalogs are split: built-in `:/models/default-presets.json` (read-only) + user `<AppData>/…/catalog.json` (read/write), merged by `id` (user wins) | Files inside Qt resources cannot be edited; the user must be able to add/customize presets. |
| 43   | A preset pins the exact pair `model + mmproj + parser + prompt + ctx-size` | `det_tokens` and the prompt are model-specific; an arbitrary vision GGUF yields unparseable output. |
| 44   | `autoStart` defaults to **off** | Otherwise the GUI would reserve several GB of RAM/VRAM on every launch, even when idle. |
| 45   | `Authorization` header is **dropped on any redirect to a different host** | Otherwise the HF token would leak to the CDN/host the redirect points at (§7.3). |
| 46   | Split locks instead of one global app lock: **`.install.lock`** (runtime installs/cleanup, in `RuntimeInstaller`), per-write **`.registry.lock`** (`ModelRegistry`), and **`.instance.lock`** as the **runtime-owner** gate (`SingleInstanceGuard`) | A second app instance can keep using External / browsing while runtime installs and server ownership stay exclusive; no single lock blocks everything (H.6). The lock is **re-checked when the Runtime settings tab opens** (`RuntimeController::refreshSingleInstanceLock()`): a window that started while another instance owned the runtime takes over as soon as the owner exits — no app restart needed. |
| 47   | Strong-quality no-orphan on macOS: the **watchdog-helper (kqueue/NOTE_EXIT) is closed — not shipped** in the MVP; the best-effort contract stays (`owner.json` + next-start detection, ADR 30) | Low value (only a hard-kill of the GUI on macOS, one residual server process, already detected at next start) vs high complexity (separate bundled binary, signing, poor testability) — unjustified for the MVP (H.4, §5.4). |
| 48   | Moving `provider/apiKey` and `hf/token` to the OS keychain is **done (ADR 227)** — they live in the OS secret store, and the plaintext copy is gone from the settings file | The deferral was "not worth the effort now (H.5)"; the effort turned out to be three small system backends, and the price of the deferral was a plaintext working key in the settings file. |
| 49   | Self-test and the server-log ring-buffer are **separate QML singletons** (`SelfTestController` / `RuntimeLog`), not `RuntimeController` roles | `RuntimeController` was a ~20-property "kitchen sink" facade; splitting removes the temptation to duplicate self-test/log logic (review 3.4). |
| 50   | `SettingsStore::resetToDefaults()` is **table-driven**: one `SettingDefault{kQSettingsKey, Q_PROPERTY name, QVariant default}` row per resettable key, written through `QMetaProperty::write()` (i.e. the setter — guards, validation, NOTIFY preserved) | A manually-maintained setter list drifted from the property declarations; the table is the single registry, `ui/window*` (UI state) is deliberately excluded, order keeps `baseUrl` before `connectionMode` and `lastExternalBaseUrl` last (review 3.5). |
| 51   | The `--version` + `--help` probe shares **one wall-clock budget**: `--version` gets the full `timeoutMs`, `--help` the remainder; if the budget is exhausted `--help` is skipped and flags fall back to the build allowlist | Previously each run got a fresh `timeoutMs`, so a hung binary could stall the UI for `2 × timeoutMs` (10 s worst case with the 5 s startServer timeout); the whole probe is now bounded by `timeoutMs` (§H.7 follow-up). |
| 52   | **Persistent capabilities cache**: successful probes are written to `<cacheDir>/capabilities-<sha1(path+mtime+size)>.json` and served by `probeCached(binary, cacheDir)` on the next app run | The in-memory probe cache only lasts a process; without a disk layer every app start re-spawned `--version`/`--help`. The key intentionally matches the in-memory `ProbeKey` strength (path+mtime+size) so any binary replacement invalidates. `probe()` (fresh “Check”) and the install path stay uncached by design (ADR 41). |
| 53   | Probing llama.cpp binaries uses a **long single-run timeout (120 s)** in the install flow AND in `RuntimeController` (Check, Start, Auto-detect), instead of the regular 5 s probe, and does **not** kill/retry | On macOS the FIRST run of a llama.cpp build past a cold Metal shader cache compiles kernel libraries (`ggml_metal_library_init` writes ~20–30 MB under `com.apple.metal`); until it finishes, `--version` blocks (observed ~17 s cold on an M-series Mac, longer under load; afterwards the cache is warm and probes answer in ~50 ms). A 5 s probe always fails on a cold cache; a retry loop that kills each attempt at 5 s repeatedly restarts the compile and never lets the cache finish. One patient run completes the cache; all later probes are fast. Warm-cache starts are unaffected (the 120 s budget is only a ceiling). |
| 54   | **Windows build toolchain = MSVC 2022 64-bit** (Qt Creator kit “Desktop Qt 6.10.3 MSVC2022 64bit”, generator `NMake Makefiles JOM`, Qt package `C:/Qt/6.10.3/msvc2022_64`); MinGW is **not** supported | The Qt **MinGW** package for Windows does **not** ship the **Qt WebEngine** module (Markdown preview, `resources/preview/`); the MSVC 2022 64-bit package does. Any MinGW-configured kit/project (e.g. `.qtcreator/CMakeLists.txt.user` with `win64_mingw_kit`) is switched to `win64_msvc2022_64_kit` and fails the build cleanly on WebEngine otherwise. macOS/Linux are unaffected (Clang/GCC). |
| 55   | Restored window position is **validated against the current screens** (`WindowSettings::visiblePosition`): the saved x/y is applied only while the window's title-bar band overlaps a connected screen, otherwise the window is centered on the **nearest** screen | A saved position from a previous session can point off-screen (monitor unplugged, resolution/arrangement changed, window closed while dragged past an edge) — the window would open with an unreachable title bar and couldn't be moved at all. Amendment to ADR 13. |
| 56   | **Request profiles** replace the individual request settings (`model/temperature`, `model/maxTokens`, `model/dry*`): the request body (after `model`/`messages`) is assembled in profile order — built-in `:/profiles/request.json` + user `<AppData>/LLocr/profiles/request.json`, merged by name (user wins; new built-in parameters appear automatically), file written only when values differ from the defaults. UI = table on Settings → Request; edits touch the draft, **Save** commits, **Cancel**/reopen discards; **Restore defaults** loads the default profile (committed on Save). Values keep their JSON kind; `dry_sequence_breakers` default is `["\uE000"]` — the server (llama.cpp `server-schema.cpp`) **rejects an empty array** with 400 «dry_sequence_breakers must be a non-empty array of strings», and the private-use character never matches, keeping the breakers effectively disabled (the old `\uE000` hack was exactly that) | One place for the request layout instead of six scattered QSettings keys (server-launch profiles follow the same pattern next); `QJsonObject` sorts keys alphabetically on the wire anyway, so `order` governs the profile/table, not the literal body order. |
| 57   | **Launch profiles** replace the `launch/*` parameter settings (`ctxSize`, `gpuLayers`, `threads`, `batchSize`, `parallel`, `flashAttn`, `cacheType*`, `noMmap`, `jinja`, `extraArgs`): `ServerLaunchConfig` emits the core flags (`--model/--mmproj/--alias/--host/--port`, single-sourced) then the active profile's rows verbatim (`--name [value]`, flags valueless, names starting with `-` passed through, reserved names skipped). Multiple built-in presets in `:/profiles/serverLaunch.json` tagged `os`/`backend`; the selection `launch/profileId` **auto-switches** (persisted) to a preset matching the installed backend (`runtime/backend`, falling back to platform detection when no runtime is installed). Settings → Launch tab: preset combobox + table with add/remove; draft discipline as ADR 56; per-preset user copies (full copies — deletions stick, preset updates reach only untouched presets) in `<AppData>/LLocr/profiles/serverLaunch.json`; `estimateModelMemory` reads `ctx-size`/`cache-type-*` from the active profile | Profiles can be updated by releases, cover per-OS/backend differences the flat settings could not, and free-form add/remove beats a fixed settings schema; the memory estimator and the wizard keep working by reading the profile. |
| 58   | The **provider concept is removed**: `ILlmProvider`/`OpenAiProvider` deleted. Two orthogonal abstractions remain: **`OcrModel`** (`src/models/`) — per-OCR-LLM recipe (prompt variants, default parser, request-body building and response parsing via virtual hooks), instantiated by `OcrModelFactory` from the `model/recipeId` setting; and **`LlamaClient`** (`src/core/`) — thin transport (`endpointUrl` + POST/timeout/abort/server-error extraction). `ProviderConfig` renamed to **`ConnectionConfig`**; `OcrRequest` moved to `core/OcrRequest.h`; the `prompt` Q_PROPERTY removed from `AppController` (the prompt belongs to the model adapter). The persisted `QSettings` keys with the `provider/` prefix are **unchanged** (no profile migration) | Only llama.cpp remains as the server; its API is OpenAI-compatible, so a provider abstraction had no second implementation. Request/response handling was really model knowledge — it moves into the model hierarchy, giving one-class-per-LLM extensibility (future DeepSeek-OCR etc.). Parsers stay decoupled in `src/parsers/` and are referenced by id from the adapter. |
| 59   | **Request profiles become per-model**: the request-profile file is an array keyed by a unique profile `id` (equal to the OCR model id — `unlimited-ocr` today, `deepseek-ocr` etc. later); the **active profile is derived from `model/recipeId`** (no extra `QSettings` key; unknown ids fall back to the first built-in profile). Built-in `:/profiles/request.json` (`schemaVersion` 2) + per-profile user copies in `<AppData>/LLocr/profiles/request.json`, merged by parameter name per profile (merge semantics of ADR 56 kept). Legacy single-profile files (v1, no `profiles` array) are adopted as the default model's profile — user values survive the upgrade. Settings: the **OCR model combobox moved from the Output tab to the Request tab**; switching it switches the *draft* profile immediately (table re-reads), everything persists on the dialog **Save** (existing draft discipline); `onAccepted` writes `Settings.modelRecipeId = RequestProfiles.draftProfileId`. **Restore defaults** drops only the edited profile's user copy (per-profile, like the Launch tab) | Each LLM needs its own request layout; deriving the active profile from the selected model makes the switch automatic and stateless. The groundwork is the profile array itself — adding a model later means adding one adapter + one built-in profile with the same id. |
| 60   | **Installed llama.cpp builds are switchable without a re-download**: `InstallTransaction::scanInstalledBuilds()` enumerates the `<runtimeDir>/llama.cpp-*` directories (parses build/backend from the tag, locates `llama-server`), and `RuntimeInstaller::activateBuild(index)` makes one active with a **settings-only commit** (`serverPath` + `installedBuild` + `runtimeBackend`); UI = an "Installed builds" list with an **Activate** button in Settings → Runtime and in the wizard's Runtime step; "Clean up unused builds" is **refused while no build is active** (a sweep with an empty keep-tag would delete every download) | A settings reset wipes `runtime/serverPath`/`installedBuild`/`runtime/backend`, but the downloaded build directories stay on disk — the app then demanded a full re-download of llama.cpp. Activation restores a previously downloaded build in one click (mirrors the Models tab's installed-model activation, ADR 46/H.6 discipline untouched: installs keep the exclusive lock, activation is a pure settings write). |
| 61   | **A running managed server is the source of truth for recognition**: `ensureConnectionReady()` applies the settings-based `configValid` gate **only when a start would be needed** — when the server is already Ready/Starting the resolve goes straight to the `/v1/models` alias check (with the §4.2 first-model fallback). In exchange, `startServer()` **refuses a Managed start without a selected model** ("Model is not selected — pick a model in Settings → Models or in the Setup wizard") so a model-less Ready server can no longer arise; the `/v1/models` "no models" failure also points at Settings → Models; `translateServerLine()`/`startServer()` messages switched from `QObject::tr()` to `tr()` | After a settings reset (or activation of another build) `launch/modelPath` can be stale/empty while the server keeps running with a loaded model — recognition refused a fully usable server with the misleading "Managed server is not configured". The running process already proves the configuration (ADR 32); a start without a model is the only way to reach the broken half-state, so it is blocked instead (§7.5). Side fix: `QObject::tr()` inside a subclass resolves the **QObject** context at runtime, so the §7.5 matrix could never pick up its translations — `tr()` restores the `llocr::RuntimeController` context. |
| 62   | **Model selection survives a settings reset without a re-download**: `ModelInstaller` reads the registry through **fresh `RuntimePaths`** (the models-dir override can change mid-session; the stale ctor copy showed an empty Models tab after a reset), exposes `refreshInstalled()` (registry re-read; `load()` rebuilds only a missing/corrupt index — `rescanRegistry()` stays manual because a rescan would drop index entries not confirmed by files, e.g. hand-registered external models) and `activatePreset(index)` (activates the installed model matching a preset by repo + model file name). UI: the **wizard's Model step gains an "Installed models" list with Activate** (parity with the Models tab) and an installed preset's button turns from a dead-end disabled "Install" into **Activate** (same in ModelsTab); the start/recognition refusals **name the recorded model path** ("Model file not found: %1 — re-select the model in Settings → Models") | After a reset the wizard could not re-select an installed model at all (Install disabled, no list) — the user believed the model was selected while `launch/modelPath` stayed empty, and the start failed with a bare "Model is not selected". Selection must be reachable where the user is looking, and refusals must show which path was recorded. |
| 63   | **HTML/PDF export reuses the preview render pipeline** (`ExportRenderer`): a headless `QWebEnginePage` loads `qrc:/preview/export.html` (marked + DOMPurify + KaTeX — the same bundle as the on-screen preview), pages are appended one by one via `runJavaScript`, crops are embedded as `data:` URLs, then the HTML is read out as sections and assembled into a self-contained file (`katex.min.css` inlined, fonts as woff2 `data:` URIs) or printed to PDF via `printToPdf` (A4, fonts settled before printing). CSS lives in `Exporter::exportStyleSheet()` and is injected into the page, so the PDF and the HTML file share one source. Fallbacks: PDF → `QPdfWriter` writer, HTML → old escaped-text writer; the Pandoc→LaTeX PDF path is removed, **DOCX stays Pandoc-only** (native Word equations). Threading: crop→PNG/base64 encoding on a worker, render on the UI thread (`QWebEnginePage` restriction), continuation via `.then(this, …)` | The old HTML export was escaped text in `<pre>` (no headings/tables/math) and the Pandoc PDF path silently failed without LaTeX, degrading to the bare `QTextDocument` fallback — while the preview already had a perfect renderer. One pipeline = WYSIWYG export with zero new dependencies. Pandoc remains for DOCX because Chromium cannot emit OOXML and KaTeX HTML would not become editable equations. |
| 64   | **Output tab** (`OutputTab.qml`) — the output settings live behind five `SettingsStore` keys: `output/splitPages` (default on; pages are separated **without any "Page N" labels** — `---` rule in Markdown, dash line in TXT, `<hr>` in HTML (hidden in print CSS), each source page starts on a new PDF/DOCX page via `break-before:page` / raw OpenXML `<w:br w:type="page"/>`; off = pages flow continuously without separators — `joinPages(pages, pageBreak)`, `exportStyleSheet(splitPages)`, `beginExport(css, splitPages)`); `output/keepPageNumbers` (default on; off = `DetTokensParser::setKeepPageNumbers(false)` drops `page_number` tokens at parse time — no text block **and** no overlay box — and `rebuildPageText(page, false)` keeps manual rebuilds consistent; applies to newly recognized pages only); `output/tablesAsHtml` (default off; on = `DetTokensParser::setTablesAsHtml(true)` passes the model's `<table>` HTML through verbatim instead of flattening it into a GFM pipe table — see ADR 19 — and `rebuildPageText(page, keepPageNumbers, tablesAsHtml)` keeps manual rebuilds consistent); `export/pdfLandscape` + `export/pdfMarginMm` (0–50 mm, default 15) build a `QPageLayout` in `AppController::pdfPageLayout()` used by both the WebEngine `printToPdf` and the `QPdfWriter` fallback | Pages must be joinable into a single continuous document (page labels and numbers only get in the way), while orientation/margins are a per-consumer choice; the parser-level filter keeps the text, the overlay and manual box edits consistent with one another. |
| 65   | **DjVu input requires DjVuLibre** (`ddjvuapi` C API), wrapped by `src/app/DjVuDocument.h/.cpp` and used by `DocumentModel`. Root CMake resolves `DjVuLibre::DjVuLibre` via Unix `pkg-config ddjvuapi`, or explicit header/library discovery with Windows Release/Debug import-library locations. All targets compiling `DocumentModel.cpp` also compile the wrapper and link the dependency, including `test_djvu_document`. Missing development files are a configure error; no optional feature gate or automatic download. On Windows the imported target records both import libraries and configuration-matched DLLs (`DJVULIBRE_DLL_DEBUG` / `DJVULIBRE_DLL_RELEASE`); `llocr` copies its DLL beside the executable after linking. | Native decoding without a converter subprocess. GPL-2.0-or-later allows GPLv3 use in LLocr; retain notices and fulfill corresponding-source obligations. Neither checked vcpkg port path (`djvulibre`, `libdjvu`) exists; Windows setup uses a separately prepared MSVC x64 DLL/import library, not invented install commands. See `06-dev-setup.md` for acquisition and deployment constraints. |

| 66   | **DjVu import uses staged background preparation**: `QtConcurrent::run` calls static `DocumentModel::prepareDjVu()` with an independent shared decoder; a GUI-owned `QFutureWatcher` commits `PreparedDjVu` under a short `m_documentLock`. Workers never capture the controller. Mixed file selections run sequentially; PDF/raster decoding stays synchronous. Separate `importing` state drives a spinner/status and gates overlapping operations without changing recognition `busy`. | Moving the old loop under a worker-held document lock would still block GUI readers. Staging keeps the visible document unchanged until completion, preserves source order and allows controller destruction without dangling worker references. Covered by `test_djvu_document` and `test_app_import`. Cancellation/per-page progress and async full-image rendering are outside this change. |

| 67 | **Unreadable DjVu pages become explicit white replacements** rather than rejecting the book. Preserve source index and bounded metadata dimensions (800×1000 if unavailable); persist `DocumentPage.sourceError` and aggregate `PreparedDjVu.warnings`. Show import warning count/first detail and the selected page's warning. OCR skips replacements, which remain unrecognized and excluded from recognized-text export. Document-open failures remain fatal. | A single invalid layer on page 564 of an otherwise readable 673-page book must not prevent working with healthy pages. Never silently pass a synthetic blank image to the OCR model or present it as recovered original content. |
| 68   | **Text verification via a second, general-purpose model** (`CheckController` + `GeneralPurposeModel` / `QwenGeneralModel`): any recognized block can be selected on the image preview; a prompt field + «Check» button send the block crop, the previously recognized text and the prompt to the same resolved runtime connection (`/v1/chat/completions`), and the returned text can be applied back into the page (rebuilt via `rebuildPageText()`, edit store + box model updated). Request parameters are hardcoded in `CheckController::defaultParameters()` for the MVP; the checking model uses the connection's `modelId`. The message is `image + one text part` (prompt + «Recognized text to verify:» + text — several text parts confuse some chat templates), plus `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` so Qwen3-family thinking models answer instead of ending inside the thinking block. The response post-processing strips `<\|…\|>` / `<｜…｜>` markers and thinking blocks (ADR 18 parity — llama.cpp emits the EOS marker as literal text) and treats a marker-only answer as an error. | A small general-purpose model (e.g. qwen3.5-4b) can catch OCR errors better than re-prompting the OCR model. Reusing the resolved runtime connection keeps the flow identical to recognition (External/Managed). Settings/UI for the check parameters and model selection are a later stage. |
| 69   | **DjVu input support is optional at configure time** (supersedes the ADR 65 hard requirement): root CMake looks for DjVuLibre (option `LLOCR_WITH_DJVU`, default `ON`) and, when the dependency is absent or incompletely configured, prints a **warning** and continues with `LLOCR_DJVU_AVAILABLE=OFF` instead of failing. `DjVuDocument.cpp` and the DjVu paths of `DocumentModel` compile behind `LLOCR_HAVE_DJVU`; without the flag `prepareDjVu()`/`appendDjVu()` return an actionable error (install DjVuLibre and rebuild, or convert to PDF). `test_djvu_document` and `test_app_import` (fixture-driven) are built only with DjVu enabled; `test_document_model` always builds. | A deleted `build/` directory must not brick compilation on machines without a prepared DjVuLibre (Windows: no vcpkg port, MSVC x64 DLL staged manually). Graceful degradation keeps image/PDF OCR fully working; the runtime error names the remedy. |
| 70   | **Settings split from one dialog into separate non-modal windows** opened from a Header menu (Interface / Output / Runtime / OCR model; the check-model window follows): `SettingsDialog` + `SDTabBar` removed. One llama-server instance serves both roles — the model is (re)loaded per task, parallel roles are only possible against external servers, which is why every model gets its own alias. Interface settings apply immediately (scoped «Restore defaults»); other windows save on Save (per-window Save/Cancel; the global «Restore defaults» was dropped — per-group reset comes with the next steps). Runtime: «Auto-detect» button + `RuntimeController::autoDiscoverPath()` / `RuntimeLocator::autoDiscover()` removed; the footer shortcut that opened the Runtime tab when the runtime was unconfigured (`openSettingsRequested`/`selectTab`/`TabsEnum`) removed as unscalable. | With per-role model settings growing, a six-tab modal dialog no longer scales; per-window Save semantics match the smaller scope. The auto-discovery duplicates the wizard/installer flow and the footer shortcut hard-codes tab indices that no longer exist. |
| 71   | **Check-model settings window** (second `ModelSettingsWindow` instance, `role: "check"`): the Location tab binds its own `check/modelPath` + `check/mmprojPath` settings; the Request tab holds the parameters of the validate request profile — moved out of the hardcoded `CheckController::defaultParameters()`. `ModelInstaller` is role-aware: `installedInfo(index, forCheck)`, `setActiveModel(index, forCheck)`, `checkActiveTitle`; installs started from the check window (`preparePreset`/`installRemote` with `forCheck`) auto-activate as the check model without touching OCR launch settings. QML singletons: `RequestProfilesOcr`/`RequestProfilesValidate`, `LaunchProfilesOcr`/`LaunchProfilesValidate`. `RequestProfileStore`/`LaunchProfileStore` carry a `Role` (Ocr/Check) that picks the user-override file (`request.json`/`requestValidate.json`, `serverLaunch.json`/`serverLaunchValidate.json`) and the settings key of the active profile id (`model/recipeId` vs `check/requestProfileId`, `launch/profileId` vs `check/launchProfileId`). `ModelPresetCatalog::load(builtInPath, userCatalogPath, …)`; `ModelInstaller` keeps two preset lists and role-aware `presetInfo`/`preparePreset`/`activatePreset`/`importCatalog`/`exportCatalog`/`resetUserCatalog` (user catalogs `catalog.json`/`catalogValidate.json`). | 73   | **External-server model names are split per role**: a new `ConnectionRole {Ocr, Check}` selects which name `RuntimeController::resolveExternal()` puts into `ResolvedConnection.modelId` — recognition uses `model/name`, text verification uses the new `check/modelName` (`SettingsStore::checkModelName`); `ensureConnectionReady()` gained role-aware overloads (the existing ones keep meaning Ocr, so recognition/self-test/tests are untouched), and `CheckController` resolves with `ConnectionRole::Check`. An empty `check/modelName` is sent as-is — llama-server ignores the request's `model` field, and a multi-model OpenAI-compatible server returns its own "model not found", surfaced verbatim (§7.5); no silent fallback to the OCR name. Runtime settings → External mode now shows «Model name (OCR)» and «Model name (validator)». The managed server keeps a single computed alias — it serves one loaded model at a time, so per-model aliases are not needed there (per-task model loading is the next stage). | When an external server hosts several models, the check request must address the validator model, not the OCR one; llama.cpp ignores the model field, so the empty default breaks nothing for the single-instance case. |
| 74   | **The single managed server is (re)loaded per task** (model-per-task execution): `ensureConnectionReady(role)` compares the model/mmproj the running server was launched with (`m_startedModelPath`/`m_startedMmprojPath`, recorded at every successful start) against the role's settings — OCR: `launch/modelPath`+`launch/mmprojPath` with the OCR launch profile, Check: `check/modelPath`+`check/mmprojPath` with the validate launch profile (`ServerLaunchConfig::fromSettings` gained a `ConnectionRole` parameter; `RuntimeController` holds the validate `LaunchProfileStore`, falls back to the OCR store when not wired). On a mismatch the server is stopped and restarted with the requested role's configuration (`beginRoleSwitch()` → Stopped → `beginManagedResolve()`); the shared alias stays `launch/modelAlias` for both roles. The per-role `configValid` gate (`roleConfigError(role)`, replacing `configNotReadyMessage()`) applies whenever a start or a switch would be needed: a Check resolve without a selected/existing check model is refused with an actionable message («Check model is not selected — pick a model in Settings → Check model»), and a Check start from `NotConfigured` is now possible when only the OCR model is missing. ADR 61 survives: when the role's model path is empty, the live server wins (no restart on settings-reset recovery), so `readyServerResolvesAfterModelSettingWiped` keeps passing. Switch status («Switching to the check model…») rides the existing StartingRuntime spinner. Recognition vs check are mutually exclusive at the AppController level (`canRecognize` includes `check.busy()`, `checkSelectedBlock` ignores calls while recognition is busy), so a switch can never kill a running task. | One llama-server instance cannot hold two models; clicking «Проверить» must not silently verify with the OCR model. The switch is automatic (no per-click confirmation) because it is exactly what the task request means; the §7.5-style refusals cover the unconfigured check model. |
| 75   | **Per-window scoped «Restore defaults» for Output and Runtime settings windows**: `SettingsStore::resetOutputDefaults()` / `resetRuntimeDefaults()` filter the table-driven `kDefaults` registry (ADR 50) and write the matching defaults through the property setters (NOTIFY, guards, persistence preserved); the reset is **immediate** (like the Interface window, ADR 70), not a draft edit. Output scope: `parser/id`, `output/*`, `export/*`. Runtime scope: `provider/mode`, `provider/baseUrl`, `provider/apiKey`, `provider/timeoutMs`, `model/name`, `check/modelName`, `runtime/serverPath` — deliberately NOT the storage dirs, autostart flags, launch keys or setup-version (edited in the wizard/model windows; resetting them there would silently discard a working runtime). `RuntimeTabInternal` gained `loadValues()` (re-assigns `serverPathField.text` — the user's editing breaks the initial binding, so a reset/reopen must restore it) and `RuntimeTab.loadValues()` now routes through it. | The global «Restore defaults» was dropped in ADR 70 promising per-group resets; defaults live in one table so the QML needs no literal copies, and the immediate semantics match the Interface window the pattern came from. |
| 76   | **Check-flow hardening from the branch review** (fix round, supersedes parts of ADR 68/71 semantics): a check result is **bound to the block it was computed for** (`m_checkPage`/`m_checkBox`, recorded at check start) — a mid-check selection/page change drops the result instead of retargeting «Apply fix», and a box removal remaps/clears the selection (no silent shift to a neighbour); `applyCheckedText` additionally re-validates the target, refuses during a recognition run, and mutates the page under `m_documentLock` (signals outside the lock). The restored `serverPicker` FileDialog and the `Settings.serverPath` field wiring live in `RuntimeTabInternal` (`onServerPathChanged` reaches the field unless it has focus); `LocationTab` carries role-aware `Connections` so activation/pickers/installer update its path fields; `RequestTab` drops its `anchors.fill` inside the StackLayout; the check panel's TextAreas set `width: parent.width` (wrap actually engages); `removeModel` refuses a model active for either role; the footer's launch-dirty tracking covers the validate profile and `check/*` keys. Minor: shared `core/ServiceMarkers.h` for the ADR-18 marker strip (parser + check model), `checkPresetCount` naming, `selectedBox()` helper, `resetToDefaults()` delegates to `resetGroup()`, `beginRoleSwitch()` null-server guard, `RuntimeTabInternal` gets `canManage` as a property (no context lookup), `ModelSettingsWindow` refreshes the Location lists on every open and the footer Save uses `requestTab.profiles`. The five settings windows stay **eagerly instantiated** (ADR 70 wiring; Loader conversion deferred until startup cost is measured — the tabs are cheap hidden objects). | The check result is user-facing page data: a mis-applied correction silently corrupts page text; the rest are stale-binding/dead-reference regressions of the settings rework, each verified by the review agents. |
| 77   | **UI text sizes are platform-compensated in `Theme.qml`** (the single place they are chosen; no QML file hardcodes a `font.pointSize`): `platformTextScale = Qt.platform.os === "osx" ? 4 / 3 : 1` multiplies every design point size. Qt converts a point size to pixels with the screen's **base logical DPI** — 72 on macOS (`QCocoaScreen::logicalBaseDpi`), 96 on Windows/X11 — so 1 pt is 1 px on macOS but 1.33 px on Windows, while every metric (`controlHeight`, `spacing`, radii) and window size in QML is in pixels and identical on all platforms. The same point size therefore came out ~25 % smaller on macOS relative to the controls around it, and `captionSize` (8 pt → 8 px, below macOS's readable minimum) made button labels look too small next to the default-font labels, which Qt sizes per platform (13 pt on macOS ≈ 9 pt on Windows, both ~12–13 px). | Points for text + pixels for geometry agree only on Windows, where the 96-dpi base matches the grid the sizes were tuned on; on macOS they disagree, so the UI text shrank but the controls did not. Compensating **on macOS only** leaves the Windows/Linux rendering pixel-identical (measured via `QT_FONT_DPI` + the offscreen platform: 8/11/13/16 pt → 11/15/18/22 px on macOS vs 11/15/17/21 px on Windows). A user text-size preference (`UiController.textScale`, still commented out) would multiply in at the same place, so no call site changes. |
| 78   | **Free-form Hugging Face search/download is removed from the UI** (both `ModelSettingsWindow` roles and the wizard's Model step): models are installed from the preset catalog or a local file. Removed: the HF search field + results list (`HfSearchList.qml` deleted), the HF-token field, the catalog Import/Export/«Restore defaults» buttons and `ModelInstaller`'s `startSearch`/`searchResult`/`installRemote`/`importCatalog`/`exportCatalog`/`resetUserCatalog`/`hfToken`/`setHfToken` plus the search properties; `ModelCatalog` lost `search()`/`parseSearchJson()`/`HfModelSummary`. `SettingsStore::hfToken` stays — preset downloads still authenticate to (gated) HF repos with the persisted token. | The free search duplicated the preset catalog and confronted users with an uncurated choice; for OCR the curated presets cover the working models. Power users can point at a local GGUF file. |
| 79   | **Connection mode is a three-choice combo; content follows the choice.** Runtime settings: «External server» / «Managed local server (Specify llama.cpp binary)» / «Managed local server (Download llama.cpp via app)». The third option is the persisted `runtime/serverPathIsManaged` flag: specify-binary keeps `serverPathIsManaged=false` and shows only the llama-server path field + probe result (picking a binary runs the full probe immediately); download variant shows only the install UI (from «Install llama.cpp» down: installed builds with a per-row «Open folder» via the new `RuntimeInstaller::openBuildFolder()`, update plaque, backend/release, progress). Start/Stop/Restart/Show-log buttons left the settings window (they live in the footer). Model settings windows: the Location tab is renamed «Model»; in External mode it shows only a notice + «Configure runtime…» (opens the runtime window); in Managed mode a source combo switches between «Specify model files» (path fields only) and «Download model» (installed models + preset catalog). The Request tab header matches the Launch tab: «Profile» + combo + «Restore profile» in one row. | One mode = one visible surface: the mixed managed tab made users deal with binary paths and release catalogs at once, and model-location settings are meaningless when an external server owns the model. |
| 90   | **Structured verification protocol replaces free-text check replies** (feature/second-llm-check): the verifier model answers with exactly one of `OK` (block correct — no change), `FIX` + a newline + the complete corrected block, or `REVIEW` (block unreadable). The request `GeneralPurposeModel::buildRequestBody()` now sends a `system` message (the protocol contract) plus a user message of three ordered parts — the **type prompt** (text), the block crop (`image_url`), and `OCR candidate:\n<ocr_candidate>\n…\n</ocr_candidate>` — with `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false` and the validate request-profile parameters (`temperature 0`, `repeat_penalty 1.0`, `presence_penalty 0.0`, `frequency_penalty 0.0`, `max_tokens 512`, `stream false`, `cache_prompt true`; profile id renamed `qwen3.5-4b` → `ocr-verifier`). `parseResponse()` maps replies to a `CheckStatus {Failed, Ok, Fixed, Review}`; a `FIX` stores the corrected block in `BoundingBox.correctedText` **separately from the recognized `text`** (`BoxCheckStatus` per box: NotChecked/Ok/Fixed/Review), and `rebuildPageText()` prefers `correctedText` in the output while the recognized text stays untouched (diff/revert via the page edit store). The verification settings window (new «Проверка» menu item in Settings, right after «Вывод») has three tabs: **Blocks** (checkbox list of which block types are auto-verified), **System prompt**, and **Block prompts** (type list on the left, prompt editor on the right). Prompts ship as `:/profiles/verifyPrompts.json` (system prompt + per-type prompts, default enablement) and persist user edits as `profiles/verifyPrompts.json` in the user profile dir — only entries differing from built-ins, mirroring `RequestProfileStore` (ADR 56/72); exposed to QML as the `Verification` singleton (`VerificationPromptStore`). «Проверка страницы» (a Header toolbar button + the panel button) verifies all enabled non-empty blocks of the current page sequentially (`startVerifyQueue`, serial `CheckController` requests, page switch/stop cancels); the old free-form prompt box and the «Apply fix» button are gone — a FIX is stored automatically. The image preview draws a colored mark next to each block label (`Theme.success` green = OK, `Theme.warning` yellow = fixed, `Theme.error` red = REVIEW). | The previous flow returned free text (everything was treated as a correction) with a manual apply; the structured protocol lets OCR corrections be verified per block, auto-applied, and visually audited. Keeping the fix separate from the recognized text preserves the original for comparison/revert. Batch verification respects the checkbox list, so header/footer/page-number noise can stay out of the (paid, per-call) verifier loop. |
| 91   | **Batch verification across pages + auto-check after recognition** (feature/second-llm-check, supersedes the page-scoped queue of ADR 90): the verify queue is generalized from `QList<int>` (boxes of the current page) to a `QList<VerifyTask>` where a task is `{page, box}` — `checkSelectedBlock`/`checkEnabledBlocksOnPage` enqueue the current page, the new **«Проверить всё»** Header button (`checkAllEnabledBlocks`) enqueues every enabled non-empty block of all recognized pages, and `applyCheckResultToBox(pageIndex, …)` writes the result into the right page (box-model/selection signals fire only when that page is the current one). **Page switches no longer cancel a running check** — the queue drains serially while the user browses (parity with recognition); only Stop clears it. New `check/autoCheck` setting (Settings → Проверка → Blocks, «Проверять автоматически»): when on, finishing a recognition run automatically starts `checkAllEnabledBlocks(true)` — the `onlyUnchecked` mode skips blocks whose `checkStatus` is not `NotChecked`, so a second recognition run only verifies the newly recognized blocks instead of re-verifying already checked pages; a user Stop within recognition suppresses it (`m_recognitionStopped`). The managed runtime already reloads per role (ADR 74) — the auto-check simply resolves with `ConnectionRole::Check`, so `ensureConnectionReady` stops the OCR server and starts the check model, while an external runtime just starts checking with `check/modelName`. The Blocks tab list gained a `ScrollBar.AsNeeded` vertical scrollbar. | Single-page checking does not scale to multi-page documents, and the previous «page switch cancels» rule made any batch run unusable. Reusing the existing role-aware resolve (ADR 74) keeps the model switch automatic and shared with manual checks; the stop flag prevents an unwanted auto-check after the user deliberately interrupts recognition; re-verifying already-checked blocks after a partial re-recognition would waste paid verifier calls. |
| 80   | **Role-filtered installed-model lists** (the OCR-model and check-model windows no longer show every registry entry): `ModelEntry` gains a persisted `roles` string list (`"ocr"` / `"check"`, key `roles` in `index.json`), recorded at install time in `ModelInstaller::completeInstall()` from `m_pendingForCheck` (a re-install from the other window merges — never drops — the roles the replaced entry already had). The installed lists switch to the role-filtered accessors `roleInstalledCount(forCheck)` / `roleInstalledInfo(index, forCheck)`: their `index` addresses the role's sublist, and the returned info map carries the full-registry `index` so `setActiveModel`/`removeModel`/`openModelFolder` keep working unchanged (the legacy full-list `installedInfo(i)` stays for tests). `ModelInstaller::matchesRole()` lists a model when: it is the role's **active** model (always, whatever its roles — settings-reset recovery included), it has the role in `roles`, or — for legacy entries with an empty `roles` — it is **inferred**: an entry active only as the verifier stays out of the OCR list, and otherwise the file layout decides (a model with an mmproj projector → OCR list, a plain text model → check list). `parser`/`prompt` are deliberately NOT used for inference — they are also recorded for models installed from the validate catalog (ADR 72), so they prove nothing. `ModelInstalledList` and the wizard's Model step bind heights/visibility to the filtered count. | With both windows showing the full registry, the OCR and validation pickers became unusable once several models were installed. Role tags recorded at install time keep the lists relevant; the active-model override and metadata inference mean no registry migration and no model can become invisible from the role that uses it. |
| 81   | **The setup wizard is a 7-step linear flow, not a launcher for settings windows.** Steps: 1 Intro (welcome + language/theme, same options as the Interface settings, applied immediately), 2 Work-mode choice — three selectable cards with a visual selected state (accent border + checkmark) writing `provider/mode` + `runtime/serverPathIsManaged` on click, 3 mode-dependent runtime step — external: the external-server fields embedded in the wizard (`RuntimeTabExternal` reused, committed on step leave); managed-binary: path field + Browse with an immediate full probe; managed-download: the existing `StepRuntime` install UI, 4 Models (skipped for external — the server owns the models) with a top radio pair «Download models» (OCR-model / check-model tabs, each with installed list + preset catalog, both role-aware) and «Specify model files» (OCR model/mmproj + check model/mmproj paths in one view), 5 Output (split-pages / page-numbers / tables-as-HTML + PDF orientation/margins, applied immediately), 6 Check-and-launch (the former launch step with the self-test), 7 Summary reflecting the chosen mode (endpoint + model names for external; runtime/model paths + autostart + port for managed). The footer shows «Step N of M» (M reflects the actual step count — 6 for external); the step list is rebuilt when leaving step 2, and steps render by visibility (not a StackLayout) because the array order differs per mode. The settings-menu gains «Мастер настройки» (Header → `setupWizardRequested`), and the Runtime settings window loses its «Launch setup wizard» button and the wizard/log-window reference pass-through (`setupWizardRef`/`logWindowRef`/`canManage` removed from `RuntimeTab`/`RuntimeSettingsWindow`). | The old wizard either bounced users into full settings windows (external) or forced a single pre-merged runtime tab; a self-contained flow with visible choices matches how the mode-dependent settings are now split (ADR 79). |
| 82   | **Refactoring round 1 — list heights, group-filtered block model.** (a) The embedded list components (`ModelInstalledList`/`ModelPresetList`/`RuntimeBuildsList`) publish `implicitHeight` = `min(count, maxVisibleRows) * rowHeight` (`maxVisibleRows <= 0` = uncapped; 0 rows = 0 height); consumers bind `Layout.preferredHeight` to it instead of hand-computed `count * N` formulas (`LocationTab`, `StepModel`, `RuntimeTabInternal` — the uncapped case keeps `maxVisibleRows: -1` + `scrollable: false`). (b) The three-column Blocks tab (`VerificationBlocksTab`) replaces three full-model `Repeater`s with `visible: group === …` (2/3 of delegates permanently invisible) by a `BlockGroupFilterModel : QSortFilterProxyModel` filtered on the `group` role; the three filtered views are exposed as `Verification.blockModelContent/Captions/Service` (CONSTANT, source lives in the store). `Q_INVOKABLE`s do not pass through a proxy, so `BlockTypeRow` no longer keeps a proxy `index` — the source row is resolved by the unique block type via the new `VerificationBlocksModel::rowOfType(type)`. A QML-only `DelegateModel` + `filterOnGroup` was rejected: not testable from C++ and requires mutating groups from the delegate. The full plan for the remaining refactoring (store dedup, `ModelInstaller`/`AppController` split) was tracked across the refactoring rounds recorded in ADR 82–88; the follow-up architectural work lives in `docs/architecture-plan/README.md`. | Hand-copied row-height constants (40/36/34 for the same component) broke silently on default changes, and instantiating every block type three times (~19 → ~57 rows) was pure waste; a C++ proxy keeps the delegate tree simple and is unit-testable (three new cases in `test_verification_prompts`). |
| 83   | **Bug fix — external-runtime settings were wiped on every app start** (root cause: `StepExternal` saved on hide). `SetupWizard` is instantiated eagerly in `Main.qml` regardless of `setupVersion`, and all nine steps initialize with `Item.visible == true` before their `visible: wizard.currentStep === step` bindings evaluate to `false`; that initial change fires `onVisibleChanged`, and `StepExternal`'s hide branch called `rtExternal.saveValues()` — before `loadValues()` had ever run — writing empty strings into `provider/baseUrl`, `model/name`, `check/modelName` and the default timeout. Evidence: the profile plist had empty `provider/baseUrl`/`model/name`, default `provider/timeoutMs`, while `provider/lastExternalBaseUrl` still held the user's URL (the mode-switch stash). Fix: `StepExternal` keeps a `valuesLoaded` flag — save on hide only after a show loaded the fields; plus a startup self-heal in `applyStartupMigration()` (external mode + empty `provider/baseUrl` + non-empty stash → restore the stash) that repairs already-wiped profiles. Two regression tests in `test_settings_store` cover the self-heal and its no-op on a non-empty endpoint. | The initial binding evaluation of a QML property fires change handlers just like any later change; any "save on hide" without a "loaded" guard wipes settings from components that are eagerly created invisible. |
| 84   | **Refactoring round 2 — shared JSON persistence for the profile stores.** The byte-identical persistence layer of `LaunchProfileStore`, `RequestProfileStore` and `VerificationPromptStore` (remove-if-empty → mkpath → QSaveFile → commit, plus read-and-warn-on-error) moved into `app/ProfileStorage.h/.cpp` (`ProfileStorage::removeFileIfExists` / `writeJsonAtomic` / `readJson`); each store keeps its own `tr()` warning texts and its JSON document shape, and passes a diagnostic `QString*` out-param instead. Behavior is unchanged (same files, same `schemaVersion` values, same atomic-write semantics), so the existing store test suites ran without edits; a new `test_profile_storage` covers the helpers directly (missing files, malformed JSON, overwrite, nested mkpath). A template `ProfileStoreBase` was deliberately rejected — the shared helpers remove the real duplication without coupling the three stores' divergent domain logic. | Persistence fixes (atomicity, diagnostics) previously had to be applied three times and had already drifted apart; the helpers make the correct pattern the only pattern while keeping translation contexts stable. |
| 85   | **Refactoring round 3 — `ModelInstallTransaction` extracted from `ModelInstaller`** (refactoring round 3; the rounds are recorded in ADR 82–88). The prepare → download → finalize pipeline for HF preset installs moved into `runtime/ModelInstallTransaction.{h,cpp}`: the `Pending` plan (renamed `InstallPlan`), `DownloadManager`/`DownloadGroup` ownership, the prepare generation gate, `beginPrepare`/`onPrepareDone`/`beginDownload`/`enqueueFile`/`expectedShaFor`/`mmprojAlreadyOnDisk`/`maybeFinishDownloads`/`completeInstall`/`cancel`. The transaction keeps its own state enum (same values as `ModelInstaller::State`) and mirrors state/busy/progress/status to the façade via signals; `installedListReplaced(QList<ModelEntry>)` publishes registry updates. `ModelInstaller` is now the QML façade only — presets, registry views, role filters, activation/removal — with the full public QML API unchanged (`state/busy/progress/statusMessage`, `preparePreset`/`installPrepared`/`cancelInstall`), so QML files and `test_model_installer` needed no edits. The pure selection logic became public statics (`repoDirName`, `selectModelFiles`) covered by the new `test_model_install_transaction`; the group-size tie now breaks by key, removing a QHash-seed nondeterminism flagged in review. Moved `tr()` strings got a new `llocr::ModelInstallTransaction` context in `llocr_ru.ts` (translations carried over); the prepare-worker errors stay under the `QObject` context as before. | `ModelInstaller` concentrated what the runtime side spreads over four classes; the façade/transaction split mirrors `RuntimeInstaller`/`InstallTransaction`, makes the install pipeline unit-testable without network, and shrinks the god class by ~370 lines. |
| 86   | **Refactoring round 4 — the verification queue and the export pipeline extracted from `AppController`** (refactoring round 4; see ADR 82–88). (a) `app/VerificationQueueController.{h,cpp}` owns the serial LLM-check queue: `VerifyTask` (`{page, box}`), the `m_verify*` state (queue, current task, active/finished flags, progress counters, `m_checkError`), `startVerifyQueue`/`startNextVerify`/`finishVerifyQueue`/`collectEnabledBoxes`, the `checkBlock`/`checkPageEnabledBlocks`/`checkAllEnabledBlocks`/`stop` entry points and `pageVerificationSupported`/`allPageVerificationSupported`; it owns the `CheckController` and reads the document through an injected `DocumentModel&` + `VerificationPromptStore&`, with `std::function` providers for the crop image (`AppController::croppedImage`) and the recognition-busy flag. Results cross the boundary as a `blockChecked(page, box, result)` signal — `AppController::applyCheckResultToBox` (document mutation under `m_documentLock`, edit store, box-model/signal fan-out) stayed in the owner, so the controller never mutates the document. `m_recognitionStopped` (auto-check suppression) also stayed. (b) `app/ExportController.{h,cpp}` owns the whole export pipeline: `collectPages`, `ExportScope`, the `exportPages` flow (pandoc/built-in writer, rendered HTML/PDF path with the writer fallbacks), `pdfPageLayout`, `finalizeRenderedExport`, `finishExport`, `m_exporting` and ownership of `Exporter`+`ExportRenderer`; injected deps are `DocumentModel&`, `PageEditStore&`, `SettingsStore&` plus crop/importing providers, and `currentPage` is an explicit argument. In both cases the `AppController` public QML API (properties, invokables) is unchanged — the methods are forwarders, controller signals relay to `checkStateChanged`/`exportingChanged`/`setStatus`. Moved `tr()` strings got a new `llocr::ExportController` context in `llocr_ru.ts` (translations carried over); `VerificationQueueController` has no user-facing strings. `test_app_import` picks the new sources up automatically (it compiles the `llocr` target's app sources) and passed without edits; full `ctest` 31/31 green after each extraction. | `AppController` was a god class concentrating import, recognition wiring, the verification state machine and the export pipeline; extracting the two cohesive state machines (same pattern as `RecognitionController`/`CheckController`) removes ~300 lines of queue/export machinery from the owner and makes each piece independently readable and testable, while the frozen QML surface keeps the change risk-free. |

| 87   | **Second OCR model: `Lfm25VlModel` (id `lfm25-vl-3b`, LiquidAI/LFM2.5-VL-3B)** follows the ADR 58/59 extension recipe — one adapter class + one same-id built-in request profile + preset-catalog entries, nothing else touched. The adapter ships the model card's official layout-annotation prompt (`image_index=<n> <label> [xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax]` + content; coordinates 0–1000, OTSL tables) as a single prompt variant and defaults to the existing `det_tokens` parser; the recommended generation params (temperature 0.2, top_k 50, repeat_penalty 1.0, max_tokens 8192, stream false) live in the new profile `lfm25-vl-3b` in `:/profiles/requestOcr.json`. `DetTokensParser` accepts an **optional `image_index=<n>` prefix** on a token start (the prefix is part of the token match, so it never leaks into block text) and converts the model's **OTSL tables** (`<fcel>/<lcel>/<ucel>/<xcel>/<nl>` tokens — TableFormer vocabulary) into a GFM pipe table by default and — when «Таблицы как HTML» is on (`output/tablesAsHtml`, ADR 64) — into a real HTML `<table>` whose span tokens become **live `rowspan`/`colspan` attributes** (`<lcel>` widens the previous cell, `<ucel>`/`<xcel>` extend the covering cell above; parity with the Unlimited-OCR model's verbatim `<table>` output). The OTSL conversion applies **wherever the tokens appear**, not only in `table [bbox]` blocks — the model leaks bare `<nl>`-separated formula rows into `text`/`equation` blocks and the page preamble; a single-column fragment becomes plain lines (no 1-col pipe-table dressing). A pre-pass in `parse()` rewrites the table-caption drift (`image_index=N <label>Long caption text…</label>` + `<content>…</content>` with the OTSL rows) into synthetic `table_caption` + `table` regions; `formatOtslTable` also normalizes cell separators written as closing `</fcel>` tags and row breaks written as plain newlines (no `<nl>`), and drops stray `<table>`/`</table>` wrappers. Two model transcriptions artifacts are cleaned in `convertMath`: a formula truncated without the closing `\)` / `\]` still becomes `$…$` math, and the `~` non-breaking-space artifact inside formulas (e.g. `\mathrm{~r}`) is dropped — within math spans only, plain-text tildes are kept. The model also nests inline `\(…\)` inside display `\[…\]` equations (both in `equation` blocks and mid-text): the display spans are converted first and shielded behind placeholders while the inline pass runs, then their redundant inner delimiters are stripped — bare parens and `\left(` are legit content and stay. `image_block` maps to the image placeholder like `image`/`chart`, and the placeholder's alt text keeps only the first meaningful line of the region text (the model's OCR-ed figure labels would otherwise become a multi-line alt); caption (Italic) blocks run through `convertMath` as well. — both opt-in paths that leave Unlimited-OCR output parsing unchanged. Presets `lfm25-vl-3b-q4_k_m` / `lfm25-vl-3b-q8_0` in `defaultLlmPresetsOcr.json` point at the `LiquidAI/LFM2.5-VL-3B-GGUF` repo (model + F16 mmproj, ctx 8192) and carry **`minBuild: b8000`** — LFM2.5-VL image tiling in llama.cpp landed in PR #19454 (Feb 2026); older builds would mis-handle large page images. Tests: adapter contract + factory registration (`test_ocr_models`), parser prefix + OTSL-table cases (`test_det_parser`). | A second supported OCR LLM proves the adapter/profile/preset extension path end-to-end; everything (prompt, sampling, download) stays data-driven through the existing Settings surfaces with zero UI changes — the model combobox on Request and the Models catalog pick the new entries up automatically. |

| 88   | **Output parsers become a per-model, data-driven layer** (refactoring round 5 — the parser extension path for further OCR models). (a) **Options leave the app layer**: `ParserOptions` (`parsers/ParserOptions.h` — `keepPageNumbers`, `tablesAsHtml`, `bboxRange`, `modelId`) is passed to `ParserFactory::create(id, options)`, and a parser is configured once at construction instead of being poked through `AppController`'s `dynamic_cast<DetTokensParser*>`; the hardcoded `kBboxCoordinateRange = 1000` becomes `ParserOptions::bboxRange` (the duplicate-region tolerance is now 1 % of that range), and the previously dead `kDedupTolerance` constant is gone. (b) **`IOutputParser` gains `rebuildText(const OcrPage&)` and `displayName()`**: re-rendering the page Markdown after a verified FIX / a removed block / a resized image block is a parser contract, not a free function in `DetTokensParser.h` that `AppController` called at three sites. `RawParser::rebuildText` returns the page text, which also closes the latent hole where a fragment-less page would have been rebuilt to an empty string. (c) **`parser/id` defaults to `auto`**: Settings → Output lists «Automatic (model default)» first and resolves through `OcrModel::defaultParserId()` (`AppController::effectiveParserId()`), so the previously dead `defaultParserId()` is finally the authority and switching the OCR model cannot leave a mismatched parser behind; the combo shows translated display names (`parserNames`/`parserLabels` carry ids/labels one-to-one, `textRole: "label"`); the combination is unchanged and remains a valid *explicit* override. `ModelInstaller::setActiveModel()` no longer writes the preset's `parser` field into the setting — **amended by ADR 96**, which reversed this sub-decision (the "only path that can pre-select a non-default parser" case is hypothetical, while the real effect was that activating a model silently overrode an explicit choice in Settings → Output and turned «Automatic (model default)» into a pinned value). (d) **Label → block style is data**: `resources/profiles/labels.json` (`default` + per-model `overrides` keyed by model id) loaded by `BlockStyleMap` (`parsers/BlockStyle.{h,cpp}`), with the pre-existing table compiled in as the fallback for test binaries and a malformed file; the shipped file names all 22 labels the LFM2.5-VL prompt advertises, so a new model's vocabulary is a JSON edit. (e) **Parse failures are no longer silent**: `OcrResult::notes` carries non-fatal diagnostics, `DetTokensParser` reports a reply with no layout tokens (the classic wrong-model/wrong-parser symptom, previously a "successful" single blob), and `DocumentPage::parseNote` surfaces it as a new `Controller.parseWarning` line in the footer. Tests: options/notes/label-map/factory cases in `test_det_parser` (48 pass), `everyModelDeclaresARegisteredParser` in `test_ocr_models`; full `ctest` 31/31. | ADR 87 showed the cost of a second model: +530 lines inside the one `det_tokens` parser, because a "generic" parser had no seam for model-specific behaviour. These five changes make a new model a data + adapter change instead of surgery in `parse()`, and stop the two failure modes that make new-model debugging slow — a silently mismatched parser and a silently failed tokenization.
