The stage-4 pipeline split (preprocessor → token scanner → styler → formatters) is deliberately **not** done yet: with the seams above, a model of the same det-token family needs no structural change, and the split is only worth its churn once a model with a genuinely different reply shape (JSON, foreign XML, float coordinates) actually arrives. |
| 89   | **Parser stage-4 split (deferred)** — the remaining ceiling of ADR 88: with one parser per model (ADR 112) the *model* seam exists, but the engine is still one `parse()`. When a third OCR model with a *different* reply shape is adopted, factor the det-token engine into ordered stages — `Preprocessor[]` → `TokenScanner` (header patterns as data) → `BlockStyler` (already `BlockStyleMap`, ADR 88) → `BlockFormatter[]` — with each model quirk registered as a stage entry beside `ParserFactory` rather than as a branch inside `parse()`. | Keeping the pipeline flat costs nothing while every supported model speaks a det-token dialect; the seam now lives in the class hierarchy, so the split is deferred until a model proves the flat engine cannot hold it. |
| 92   | **Block deletion has exactly one entry point: `AppController::removeBlock(index)`.** The document owns the truth; the controller mutates `DocumentModel`, then mirrors the change into `BoxListModel` (plain C++ `removeBox()`, no `Q_INVOKABLE`, no `boxRemoved` signal), the `PageEditStore` (rebuilt page text) and the selection, then signals. QML (`ImagePreview`, `BlockEditPanel`) calls the controller, never the model. Regression test: `TestAppImport::blockRemovalUpdatesDocumentAndView` (recognition against a loopback det-token server, then delete, assert the rebuilt text, the row count, the selection and the refusal of out-of-range indices); `TestBoxModel::removeBoxIsNotQmlCallable` pins the view-model contract. | The «Delete block» button used to mutate only `BoxListModel`: the page kept the box, so the rebuilt text, the export, the verification queue and the next `updateBoxesForCurrent()` all disagreed with the UI, and `AppController::onBoxRemoved` — the handler that did the real work — had no callers at all. A view model with a QML-callable mutator and an unconnected signal is a silent data-loss path; the document must be the only source of truth and the controller the only writer. |
| 93   | **`RuntimeController` resolves are keyed by `ConnectionRole`.** `ensureConnectionReady(role, …)` joins the in-flight batch only for the **same** role; a request for the other role goes to `m_deferredResolves` and, when the current resolve completes, is promoted to its own batch and dispatched by the extracted `startResolveForRole(role)` — which re-evaluates `serverRunsRole()` and performs the role switch (ADR 74). `cancelPendingStart()` drops the deferred batch first (a Stop must not let a queued other-role resolve restart the server). `completeResolve()` refuses to promote if a callback already started a resolve, so a re-entrant request is never clobbered. Test: `TestEnsureConnection::crossRoleRequestDuringResolveIsNotAnsweredWithTheWrongRole` asserts the OCR caller is answered first, then a `Stopped` transition, then the check caller. | A single managed server serves one role at a time, but the resolver had one slot: a `Check` request arriving during an in-flight `Ocr` resolve was queued into the same callback vector and answered with the **OCR** connection — the verification silently ran against the OCR model, with no error and no role switch. Dedup of concurrent callers (ADR 26) stays; only cross-role coalescing is gone. |
| 94   | **The raw request/response debug dump is opt-in (`LLOCR_RAW_DEBUG=1`).** `OcrModel`'s `rawDebugEnabled()` no longer keys off the application name (which made the dump *on* in every release build); it is on only when the environment variable is set to anything but `0`. The request body is dumped before the POST instead of from the response handler, so an aborted request is recorded too. Test: `TestOcrModels::rawDebugDumpIsOptIn`. | The dump writes every request verbatim — including the full base64 page image — into `<AppData>/raw-debug/`, unrotated, for every page of every document. That is a disk-growth and a privacy problem in a shipped app, and a debug facility should be something the user turns on. |
| 95   | **An optional-dependency define must not change a class layout.** `DocumentModel.h` now includes `app/DjVuDocument.h` unconditionally (it only forward-declares the `ddjvu_*` structs, so it needs no DjVuLibre headers); the DjVu members are declared unconditionally and `LLOCR_HAVE_DJVU` selects only the *implementation* in `DocumentModel.cpp`. | The header wrapped **members** in `#ifdef LLOCR_HAVE_DJVU` while the define was `PRIVATE`: any target compiling `DocumentModel.cpp` without it — a test target, a future library split — produced a different class layout for the same type, which is an ODR violation and silent memory corruption rather than a link error. Verified by compiling `DocumentModel.cpp` with the define stripped. |
| 96   | **`ParserFactory::create()` rejects `auto`, and activating a model no longer rewrites the parser setting.** `kAutoId` is a Settings value, not a parser id: `create("auto")` returns `nullptr` with a warning instead of silently degrading to `raw`; an unregistered id still degrades to `raw`. `ModelInstaller::setActiveModel()` no longer copies `ModelEntry::parser` into `Settings.parserId`. Test: `TestDetParser::autoIdIsSelectableButNeverCreatesAParser`. | The header already claimed `auto` "never reaches `create()`" while the implementation quietly answered with `raw` — the invariant was kept by convention, and a new call site would have inherited a silently mismatched parser. Separately, activating a model overwrote an explicit choice in Settings → Output and converted «Automatic (model default)» into a pinned value (see ADR 88 (c), amended). |
| 97   | **The model index is updated read-modify-write under its own lock.** New `ModelRegistry::update(modelsDir, mutate, error)` takes `.registry.lock`, reads the index (`readIndex`) and writes it (`writeIndex`) without releasing the lock in between; `save()` is now lock + `writeIndex`. The two callers that used to build a list from an unlocked snapshot (`ModelInstallTransaction::completeInstall`, `ModelInstaller::removeModel`) go through `update()`. | `save()` guarded only the write, so two instances installing models concurrently both wrote a list derived from their own pre-lock snapshot and the second silently dropped the first's entry — the installed-model list then disagreed with the files on disk. |
| 98   | **The release catalog's offline cache is never dropped by a failed fetch.** `fetchReleasesLocal()` no longer calls `resetCache()` (the function is gone) on timeout / 403 / non-200 / malformed body; it falls back to the last known good list, reported through the new age-agnostic `readCacheFile()` while `loadCache()` keeps the 6 h TTL gate for the "is it fresh" decision. The nested event loop got a watchdog timer (`timeoutMs + 5 s`), and `X-RateLimit-Reset` is read before `deleteLater()`. The endpoint became an injectable parameter (default: the GitHub API) so the policy is testable. Test: `TestReleaseCatalog::staleCacheSurvivesFailedFetch` (500 / 403 / malformed). | A single flaky network moment deleted `releases.json`, and the next offline start reported «No releases available» instead of the last known catalog — the cache existed precisely to cover that case. `ReleaseCatalog::resetCache()` had no other caller. |
| 99   | **Every test gets a timeout and a headless platform by default.** `tests/CMakeLists.txt` ends with a directory-level loop over all `add_test` targets: `TIMEOUT 300` when a test does not set its own, and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` appended when the test has none. The one exception is `test_export_renderer`, which needs a real render stack and gets `QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS=--disable-gpu --no-sandbox` instead — plus `--single-process`, added by the test itself (macOS only): Chromium's multi-process model registers a named Mach rendezvous server in the bootstrap namespace, a restricted session (a sandbox, a container, a shell without a GUI session) refuses the registration and Chromium aborts with SIGTRAP — a crash dialog on the developer's desktop and a red test in the suite. The test renders the app's own local bundle, so the renderer runs in the browser process: no rendezvous, no crash, and the assertions stay about the rendered output, not about the process model. `test_app_import`'s own `TIMEOUT 90` still wins (an explicit entry is left alone). | Only `test_app_import` had a timeout, so a single hung `waitForStarted()` in one of the three process-spawning tests blocked `ctest` forever — and a test that creates a `QGuiApplication` without a platform plugin cannot run headless. The defaults are applied once instead of 31 times. |
| 100  | **One verification command, and CI runs the same one.** `scripts/check.sh` does configure (only if needed) → build → `ctest --output-on-failure` → `clang-format --dry-run --Werror` → `qmllint`, reusing an existing tree by default (`--configure`, `--no-tests`, `--no-format`, `--no-lint`; `BUILD_DIR`, `JOBS`, `QT_PREFIX`, `BUILD_TYPE`). A missing tool degrades to a **notice**, never to a silent pass. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs it on macOS/Windows/Linux. | The review found the tree verifiable only by hand (configure, build, ctest, format — in that order, with the flags remembered). The workflow's Qt provisioning step stays a `TODO` because the project builds against a **custom** Qt 6.10.3, not an official installer build: the workflow must not be enabled for `push`/`pull_request` until that artifact is wired in, otherwise every run fails at the configure step. |
| 101  | **The QML tree is a real Qt module (`qt_add_qml_module`, URI `LLocr`).** The 49 files are globbed with `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS` (the hand-maintained 49-line `qt_add_resources` list is gone) and live at `qrc:/qt/qml/LLocr/…`, which is the URL `main.cpp` loads. The module is declared in the root `CMakeLists.txt`, not beside the `llocr` target in `src/` (amended Oct 2026, Windows build fix; superseded again by ADR 176 — the module is declared beside the target and the files live in `src/qml/`): the qmlcachegen output path is `<target>_<path relative to the declaring directory>`, and declaring the module in `src/` while the files live in `resources/qml` embeds a `llocr_../…` component there that Win32 can neither create nor resolve (macOS/Linux treat it as a literal name). `qt_add_qml_module` wires the cross-directory case itself. Each QML file gets a `QT_RESOURCE_ALIAS` relative to `resources/qml`, so the `MainWindow/`, `Settings/`, `Common/`, `Setup/` layout is preserved. The two QML singletons additionally need `QT_QML_SINGLETON_TYPE` (the `pragma Singleton` in the file alone is not enough for the generated `qmldir`), and `BlockNames.qml` moved from `Common/` to the module root because `qmllint` resolves a singleton through the nearest `qmldir`. The `QML_ELEMENT`/`QML_SINGLETON` macros were removed from the five classes that carried them: with a real module they would make `qmltyperegistrar` emit a default-constructor call for classes that have none. The C++ services stay registered by hand in `main.cpp` — the QML API is unchanged. `.qmllint.ini` (CamelCase directive names, as in `qmllint --write-defaults`) keeps `UnqualifiedAccess` and `UnusedImports` at info and gates everything else; it found the `Theme`/`BlockNames` singleton problem, the `SetupWizard.currentStep.complete` type error and a stale import list on first run. | The QML was shipped as raw `qrc:/qml/*` resources with the file list maintained by hand and no type checking at all, so QML errors surfaced only at runtime; and the inert `QML_ELEMENT` macros were a trap that would have broken the build the moment a module appeared. The C++ singletons need `create()` factories and an ownership story (they are stack objects in `main()` that outlive the engine) — that is the layering work of stage 4, not a packaging detail. |
| 102  | **One index space in the det-token parser; the page text is a function of the boxes.** The det-token parser builds `page.boxes` and nothing else, then sets `page.text = rebuildText(page)` — the same renderer the app calls after an edit. The parallel `QStringList blocks` and the `rawCoords` list are gone; duplicate-region detection compares normalized rects directly over `page.boxes`, skipping fragments the model did not place (`BoundingBox::positioned == false`: the untagged preamble and XML-drift headers without a bbox, which all share the zero rect). The tolerance is 1 % of the page in normalized coordinates (the same 10 units of 0–1000). | The three lists desynchronised twice — an empty-text block was appended to `page.boxes` but not to `blocks`, and `dupIndex` was an index into `rawCoords` (bbox tokens only) that was then used to write `page.boxes` and the image index. With an unpositioned fragment or an empty block in front, the "replacement" overwrote the wrong box, the wrong Markdown block and the wrong `image://ocr/crop/<n>`. Tests: three new cases (drift-then-duplicate, preamble-then-duplicate, and the `page.text == rebuildText(page)` invariant over five reply shapes); the 48 existing parser cases are unchanged. |
| 112  | **One parser per model, named after the model.** `DetTokenParserBase` holds the engine every det-token model shares — token scan → normalized `boxes` → Markdown through `rebuildText()`, the OTSL and `<table>` sub-formats, service-token stripping, the no-header fallback with its diagnostic. `UnlimitedOcrParser` (`unlimited-ocr`) and `Lfm25VlParser` (`lfm2.5-vl`) each answer three questions about the reply they read: the header pattern, whether the stream escapes line breaks as `\n`, and what to repair before tokenizing. The model profile names its own parser in `roles.<role>.parser`, `auto` still resolves it (ADR 88), and a stored `parser/id = det_tokens` is rewritten at startup to the selected model's parser. **The criterion: a new model is one JSON profile, one small subclass and two CMake lines, with no edit to an existing parser.** | ADR 87 cost +530 lines inside the one `det_tokens` parser and the additions were not removable from it: the `image_index=` header, the XML-drift repair (its own module) and the OTSL leak in non-table blocks all came with comments naming the model that needed them. A shared parser is only shareable while no model has a shape of its own, and by the third one the parser *is* the accumulated patch set. What stays shared is what is genuinely about the block content (the vocabulary is already per-model data — `BlockStyleMap`, ADR 88), and what moves to the subclass is what is about the reply. The stage-4 split of the engine (ADR 89) is still deferred: two models of one family do not pay for it. |
| 103  | **The page owns its text; `PageEditStore` keeps only the revert baseline and the "edited" marker.** All text writes go through `AppController::setPageText()` (it keeps `result.text` and `result.pages[0].text` in step) and all reads through `pageText()`. The store shrank to `reset/baseline/revert/isEdited/setEdited` + the page-index remaps (now generic helpers in `PageIndex.h`), and `ExportController` lost its `PageEditStore&` dependency — it reads `page.result.text` like everyone else. Revert restores the *recognized* text, which is what the button promises; typing what the page shows is a no-op, not a revert. | The text lived in two places: `OcrResult::text` (parse time) and the edit store. `setCurrentPageText` compared the user's input against the parse-time text, so re-typing the visible text deleted the edit-store entry and **resurrected a removed block / lost a verified correction**. The same stale comparison made `revertBlockCorrection` unreliable. Tests: `TestAppImport::typingTheVisibleTextBackKeepsStructuralEdits` plus a new `test_page_edit_store` (baseline, revert, remap after remove/move) — the store had no unit test before. |
| 104  | **The document's threading contract is written down instead of implied.** `DocumentModel` and `AppController` document that the page list and results are GUI-thread resident and name the two deliberate crossings: the export pipeline resolves block crops from a worker thread (holding the document lock), and the image provider may render a page. Callers are told to go through a locking accessor rather than taking the model directly. The lock itself stays. | The review found the lock applied at 12 of 63 `m_document` accesses, which reads as "mostly safe" while guaranteeing nothing. Re-checking it during this stage corrected the review in one respect: the export worker really does read the document through `croppedImage()`, so the lock is load-bearing there — it is not a data race today, but a lock held across a full render from a worker. The remaining defect (a page render — up to 30 s of DjVu decoding — happening inside the synchronous image provider) is unchanged and is tracked as C1 in `docs/architecture-plan/README.md`. |
| 105  | **No full-file hashing or process probing on the GUI thread.** (a) `RuntimeController::startServer()` runs `RuntimeLocator::probeCached()` on a worker and continues in `finishStartServer()`; a generation counter drops a probe that was cancelled (Stop during start) or superseded, and a probe failure reaches `failResolve()` only when a resolve is waiting for it. `probeRuntimePath()` keeps its signature and publishes the summary in `statusMessage`, after setting «Probing <binary>…» at once. (b) A resumed download no longer re-reads its `.part` at start: sha256 cannot be seeded from a digest, so the finished file is hashed on a worker at verification time (the file is warm in the page cache) — `hashExistingPart()` is gone. (c) The install transaction decides "projector already on disk" (a multi-GB digest) on a worker, with every input captured by value. | The probe spawns the binary and waits with a **two-minute** budget for a cold cache, so the window froze for the whole start and the same freeze was reachable from the «Check»/«Probe» buttons; a resumed multi-GB download re-hashed the partial on the GUI thread before its first byte arrived. Rule for the tree: nothing that walks a file or waits for a process runs on the GUI thread. Test: `TestEnsureConnection::managedStartKeepsTheEventLoopRunning` — the mock delays its `--version` answer (`LLOCR_MOCK_VERSION_DELAY_MS`) and the test asserts the *longest gap* between 5 ms timer ticks stays below half that delay (verified in both directions: a synchronous probe measures a 1767 ms gap and fails). `TestDownloadManager::resumeWithCorruptedPrefixFailsChecksum` pins that a resumed file is still verified as a whole. |
| 106  | **The code is split into layer targets; what a layer may use is decided by the link graph.** `src/CMakeLists.txt` builds `llocr_core` → `llocr_config` → {`llocr_models`, `llocr_parsers`} → `llocr_runtime` → `llocr_app` → `llocr` (the executable keeps `main.cpp`, the resources and the QML module). `llocr_config` holds the settings/profile stores plus `RuntimePaths`, which is what breaks the old `app ↔ runtime` cycle: the runtime read `SettingsStore`/`LaunchProfileStore`/`RequestProfileStore`, and those needed `RuntimePaths` back. `LaunchProfileStore` could not stay in the config layer — it drafts through `LaunchParametersModel` (a QML list model) and asks `ReleaseCatalog::detectPlatform()` for the active preset — so it and `LaunchParametersModel` sit in `llocr_runtime`; `RequestProfileStore` has no such dependency and stays in config. `ProcessGuard_{win,mac,linux}.cpp` moved onto `llocr_runtime`, one per platform. `LLOCR_HAVE_DJVU` is now a `PUBLIC` define on `llocr_app` and `DjVuDocument.cpp` is the only conditional source (its header is unconditional, so the layout never depends on the optional dependency). The 31 tests link the same targets instead of re-listing 241 lines of sources. | Everything was one `qt_add_executable` with ~140 sources and a single include root, so the layer diagram in this document was a convention with no enforcement; a test could compile against a different translation-unit set than the app; and touching a `core/` header rebuilt the whole program. The split made the dependencies real — the first configure surfaced two hidden couplings (the QML include path for `SelfTestController.h`/`ModelInstaller.h`, and `LaunchProfileStore`'s dependency on the release catalog) that the per-test source lists had been papering over. Editing one `core/` header now rebuilds 2 translation units instead of ~140. |
| 107  | **The llama-server owner record is read back, and a download group reports its own progress.** (a) `ProcessGuard` gained `isProcessAlive`/`processImagePath`/`terminateProcess` (per platform: `/proc/<pid>/exe` + `kill` on Linux, `proc_pidpath` on macOS, `QueryFullProcessImageNameW`/`TerminateProcess` on Windows). `ServerOwner` reads `<rootDir>/owner.json` and decides whether the recorded process is an orphan: the pid must be alive, the image must match the recorded program (canonical path, then file name), and our own server never counts; where the platform cannot report the image, the recorded *parent* being gone is the evidence instead. `RuntimeController` scans at construction and when the runtime directories change, exposing `orphanDetected` / `orphanInfo` / `terminateOrphan()`, and the footer offers «Stop it» — the process is never killed without a click. (b) `DownloadGroup::progress()` sums over the tasks *it* enqueued instead of the manager's aggregate. | `owner.json` was written and cleared but never read, while ADR 30/47 and the runtime plan promised next-start detection of an orphaned server — on macOS, the platform whose `ProcessGuard` is a no-op, a hard-killed GUI left `llama-server` holding the model in VRAM and the next start picked a new port without noticing. A recycled pid must never be killed, hence the image check. And the manager is shared and keeps up to ten terminal tasks, so a second install's progress bar was diluted by the first one's leftovers and stalled below 100 %. Tests: `TestEnsureConnection::orphanedServerIsDetectedAndCanBeTerminated` (dead pid / our own pid / a live foreign process, then termination) and `TestDownloadManager::groupProgressIsPerGroupNotTheManagerAggregate` (verified to fail against the manager-aggregate implementation). |

| 108  | **One HTTP policy for the runtime layer.** `HttpClient` owns the transfer timeout, manual redirect following, a hop budget, https-only (or explicitly allowed loopback), and the rule that the `Authorization` header never follows a redirect to another host; the system proxy is applied from the request itself. `ModelCatalog` and `ReleaseCatalog` use its blocking `get()` (worker threads only — ADR 105) and keep the Hugging Face `Link` pagination and the GitHub rate-limit hint; `DownloadTask` keeps its resume logic (`Range`/`If-Range`/206) and only borrows the policy; `RuntimeController`'s `/v1/models` call goes through it as well. | The same policy was spelled out four times, and the system proxy was enabled as a side effect of constructing `DownloadManager` — so a build that never constructed one silently ignored the user's proxy settings. Test: `test_http_client` (a two-hop loopback server proves the token is sent on the first hop and dropped on the second; a redirect loop fails instead of spinning). |
| 109  | **One `InstalledState` answers “where is the runtime, and where is its lock”.** A single instance created in `main.cpp` is handed to `RuntimeController`, `RuntimeInstaller`, `ModelInstaller` and `ModelInstallTransaction`; it derives `RuntimePaths` from the settings on every call and recreates the install lock (`QLockFile` cannot be renamed) when the directory moves, emitting `pathsChanged` so the installed-builds list is re-scanned. | `RuntimeInstaller` captured its paths **and** its `QLockFile` in the constructor while `ModelInstaller`, `ModelInstallTransaction` and `RuntimeController` built fresh paths per call — so moving the runtime directory left the installer downloading and locking in the old tree while `rescanInstalledBuilds()` already looked in the new one, and the install guard protected a directory nothing was installing into. Test: `TestEnsureConnection::movingTheRuntimeDirectoryMovesTheInstallLock`. The other half of the old D4 — reconciling the *three* sources of truth (settings vs `index.json` vs the filesystem scan) with an explicit priority — is deliberately not claimed: `ModelRegistry::load()` already merges the scan into the index, and a real reconciliation policy is a design decision, not a bug fix. |

| 110  | **The OCR model and the OCR sampling profile have separate ids, and the UI picks the model by id.** `model/recipeId` names the adapter; the new `model/requestProfileId` names the sampling profile (same default, so an untouched profile behaves exactly as before, and `applyStartupMigration()` adopts whatever `model/recipeId` held once). The model list is a real `OcrModelListModel` (`modelId` / `displayName` roles) exposed as `Controller.ocrModels`, and `RequestTab` binds `textRole`/`valueRole`; `Controller.modelNames` / `modelIdToName` / `modelNameToId` are gone, and `OcrModelFactory::idForDisplayName()` returns an empty id for an unknown name instead of the default. | One setting carried two meanings: picking a model in Settings → Model also swapped the sampling profile, and the combo round-tripped through *display names*, where an unknown name silently resolved to the default model — the UI could show one model while recognition used another. With ids as roles there is nothing left to lose. `TestRequestProfile::activeProfileIsIndependentOfTheModel` covers the decoupling; the same test file also had a latent dangling pointer (`findParameter(store.activeProfile(), …)` binds into a temporary), which is why a behaviour change there showed up as a hang instead of a failure — every occurrence now names the profile first. |
| 111  | **One chat exchange, one image encoder, and a reply path that does not depend on the adapter's lifetime.** `runChatExchange<Result>()` (models/ChatExchange.h) owns the whole round trip every adapter shared by copy: build the body on a worker, POST it, parse the reply, resolve the promise — with the body builder, the parser and the encode error as parameters. `encodeImageDataUrl()` (models/ImageDataUrl.cpp) is the single encoder and builds the data URL entirely in QByteArray instead of QString → Latin1 → arg → QJsonDocument (three extra copies of a multi-megabyte string per page). `GeneralPurposeModel` lost its copy of the machinery (220 → 171 lines, `OcrModel` 232 → 175). The body/parse hooks stay `static` on purpose, and `ConnectionConfig::timeoutMs` now defaults to the 120 s of `SettingsStore` instead of contradicting it with 240 s. | The verifier adapter had copied `encodeImageDataUrl` byte for byte, plus the whole promise/watcher pipeline and the parameter sort. Two attempts to make the hooks `virtual` were reverted: dispatching them would capture `this`, and `recognize()`/`check()` must still complete after the adapter is destroyed (shutdown with a request in flight — `futureCompletesAfterModelDestruction` hung the moment the lambda captured `this`). A different wire shape is therefore added by overriding the exchange in the subclass or by a sibling adapter, not by a hook — the extensibility story stays open, and the duplicated class is gone. |
| 112  | **Both installs publish by an atomic rename, and the model install takes the install lock.** `StagedInstall` (runtime/StagedInstall.{h,cpp}) is the single publish mechanism: everything is written into a staging directory, `commit()` moves an existing directory aside, renames staging into place and drops the backup (restoring it if the rename fails), and the destructor removes an uncommitted staging directory. `InstallTransaction` (runtime binaries) uses it, as before but without the inline copy. `ModelInstallTransaction` now takes the **same** `.install.lock` as the runtime install for the whole install, downloads into `<staging>/model-<org>__<repo>` and commits into `<modelsDir>/<org>__<repo>` in `completeInstall()`, releasing the lock on success, failure, `cancel()` and `shutdown()`. The staging name is deterministic (not a uuid) so a resumed install finds its own `.part` files, and the startup `cleanupStaging()` keeps `model-…` directories for the same reason — they live outside `<modelsDir>`, so a scan cannot adopt them. | The model install wrote straight into its final directory, took **no** install lock, and had no rollback: a cancelled or failed install left a half-populated `<org>__<repo>` that the next `scanModelsDir` accepted as a valid model, and a second instance installed into the same directory at the same time. Tests: `test_staged_install` (uncommitted staging disappears, `commit()` replaces the previous install and leaves no `.old-*`, a second commit is refused) and `TestModelInstallTransaction::cancelledInstallLeavesNoModelDirectoryAndReleasesTheLock`. The two transactions still share their domain logic rather than one parameterised engine — the duplication that remains is the *shape* (state enum, busy/progress mirroring), not the risky part; merging them further is not claimed. |
| 113  | **QML states the rules; C++ decides them.** (a) The «Launch settings changed — restart» banner is `RuntimeController::launchConfigDirty`: the configuration the live server was actually started with (`ServerLaunchConfig`, now with value equality) is compared against the one `ServerLaunchConfig::fromSettings` produces now, for the role that server serves; the flag clears when the server stops, because there is then nothing to restart. The subscription is a walk of `SettingsStore`'s meta-object (`connectEveryChangeSignal`) instead of the eleven hand-named QML signals, so a launch setting added later is picked up automatically — and one the launch config does not read (window geometry) can no longer raise a false banner. A saved `LaunchProfileStore` is subscribed explicitly. (b) The wizard step gates are `Runtime.serverPathValid` / `modelPathValid` / `configValid` — «a path that is set but missing on disk is not a configuration» — and the memory warning threshold (90 % of RAM) moved next to the estimate as `estimateModelMemory().overBudget`. | The banner was raised by eleven named signals, so a launch parameter saved into a launch profile (the most common way to change one) only worked because two store signals happened to be listed, and the wizard advanced on a non-empty string that the start then rejected with «File not found»; both rules were invisible to the tests. Tests: `TestEnsureConnection::launchConfigDirtyFollowsTheRealLaunchConfiguration` (a profile change raises it, a window-geometry change does not, a fresh start clears it) and `::configGatesRequireTheFilesToExist` — the first verified to fail against the old signal list. `retranslate()` for the app-layer controllers and the `QVariantMap` → list-model conversion are still open. |
| 114  | **A message is stored untranslated and rendered when it is read.** `StatusMessage` (core/StatusMessage.{h,cpp}) carries either verbatim text (a server reply, a Pandoc error, a path) or a translation context + source key + `%n` arguments, and `text()` renders in the language installed *right now*. `OcrResult::errorMessage`, `CheckResult::errorMessage`, `Exporter::Result::message`, `RecognitionController`/`CheckController`/`ExportController`'s `statusRequested` and `AppController::m_statusMessage` are all of that type, so `AppController::retranslate()` is just two signals — `statusChanged` and the new `retranslateRequested`, which replaced `CONSTANT` on `exportNameFilters`, `parserNames` and `parserLabels`. The factory is named `translate(context, key)`, not `tr`, because `lupdate` reads the first literal of a `translate(…)` call as the context; a `StatusMessage::tr(…)` is filed under the class and never matches at runtime. | Switching the language left the status line, the check-error line and the export dialog's file filters in the *previous* language until something else happened to overwrite them, and `exportNameFilters` was `CONSTANT`, so QML could never re-read it. Merely wiring `retranslate()` into the four controllers would have changed nothing: they cached the translated string, and there is no key left to re-render. Test: `TestAppImport::retranslateRendersTheStatusLineInTheNewLanguage` — verified to fail against a `text()` that resolves once and caches. The context names move from `llocr::X` to `X` for these controllers; the now-vanished entries are kept in `llocr_ru.ts` rather than dropped. |
| 115  | **The installer lists are list models, not stringly-typed maps.** `InstalledBuildsModel` (runtime/InstalledBuildsModel.{h,cpp}) and `InstalledModelsModel` (runtime/InstalledModelsModel.{h,cpp}) are `QAbstractListModel`s with declared roles; `RuntimeInstaller.installedBuilds` and `ModelInstaller.installedModels` / `.checkInstalledModels` expose them, and the QML delegates declare `required property` roles instead of calling `installedBuildInfo(row)` / `roleInstalledInfo(row, role)` and reading a `QVariantMap` by string key. The role split and the "active" highlight are derived from the settings on every read, so a `launchModelPath` / `checkLaunchModelPath` / `serverPath` change re-evaluates both the highlight *and* which list a model belongs to; `ModelEntry` gained value equality so a re-scan that finds the same models does not reset the model. `sourceIndex(row)` maps a row back to the installer's own list for the calls the UI drives by row. | A misspelled key produced an empty cell rather than any kind of error, and each delegate had to re-fetch its own row in a `Connections` block on every change signal — two places to keep in step, neither checked by the compiler. The role-filtering rule moved with it, so the list and the installer can no longer disagree about which models belong to which role. Test: `TestModelInstaller::installedModelsExposeNamedRoles` (role values, the declared role *names*, an out-of-range row, no reset when a re-scan changes nothing, and the highlight after activation) plus the rewritten `TestModelInstaller::roleFilteredInstalledLists` and `TestInstallLock::…` cases. |
| 116  | **One reconciliation decides between the settings, `index.json` and the filesystem — and says which of them wins.** `reconcileInstalled()` (runtime/InstalledReconcile.{h,cpp}) takes all three and returns the list plus a report. The policy, stated once: *existence is the filesystem's* (an index entry whose files are gone is dropped, a directory the scan finds is added); *measurable file facts are the filesystem's* (`modelPath`, `parts`, `byteSize`, `dir`); *curated metadata is the index's* (`revision`, `license`, `sha256`, `parser`, `prompt`, `ctxSize`, `roles`, `mmprojPath`, `quantization`, …); *the settings are pointers, not membership* — a selected model or binary that no longer resolves is reported, never silently dropped, because the file may live outside the models directory. A models directory that is not there is **unknown, not empty**: an unmounted drive leaves the index untouched. `ModelRegistry::load()` now has one path instead of four near-identical "rescan and save" branches and fills the report; `ModelInstaller` turns a stale selection into a message naming the file. | The merge only ever *added* to the index, so a model deleted by hand stayed in the list forever and offered an «Activate» that could only fail with «Model file not found»; and the three sources had no written-down priority, so every caller re-decided it. Two rules were found by tests rather than by inspection, and both cut the same way: a scan attaches the *first* projector in a folder to *every* model in it, and it re-derives the quantization from the filename — neither may overwrite what the index recorded, or a model silently moves from the check list to the recognition list. Tests: `dropsEntriesWhoseFilesAreGone`, `keepsIndexMetadataAndRefreshesFileFacts`, `keepsProjectorPairingAndRecordsItsAbsence`, `reportsStaleSelectionsWithoutDroppingThem`, `anUnreadableModelsDirectoryIsNotAnEmptyOne`, `reportsWhatItReconciled` and `TestModelInstaller::aSelectedModelThatVanishedIsReported` — the first verified to fail against the old "never drop" behaviour. This is the half of the old D4 that ADR 109 deliberately did not claim. |
| 117  | **One `ProfileStore<T>` for the profile stores, and no dead `launch/presetId`.** `app/ProfileStore.h` is a template over the profile type that owns what `LaunchProfileStore` and `RequestProfileStore` were two copies of: the built-in list, the user overrides keyed by id, the merge on read, the save that writes only the diff (deleting the file when there is none) and the reset. `ProfileStorage::readEnvelope` / `writeEnvelope` add the one `schemaVersion` policy the three stores lacked — the launch and request files carried a version nothing ever checked, so a file written by a newer build was read as current. ADR 84 rejected a *base class* because the domain logic diverges, and it still does: the merge policy stays on the type, and it is genuinely different (`LaunchProfile::merge` replaces the parameter set, which is what makes «remove this row» work; `RequestProfile::merge` merges parameter-wise, which is what a legacy id-less file needs) — the template calls it rather than deciding it. `merged()` returns the built-in verbatim when there is no user copy, because "no copy" and "a copy with no parameters" are different states. `launch/presetId` is gone: the preset installer wrote it and nobody read it (`launch/profileId` is the active profile), and a startup migration drops the key from an existing profile. The 240000/120000 timeout divergence the plan also listed was already fixed by ADR 111. | The two stores had already drifted: the schema version was written by one and ignored by both readers, and `saveDraft()` had to remember separately that selecting a built-in preset must still switch the active profile even when no file changed — a `putUserProfile()` that returned early silently stopped the preset from taking effect. `VerificationPromptStore` is deliberately *not* a profile store (its document is a block list plus a system prompt) and keeps its own shape, but uses the same envelope. |
| 118  | **The preview page render happens on a worker (the C1 half ADR 104 tracked).** `DocumentModel::renderDetached(RenderRequest)` renders one page touching **no** model state: the request carries a copy of the `DocumentPage` and the shared DjVu handle, and a PDF page gets its own `QPdfDocument` inside the worker because the model's handle is GUI-thread state and `QPdfDocument` is not safe to share. `AppController::previewImage(index)` answers from a cache, starts a worker when it has nothing, and returns the thumbnail meanwhile; `pageImageReady` plus an `imageRevision` bump make QML ask again, and the preview shows a «Rendering page…» label so a soft thumbnail is not read as the final image. A generation counter drops a result whose document moved on, and `notifyDocumentChanged()` clears the cache. Only the image provider goes through it — recognition and export keep the synchronous `pageImage()`, which already runs off the GUI thread. | `OcrImageProvider::requestImage` called `currentImage()`, so a DjVu page that takes up to 30 s to decode froze the window for its whole duration, holding the document write-lock with it. Tests: `TestAppImport::previewRenderKeepsTheEventLoopRunning` (the first call is empty, the loop keeps ticking, the full-size image arrives and is then served from the cache — verified to fail against the old inline render) and `::previewRenderIsDroppedWhenTheDocumentChanges`. **The visual pass is not machine-verifiable**: opening a DjVu book and flipping pages should be checked by a person — the thumbnail-to-full transition, the label's placement, and that no stale page is ever shown. |

| 119  | **A diagnostic has one home, and the footer is one line tall.** `ProblemLog` (app/ProblemLog.{h,cpp}, QML singleton `Log`) is the append-only place where «something did not work» is collected: the per-page decoder messages and the skipped-file reasons from an import, and the failed-check errors (`VerificationQueueController::problemReported`, forwarded by `AppController`, which holds the log through a non-owning `setProblemLog` — the same shape as `RuntimeController::setLogTarget`). Entries are `StatusMessage`s, so they render in the language installed *right now*; notifications are coalesced through a 200 ms timer (a book with 600 unreadable pages must not re-render the log 600 times) and the list is capped at 2000 entries with a translated «… N earlier entries dropped» note rather than growing silently. `ProblemLogWindow.qml` shows the log wrapped and selectable, with copy and clear. The footer keeps **one** elided line: the current page's decode error, else the check error, else the parser note — `NoWrap` + `ElideRight`, with the full text in the tooltip — plus a count and a «Details…» button. | Three wrapping labels sat above the toolbar, and the messages they held are paragraphs: one unreadable DjVu page yields a path, a page number and the decoder's own complaint, which grew the footer until the status line was pushed off the window. The same text was also in the status line, because `finishImport()` joined the summary with the first warning. Eliding is not a fix for a paragraph — the text stops being readable — so the text moved and the count stayed. Tests: `TestAppImport::aSkippedFileGoesToTheLogNotTheStatusLine` and `::theProblemLogCoalescesAndStaysBounded` / `::theProblemLogRendersOnRead`, plus the rewritten expectations in `::damagedPagesImportWithWarningsAndKeepQueueOrder` (verified to fail against the old joined status line). |
| 120  | **A list model repaints when a *derived* role changes, not only when the entries change.** `InstalledModelsModel::setEntries()` compares the *computed* row set, not the input list: a changed row set is a `modelReset`, and an unchanged one is a `dataChanged` carrying **all** the roles, because `active` and the role split are read from the settings on every call. A rescan that finds exactly the same entries therefore still repaints. The `settingsChanged()` that was meant to cover the case was never called by anyone and is gone — `ModelInstaller` already re-publishes on `launchModelPathChanged` / `checkLaunchModelPathChanged`. | Activating a model changed neither the entries nor (when both models were visible in that role) the row set, so `setEntries()` took its early return and emitted nothing. `data()` reads the settings live, so the *value* was right and only the notification was missing — the list kept the previous model marked active until the window was rebuilt, which is why closing and reopening Settings «fixed» it. `InstalledBuildsModel` never had the bug: it has no role split and its `settingsChanged()` is connected directly. Test: `TestModelInstaller::activatingAModelRepaintsTheHighlight` — verified to fail against the old early return. |

| 121  | **CI is two tiers: a Qt-free format gate on every push, the full matrix on `workflow_dispatch` only.** The workflow gained a `format` job (`scripts/check.sh --format-only` → clang-format over `src/` and `tests/`, no configure, no build, no Qt) and the matrix job now carries `if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`; the concurrency group gained `github.event_name` so a manual run is no longer cancelled by a push on the same ref. `check.sh` grew `--format-only` (configure/build/tests/lint off) rather than the workflow re-spelling the `find | xargs clang-format --Werror` line — otherwise the job and the local run could drift onto different file sets. Its `-h\|--help` range became `sed -n '2,/^$/p'` (the usage block no longer has a fixed end line) and the “Tests skipped” message lost its flag name, which `--format-only` made wrong. The workflow header's TODO was corrected in the same pass: **Qt 6.10.3 is an official online-installer build** (`~/Qt/components.xml` → `qt.qt6.6103.clang_64`, `extensions.qtwebengine.6103`; `InstallationLog.txt` → the stock `qt-online-installer-macOS-universal-4.11.0`, no Qt account), so provisioning can install the official package with `jurplel/install-qt-action`/aqtinstall (gcc_64 / macos / msvc2022_64) instead of hunting for an internal artifact. | The matrix carried `push`/`pull_request` triggers from the commit that introduced it (`6f05ec1`) while the `TODO(provision-qt)` step was never implemented, so **every** push died in ~25 s at `find_package(Qt6 6.10 REQUIRED …)` and mailed “All jobs have failed” — a gate that can never pass is worse than none, because the reader learns to ignore red and a real failure is then missed too. Clang-format is the one check that needs no toolchain, so it is the only thing worth spending a push on; everything that needs Qt stays one button away, and the Windows/macOS builds it would re-verify already have machines. Two known trade-offs: the Linux runner's apt `clang-format` is older than the local Homebrew LLVM 23.x (`.clang-format` uses only long-stable options; if the two ever disagree the local one is authoritative — `clang-format -i`), and a full three-OS Qt+WebEngine build would burn tens of minutes per push, with macOS billed at ×10 on private repositories. |
| 122  | **One models list: a row is a model, and its quantizations are chosen on the row.** `ModelQuantModel` (runtime/ModelQuantModel.{h,cpp}) replaces `InstalledModelsModel` as what the models UI binds to, and `Common/ModelDownloadList.qml` replaces `Common/ModelInstalledList` + `Common/ModelPresetList`. A row carries the model profile's quantizations (`ModelProfiles::Files::quants`) *and* the state of each — installed / active / size on disk / downloadable — so «Use» and «Download» are two states of one button, and the ⋮ menu offers **Open folder**, **Delete quantization** and **Delete model** (the last behind a confirmation, and the first two hidden for a model the user brought themselves, which the registry refuses to delete). `ModelInstaller` grew the matching API — `selectQuant` / `useQuant` / `downloadQuant` / `removeQuant` / `removeModelRow` / `openQuantFolder` — each addressed by `(row key, quantization label)`, and `presetInfo()` / `activatePreset()` / `preparePreset()` are gone: the row resolves to the registry index (`entryIndexFor`) and to the preset (`presetIndexFor`), so the actions the UI drives by row no longer address a preset list by position. Three rules decide what a row holds: (1) a quantization **on disk that the profile does not list** (hand-downloaded, or left by a catalog that moved on) stays in the row, marked not downloadable — otherwise those files have no row and can be neither activated nor deleted; (2) models that belong to no profile get a row of their own (title + folder), keeping ADR 80's role filter intact (`ModelQuantModel::matchesRole()` is the same rule, moved); (3) the **recommended** profile (`default: true`) sorts first, the rest by title. The selected quantization is remembered per profile in `models/quant/<profileId>` (`SettingsStore::selectedQuant`), resolved as *saved → active → first installed → first in the profile*; a pick that is not on the row is dropped rather than saved. `refresh()` repaints instead of resetting when the row shape is unchanged (ADR 120 again, now with the quant list inside the row). **A second quantization used to take the first one with it**: publishing an install renames the whole `<modelsDir>/<org>__<repo>` folder aside (ADR 112/117), so the quantizations already in that folder — which the old preset list made invisible — were deleted with it. `ModelInstallTransaction::preserveExistingFiles()` now moves whatever the install does not write into the staging directory before the commit (a sibling quantization, a second projector, a `README` the repo ships), leaves partial downloads alone, and rolls its own moves back if one of them fails; a name the install writes again keeps the staging copy (the one that was just verified) and drops the older one. This was found by hand, not by a test — the one-row list makes «keep two quants of a model» a single click, so the case finally mattered. | The flat preset list came from `ModelPresetCatalog::expand()` multiplying a profile by its quantizations: the user saw `Unlimited-OCR (q8_0)` and `Unlimited-OCR (q4_k_m)` as two models next to a *second* list of what was installed, so «is this quant on disk?» and «can I get it?» were in two places, and a quantization the catalog no longer listed was invisible and undeletable. Addressed by index (`presetInfo(i)` from the delegate, ADR 115's rejected pattern) it also meant the row and the installer could disagree about which preset was meant. Keeping the two datasets in one row is the only place the answer can live; the deletion guard now has all of a model's files before it touches the first one, because `removeModel()` drops the entry it is given and the registry indexes below it move. Tests: `TestModelInstallTransaction::publishingKeepsTheQuantizationsAlreadyInstalled` / `::aFileAlreadyInTheStagingDirectoryIsNotOverwritten` / `::preserveIsANoOpWhenTheFolderDoesNotExistYet`, plus `TestModelInstaller::quantRowsListTheModelsOfTheRole`, `quantRowsTrackInstalledQuantizations`, `quantRowsKeepQuantizationsTheProfileDoesNotList`, `selectedQuantIsRememberedAndYieldsToTheActiveModel`, `deletingAModelTakesEveryQuantizationOfIt`, `quantRowsExposeNamedRoles`, `activatingAQuantizationRepaintsTheRows`, `installedListFilteredByRole` (all rewritten from the `InstalledModelsModel` versions, ADR 115/120). `llocr_ru.ts` was **hand-edited** for this change: `lupdate` reformatted 581 lines and marked 45 unrelated strings unfinished (115 → 160), dropping 20 existing translations, so the new entries were added and the dead ones marked `vanished` by hand instead. The visual pass is not machine-verifiable — the row layout, the quant menu and the confirmation dialog should be looked at by a person. |
| 123  | **The check role gets a profile picker too, and the picker's order is one rule, not two.** The Request tab's «Profile» row showed the OCR model combo and, for the check window, the label with nothing beside it (`visible: !root.checkRole`): the check model is chosen on the Model tab, by path, or by an external server, and none of those paths could name a request profile — `SettingsStore::selectModelProfile()` needs a repo, and a path has none — so `checkRequestProfileId` stayed at whatever the default was. `RequestProfileStore` now owns `RequestProfileListModel` (`profileModel`: `modelId` / `displayName`, `rowOfId()`) listing the model profiles that answer **its** role, and `RequestTab` binds the combo to it for the check role and to `Controller.ocrModels` for the OCR one (both expose the same role names, so one `textRole`/`valueRole` serves both). `ModelProfiles::forRole()` — the models of a role, the recommended one (`default: true`) first, the rest by title — is now the single place that order is decided; `ModelQuantModel` (the models list) and `RequestProfileStore` (the built-in profiles *and* the picker) both call it, which is also what the store's unknown-id fallback follows: the first profile offered is the one the fallback picks. | The check window was a dead end on purpose nobody stated: the OCR model combo was made for a model *registry* (ADR 110), and the check role has none, so the control was hidden rather than given a source. Picking a request profile is independent of picking a model — `Save` already wrote `checkRequestProfileId` from the draft id (it was only the draft that could never be chosen), so the missing piece was the list, not the plumbing. Test: `TestRequestProfile::profileModelListsTheModelsOfTheRole` (both roles, the recommended-first order, an id of the other role is not offered, and the store still answers the setting) and the rewritten tail of `::activeProfileIsIndependentOfTheModel`, which now pins *which* profile the fallback returns and why. |
| 124  | **A profile that no longer needs a warning simply drops it, and nothing may hang on a shipped model carrying one.** TeleOCR's `runtimeNote` said the stock `llama-server` cannot load that GGUF (the `qwen2vl` QK-norm patch never reached upstream), which sent the model to External mode with a hand-built server. The weights moved to `konradjr007/NaviDC-OCR-GGUF`, which a stock build loads (verified on an external runtime), so the note is gone: the model runs from the managed runtime like every other one, and the warning disappears from the Launch tab, the wizard step and the download dialog. The **mechanism** stays — `ModelProfiles::runtimeNoteFor()` and the three places that show it are how the next unrunnable model says so, and `minBuild` still cannot express "needs a patched core" (it means "not older than bNNNN"). `TestModelPresetCatalog::runtimeNoteReachesTheSettings` now builds its own profile instead of borrowing teleocr's: a test that needs a model which the shipped catalog happens not to have fails the day that model becomes runnable, which is exactly the wrong moment. | The note was written when there was one affected model, so the mechanism and the example became the same thing — and the warning text stayed in three places pointing at a model that no longer needs it. Keeping the field honest ("empty unless this model really cannot run") is what makes it trustworthy: a note that is never removed is a note nobody reads. Docs `10-model-profiles.md` §6.1 and the finding in `11-profile-findings.md` #23 record the change; the shipped-profile table in the preset-catalog test was updated to the profile's two quantizations (`q4_k_m,q8_0`). |
| 125  | **The draft is composed the way the server is composed, model layer included.** `LaunchProfileStore::compose(profile, modelId, role)` merges policy + platform + the model's own rows (+ the global fallback when the model has none) — that is what `ServerLaunchConfig` starts the server with. The **draft**, the table the Launch tab shows and edits, called `compose(..., QString(), QString())`: no `modelId`, so no model layer, so the tab listed only the shared policy and the platform rows while the server ran on top of them with `ctx-size`, `image-*-tokens`, `dry-sequence-breaker` and `special` the user could not see. `reloadDraft(role)` now carries the role being edited (`m_draftRole`), resolves the model the way the runtime does (`modelRecipeId` for ocr, `checkRequestProfileId` for check — one `modelIdForRole()`, which `modelProfileMissing()` / `modelRuntimeNote()` use too), and `modelRecipeIdChanged` / `checkRequestProfileIdChanged` re-compose the draft when the model behind the open tab changes. Rows a layer owns are now **read, not edited**: `LaunchParametersModel` locks by name as well as by position prefix, so the policy, the model and the fallback rows are greyed out, `setValue` / `removeRow` / `appendRow` refuse them, and `saveDraft()` strips them — a user copy of a platform profile carrying `ctx-size` would freeze one model's values for every model on the machine (and the constructor's `dropUserProfiles()` drops exactly such rows at the next start, taking the user's platform edit with them). | The bug is what the tests could not see: `activeProfile()` was right all along, and a test on the *draft* asserted the wrong thing — `staleUserCopyIsPrunedToTheLayersItMayOwn` asserted the draft has no `ctx-size`, which was true only because the draft was incomplete; it now asserts the draft shows the fallback's 16384 and not the stale 8192 the pruned user file carried. The layers stay separate on disk — only the view is composed, and the read-only marking is what makes "greyed" mean "the profile owns this" rather than "the app cannot write this". |

| 126  | **`ctx-size` and `n-predict` belong to the machine, not to the weights.** They moved out of the model profiles and into the **platform** profiles of `serverLaunch.json` (`n-gpu-layers`, `flash-attn`, `ctx-size`, `n-predict` per os+backend; 16384 by default, 8192 for `cpu`), because what they follow is how much memory the machine has — not what the weights need — and the user turns them where they already turn `n-gpu-layers`: Settings → Launch. What stays per-model is what the weights decide: `image-min/max-tokens`, `dry-sequence-breaker`, `special`; `teleocr` and `qwen3.5-4b` now declare **no** launch parameters at all, which the composition has to allow. Two consequences the code had to absorb: (1) the **global fallback** names the same two parameters, so `compose()` now fills gaps by name instead of appending — a second `ctx-size` row would put `--ctx-size` on the command line twice (`ServerLaunchConfig::toArguments()` de-duplicates nothing but the reserved names) and the server would refuse to start; (2) the Launch tab's "not in the catalog" notice asks `ModelProfiles::find()` rather than "does this model declare a launch list", because a catalog model with an empty layer is a normal thing now and the old predicate would have warned on three of the four models, with a text ("This model is not in the catalog") that had become false. | The layer boundary is the point of the design: a parameter that applies to every model on this machine must not sit in a per-model file, and a per-model one must not be clobbered by a machine-wide default. `ctx-size` was on the wrong side of that line — the profile pinned 16384 for models whose weights are the same on a 8 GB laptop and a 64 GB workstation, and the user could not change it without an external runtime. Composing the machine's value first and the model's last means the two can now coexist: a model that *does* declare `ctx-size` still wins, and one that declares nothing takes the machine's. Tests: `TestLaunchProfile::theMachinesContextWinsOverTheGlobalFallback` (the platform's 8192 stands, the name appears once, the row is the user's to edit) and `::modelParametersArePerRole` rewritten for the new predicate; the pinned launch lists in `TestModelPresetCatalog` lost `ctx-size,n-predict`. |

| 127  | **A build tag is a number, and the stored profile follows the weights that are actually loaded.** Two defects, both reported from the same screenshot: the footer refused to start with «llocr-unlimited-ocr needs llama.cpp b4000 or newer (this build is b11312)» — a build three times the minimum — and it named the **wrong model** besides. (1) `RuntimeController::modelBuildError()` compared `probe.capabilities.build` with the profile's `minBuild` by `QString::compare`, so `«b11312» < «b4000»` as text: **every five-digit build** was refused as older than every four-digit one, and the refusal pointed at Settings → Runtime for a runtime that was already new enough. The comparison moved to `ServerCapabilities::buildAtLeast()`, which parses both tags to numbers through the existing `extractBuildNumber()`; a tag it cannot read does not refuse a start (the probe reports that instead). `ServerCapabilities::detect()` had always compared numerically (`belowMinimum` against `kMinimumBuildNumber`) — the two halves of the same question now agree. (2) `modelRecipeId` had been left pointing at the previously selected model: TeleOCR's weights moved to the `konradjr007/NaviDC-OCR-GGUF` repo (ADR 124), but the model was installed on 30 Sep against the old `nandraj/` repo, so the registry entry's repo matched no profile, `SettingsStore::selectModelProfile()` returned early **silently**, and the id stayed `unlimited-ocr`. The alias, the parser, the prompt and the launch rows then all belonged to weights that were not loaded — which is why the footer named Unlimited-OCR while TeleOCR was active. `ModelInstaller::syncProfileToActiveModel()` re-derives the profile from the active entry's repo on every `refreshInstalled()`, so a profile that is renamed, moved or arrives after the model no longer strands the selection. | The refusal named a model and a version at once, and only one of the two was believable: a user who reads «needs b4000 or newer» on a b11312 build concludes the version check is broken, and the model name in the same sentence is the part they trust — so it was checked second, and it was the actual fault. The silent early return in `selectModelProfile()` is the deeper of the two: it made "the catalog does not know this repo" indistinguishable from "the selection did not change", so a settings-reset-shaped desync (ADR 61/62) had no repair path. Comparing versions as strings is the kind of defect that survives review because the sample values in the fixtures (`b4000`, `b10594`) differ in length *and* in leading digit, and every hand-picked pair of them happens to sort correctly in one direction only. Tests: `TestCapabilities::buildTagsCompareByNumber` (the five-digit case that started it, plus an unreadable tag on either side), `TestEnsureConnection::minBuildIsComparedByNumber` (the user's own values, b11312 against `minBuild: b4000`, asserted through the refusal text the footer shows — this target gained the shipped model profiles as a Qt resource, without which `minBuild` was empty and the check could not run at all), and `TestModelInstaller::theStoredProfileFollowsTheActiveModel` (a registry entry whose repo matches a profile, next to another model's stored id). Both fixes were verified by reverting each and watching its test fail. |

| 128  | **The manual-path options are gone: a managed runtime means the app-installed one, and a model means one from the catalog.** Removed from the UI and the settings: the «Managed local server (Specify llama.cpp binary)» connection-mode variant (the `runtime/serverPathIsManaged` flag — managed now always means the downloaded/activated build, `Settings.serverPath` is written only by the installer and `activateBuild`), the model-source combo «Specify model files» / «Download model» in Settings → Model and the wizard's Model step (the `launch/sourceDownload` / `check/sourceDownload` flags), and with them every path field + `FileDialog`: the model/mmproj pickers in `LocationTab` and `StepModel`, the binary pickers in `RuntimeTabInternal` and `StepRuntime`, and the whole wizard `StepBinary.qml` (deleted; the wizard's welcome cards went 3 → 2 and `SetupWizard.rebuildSteps` lost its `stepBinary` branch). The dead code followed: `SettingsStore` lost the three properties/keys, `RuntimeController` lost `probeRuntimePath()` and the `localPath()` invokable (the probe-the-binary-UI's only reasons to exist; `RuntimeLocator::probe`/`probeCached` stay — the install transaction and `startServer()` probe on their own), `RuntimeLocator::probeSummary()` (only `probeRuntimePath` read it), and `applyStartupMigration()` now removes the three orphaned keys from profiles written by older versions. A user who had chosen their own binary keeps working: `serverPath` is still what `startServer()` launches, the flag just no longer exists. | The options were test-era affordances: three connection-mode entries where two carry meaning, a source combo defaulting to «Specify model files» (the flags defaulted to `false`) in front of the download catalog that is the actual flow, and a wizard step that asked for a path before the app could have validated anything about it. Every path field also duplicated what «Activate» on an installed model writes automatically. `llocr_ru.ts` hand-edited per ADR 122 practice (dead entries `vanished`, «Managed local server» and the two reworded `StepRuntime` strings translated); tests touched: `test_settings_store` (defaults, no flag), `test_install_lock` (the `serverPathIsManaged` assert). |

| 129  | **The profile pickers left the tabs — a profile is now something the app resolves, not something the user picks.** Removed: the «Profile» combo + «Restore profile» button from the Request and Launch tabs of both model-settings windows (the window-level «Restore defaults» footer button stays and is the one reset path), the «Launch profile» combo from the wizard's Check-and-launch step, and with them `RequestProfileStore::selectDraftProfile()`, `LaunchProfileStore::selectDraftProfile()`, the launch store's `presetIds`/`presetNames`/`otherPresetNames`/`applicablePresets()` (pure picker fodder — `activeProfileId()` already iterates the presets itself) and the «These profiles need another one» notice row. `ModelSettingsWindow`'s Save no longer writes the request draft id back into `modelRecipeId`/`checkRequestProfileId` — that write-back was the picker's commit path. What replaced picking: **the draft follows the selection.** `RequestProfileStore` connects `modelRecipeId`/`requestProfileId` (ocr) or `checkRequestProfileId` (check) to a `followActiveProfile()` that reloads the draft whenever the active id changed — activating a model on the Model tab (which calls `selectModelProfile`) or an install completing now re-points the Request table live. `LaunchProfileStore::ensureProfileResolved()` (already wired to `runtimeBackendChanged` — i.e. a build install or activate) additionally reloads the draft from the newly resolved preset, so the Launch table always shows what the installed build will be started with. In **External** mode the Model tab gained the one picker that earns its place: a «Model profile» combo over `RequestProfileListModel` (the ADR 123 machinery, now with its original job) that writes `modelRecipeId`+`requestProfileId` (ocr) or `checkRequestProfileId` (check) — the external server owns the weights, so the profile is the only thing the app *can* be told. `AppController::ocrModels` and `OcrModelListModel` are deleted (the Request-tab picker was their last consumer). Also fixed en route: the ADR 128 commit had dropped the whole `applyStartupMigration()` key-cleanup block (`launch/modelAlias` + the three retired flags), which `migrationDropsTheStoredAlias` catches — restored. | Three pickers said the same thing in three vocabularies («which profile's rows am I editing?») while the app already knew the answer from the model/runtime selection; the combo defaulted sensibly and nobody switched it, but everybody paid for it in UI rows, store API (`selectDraftProfile` + cancel semantics), and the Save-button write-back that could silently change the model. The one case with no app-side answer — an external server the app cannot interrogate — keeps a picker, and it drives the same settings ids the automatic paths write, so there is exactly one notion of "the current profile". Tests: `TestRequestProfile::draftFollowsTheModelSelection` (rewritten from `selectDraftProfileSwitchesDraft`) and `TestLaunchProfile::draftFollowsTheResolvedProfile` (new); the picker-only `thePresetListHoldsOnlyWhatThisMachineCanRun` and `storeSelectDraftProfileSwitchesRows` are gone. `llocr_ru.ts` hand-edited (dead entries vanished, «Model profile» and the reworded StepLaunch hint translated). |
| 130  | **Header consolidation, one Stop, and the installed-builds list takes the models-list row shape.** (1) The Header's standalone «Open…» and «Export…» buttons are gone — both moved into a new **File** menu (first control on the left, before «Recognize»); the `openFileRequested`/`exportRequested` signals and their enable rules are unchanged. (2) There is one Stop button in the Header, placed right after «Check all»: it stops the recognition job when `Controller.busy` and the verify queue otherwise (`stopCheck()`), and the Footer's separate check-Stop button is deleted (the import-Stop stays — import has no header counterpart). (3) `Common/RuntimeBuildsList.qml` is rebuilt on the `ModelDownloadList` row pattern (ADR 122): two text lines on the left (build token over backend type), then the size column, one action button («Active»/«Activate») and a ⋮ menu with **Open folder** and **Delete build** (behind a confirmation dialog); the standalone per-row «Open folder» button is gone. Supporting C++: `InstalledBuildInfo` gains `sizeBytes` (recursive directory size, computed in `InstallTransaction::scanInstalledBuilds`), the model exposes it as the `sizeBytes` role, and `RuntimeInstaller::removeBuild(index)` deletes a non-active build's directory under the install lock (active build refused with an actionable message, busy refused, removal failure reported); the list emits `actionError` which `RuntimeTabInternal` and `StepRuntime` show in a red caption row. `StepRuntime`'s `rowHeight: 34` override is dropped (the two-line row needs the default 46). | Two stop buttons for the same footer row (one per busy kind) and two header buttons that are each used once per document made both bars noisier than their jobs warrant; a File menu is where every other desktop app keeps Open/Export. The builds list had drifted from the models list it sits next to (single text line, no size, an always-visible Open-folder button), so the same row grammar — build / size / one action / ⋮ menu — now covers both, and per-build deletion replaces the all-or-nothing «Clean up unused builds» as the way to drop one old build. `llocr_ru.ts` updated via `update_translations` with the new strings hand-translated («Файл», «Удалить сборку», «Сборка %1 будет удалена с диска.», «Эта сборка активна — сначала активируйте другую», «Не удалось удалить %1», «Удалена сборка %1»). The row layout is a visual change — the builds row, the ⋮ menu and the delete confirmation should be looked at by a person. |
| 131  | **A project is a self-contained `.llocr` ZIP: `project.json` + the embedded original sources.** The container stores the *original files* the pages were imported from (images whole, a multi-page PDF/DjVu once for all of its pages), not renders: `DocumentModel` re-renders full-resolution pages lazily from the source and evicts them, so embedding the source is the only form that keeps a reopened project fully functional — re-recognition, verification crops and export all work, with zero new rendering code. New pieces: `runtime/ZipWriter` (store-only ZIP writer; payloads are already compressed, and `ArchiveExtractor` reads what it writes — UTF-8 names, patched CRCs, central directory) and `app/ProjectStore` (schema version 1: sources + ordered pages; per page the effective text, the recognized baseline and the edited flag per ADR 102/103, the boxes with `checkStatus`/`correctedText`/rects, `parseNote`, `hasDuplicates`; `format`/`version` envelope like the profile stores). `AppController::saveProject` snapshots on the GUI thread and writes in a worker (atomic tmp+rename; a gone source file falls back to the page's resident rendered image, else the save refuses and names the file); `openProject` extracts in a worker into a session `QTemporaryDir` the controller owns (the sources' lifetime), then rebuilds the document through the existing `appendFile`/`removePage`/`movePage` path — a saved page maps to the first appended range that still has its `(source, page index)` slot free, which also reconstructs a file imported twice. File menu: «Open project…», «Save project» (reuses the path, `Ctrl+S`), «Save project as…» (`Ctrl+Shift+S`); `projectBusy` feeds the footer spinner; the window title shows the project file. App-level settings (connection, models, parser, verification prompts) are *not* project state. | References-instead-of-embeds breaks the project the moment a source moves; per-page renders cap every later recognition at the render's DPI and cost one PNG per page; one JSON with base64 has no PDF dedup and +33% size. Rebuilding on load through the public `DocumentModel` API (not a deserializer that pokes pages directly) is what keeps the load path honest: the same eviction, the same lazy rendering, the same DjVu path. Tests: `test_project_store` (ZipWriter↔Extractor round-trip, unsafe names, full data round-trip, embedded-bytes equality, fallback image, missing source, format/version rejection) and `TestAppImport::projectSaveAndReopenRestoresTheWorkingState` (recognize → remove a block → edit text → reorder → save → reopen: pages, boxes, edits, baseline revert, re-render of the embedded raster, save+open again from the session dir). Dirty-tracking («unsaved changes») is deliberately v1-out, consistent with «Open…». |
| 132  | **«Recognize all» skips the pages that already have a result; «Recognize» does not.** The skip predicate became a reason instead of a bool — `PageSkip { None, Unreadable, AlreadyRecognized }` with `SkipResolver = std::function<PageSkip(int, bool batch)>`, so one callback serves both rules and the batch flag decides. `AppController`'s resolver returns `AlreadyRecognized` for `page.recognized` only when `batch` is true: re-running the OCR for the page you are looking at is exactly what the single-page button is for, while a batch must not redo work that already has an answer (and, on a document opened from a `.llocr` project, must not discard the manual edits and verification results that page carries). The new `firstPageToRecognize()` scans before the run and **bails out before `setBusy()`/`ensureConnectionReady()`** when nothing is left — otherwise a click on a fully recognized document would resolve a connection, which starts the managed server and loads a model for zero pages (the pre-existing unreadable-page skip had the same hole). Status lines follow the counters: «Nothing to recognize: N page(s) already recognized, M unreadable page(s).» and «Recognition finished.» + the same report, which replaced the single `Recognition finished. Skipped %1 unreadable page(s).` string. | The old rule only knew one reason to skip, so «recognize all» re-ran the model on every page of a document that had already been processed — the cost of a model round trip per page, and it silently replaced verified blocks and manual edits with a fresh guess. The early bail-out matters more than it looks: `ensureConnectionReady` is not free (a server start plus a model load), so a no-op click used to pay for it. Tests: `TestAppImport::theBatchSkipRuleOnlySkipsPagesWithAResult` (the rule in isolation: only page 1 of 4 is sent, `startCurrent` on a page with a result still sends it, an all-recognized batch sends nothing and never becomes busy) and `::recognizeAllSkipsPagesThatAlreadyHaveAResult` (the same through the real `AppController` + document). `recognitionSkipsPlaceholdersWithoutRequestingImages` now asserts `busySpy.count() == 0` for the all-unreadable batch — the early exit it used to not have. |
| 133  | **Stop stops the job, not the server.** The Header's one Stop (ADR 130) already branched to `stopCheck()` when a verification was running, but `CheckController::stop()` also called `RuntimeController::cancelPendingStart()` — so stopping a check killed the runtime work the check itself had asked for: with a separate check model every «Check page/all» triggers a role switch (`beginRoleSwitch()` → server stop → start with the check model), and a Stop pressed while that model was loading stopped the server for good. That call is gone: the managed server is app-wide state with its own explicit control («Stop server» in the footer, Settings → Runtime), and a job that borrows it must not tear it down. `RecognitionController::stop()` keeps `cancelPendingStart()` — for recognition the start and the request are one thing, so cancelling it is the "don't load a model for a job I just cancelled" courtesy. A stopped check stays `checkBusy` (status «Stopping…») until the resolve lands, then reports «Stopped before check started.» and no `checkFinished` — the abandoned block produces no result. Footer leftovers from the moved button are gone: `requestStop()`/`doStop()` (the Stop-button-shaped pair that only the runtime toggle ever used) are inlined into the toggle, and the dialog is `serverStopConfirmDialog` — the footer no longer reads as if it owned a Stop button. | The user-visible symptom was «Stop during verification stops the server», and the cause was a courtesy copied from recognition into a job that does not own the server: the check aborts its own HTTP request (correct) and nothing else. Ownership is the rule that separates the two — recognition's start exists only to serve that request, the check's start is the shared server that the footer toggle, the wizard and the next recognition all expect to find running. Test: `TestEnsureConnection::stoppingACheckLeavesTheManagedServerRunning` (managed mode + mock server with a delayed bind, check stopped while the resolve is in `Starting`: no result is reported, the server still reaches `Ready`, `Stopped` never appears); `test_ensure_connection` now links `llocr_app` for `CheckController`. |
| 134  | **The verification queue asks only for blocks that have no answer yet — «Проверить страницу» and «Проверить всё» included.** The `checkStatus != NotChecked` filter that ADR 91 had added for the auto-check became unconditional: `collectEnabledBoxes()` skips answered blocks for both header entry points, so `VerificationQueueController::checkAllEnabledBlocks()` and `checkPageEnabledBlocks()` lost the `onlyUnchecked` parameter (it is now always true, and `AppController::checkAllEnabledBlocks()` follows). An empty queue reports «Nothing to check: N block(s) already verified.» instead of doing nothing silently — the ADR 132 wording, mirrored. The per-block «Verify» button (`checkSelectedBlock` → `checkBlock`) is deliberately unchanged: an explicit per-block re-verify still works, and a re-recognized block has no answer again, so it returns to the queue. | Both buttons used to re-run the whole set, so a second «Проверить всё» paid the verifier for every block on the document a second time and overwrote a stored `FIX` with a fresh guess — the exact loss ADR 132 fixed for «Recognize all». Once the filter is unconditional the parameter had one caller value left, and a status line is needed because "nothing to check" became the normal outcome on a verified document rather than a rare edge. Test: `TestAppImport::theQueueAsksOnlyForBlocksWithoutAnAnswer` — the in-process chat stand-in now answers **both** protocols (the check request is the one carrying a `system` message) and counts requests, which is the only way to tell "nothing was sent" from "it was re-checked and agreed": page queue covers both blocks (3 requests), a second «Check page» and a «Check all» send none and the status line says why, «Verify» on one block sends one, a re-recognition puts both blocks back in the queue. `llocr_ru.ts` hand-edited: «Проверять нечего:», «уже проверенных блоков: %1». |
| 135  | **Duplicate handling: text-based detection, tail-joining, and a grey suspect mark for the verification pass.** (a) The parser's dedup is no longer bbox-only: after the exact-rect pass (ADR 102) a second pass drops a block whose text substantially repeats an earlier block — normalized texts equal, or a verbatim run of ≥ 8 words covering ≥ 60 % of the shorter block (word-level longest-common-substring on letter/digit tokens, whitespace/punctuation-insensitive). The real-world failure this catches is the model re-emitting a paragraph at **shifted** coordinates (zero geometric overlap) or resuming mid-sentence from the previous block. A re-emission that repeats most of the candidate and continues with fresh text is **tail-joined**: the unique tail (≥ 8 chars) is glued onto the earlier block (space, or directly after a hyphen), otherwise the more complete copy wins. (b) Every surviving block that had duplicates merged into or dropped against it gets `BoundingBox::duplicateSuspect` (persisted in `.llocr` projects, ADR 131).
