The verification queue treats it as if its type were enabled in Settings → Проверка (`collectEnabledBoxes` / `pageVerificationSupported` / `allPageVerificationSupported`), and the image-preview / block-panel status dot shows **grey** (`Theme.nothing`) while it is still `NotChecked` — green/yellow/red once verified. A short-text guard (< 16 chars or < 8 words) keeps repeated titles, captions and equation tags out of the net; slightly overlapping blocks with distinct text are untouched. Tests: `dropsTextDuplicatesAtShiftedCoordinates`, `dropsPartialTextDuplicates` (tail asserted in the page text), `keepsOverlappingBlocksWithDistinctText`, suspect-flag asserts in `detectsAndReplacesNearDuplicateBboxes`, `duplicateSuspect` round-trip in `test_project_store`. | bbox-only dedup missed the dominant garble mode of the det-token models: the duplicated paragraphs in the field sample shared no pixels with the originals, and the resumed block carried a unique sentence fragment that wholesale dropping destroyed — joining the tail keeps the reading order intact without a second model pass. The join is exactly the kind of edit the second-LLM check (ADR 90) exists to audit, so the survivor is routed into that pipeline regardless of the block-type filter and shown in grey until the verifier answers. |
| 136  | **LFM2.5-VL output fixes: equation blocks lose their inner `$…$` wrappers, and the model's coordinate inset is mapped back out.** Two field defects from one real page (Unlimited-OCR paper p. 7). (a) The model writes equations already dollar-wrapped with the formula number outside — `$C_{MHA}(T) = L_m + T$. (5)` — and `formatEquation` wrapped that verbatim into `$$…$$`; the inner dollars break KaTeX and the whole block rendered as red literal LaTeX. The dollar wrapper (single or double) is now stripped and the trailing `(n)` becomes `\tag{n}`, matching the `\[…\] … \tag{n}` style the same model emits elsewhere. (b) Every coordinate the model emits is grounded not on the page but on its **3:2 letterbox of the page** — the width fills the grounding canvas, the height is centered at (H/W)/1.5 of it (an A4 page: model_y = 0.945·true_y + 27.5 of 1000, x exact; measured against the pixel geometry of a real reply — residuals ≤ 4 units against +20/−25 uncorrected). `DetTokenParserBase` gained a `calibrateRect()` hook (identity by default) applied where `parse()` normalizes a token, `Lfm25VlParser` overrides it with the inverse letterbox map (a page taller than 3:2 letterboxes the width instead), and `ParserOptions::pageAspect` (H/W, A4 by default) carries the page geometry — `AppController::applyRawResult` fills it from `DocumentPage::pixelSize`. The exact-rect duplicate pass (ADR 102) compares calibrated rects. If a llama.cpp/LiquidAI update removes the letterbox, deleting the override restores identity. Tests: `calibratesInsetCoordinates`, `calibrationFollowsThePageAspect`, `stripsDollarWrappersInEquations`; `normalizesSwappedCoordinates` / `honorsBboxRange` re-pinned (x is exact for the usual portrait page). | The overlay and the normalization were correct — the model's own coordinates were biased, so nothing app-side could draw them onto the right pixels. The first eyeball fit (an inset on both axes) was wrong and over-corrected; remeasuring the source image programmatically (row/column darkness bands against the raw reply) showed x exact and y letterboxed, and the 3:2 canvas explains the numbers exactly: 1.4142/1.5 = 0.943 ≈ 0.945, bars (1−0.943)/2 ≈ 0.028 ≈ 27.5/1000. A per-model affine in the parser is the same shape of repair as the drift/OTSL pre-passes (ADR 87/112), and the hook keeps it out of the shared engine. |
| 137  | **Speculative-decoding draft modules (DSpark/MTP) are a first-class part of a model profile.** `ModelProfiles::Module` gains optional `repo`/`revision` (empty = the profile's main repo/its pinned head), so a draft that ships in its own HF repo — `lfm25-vl-3b` now carries `files.mtp` = `LFM2.5-VL-3B-DSpark-F16.gguf` from `LiquidAI/LFM2.5-VL-3B-DSpark-GGUF` with a pinned sha256 — is expressed declaratively. The install pipeline downloads it: `ModelPreset` carries `mtp`/`mtpRepo`/`mtpRevision` (`expand()` copies them, sha into the pinned map), `beginPrepare` resolves the draft repo's head sha (when unpinned) and locates the file by leaf name (a missing file is an install error), `beginDownload` checks mmproj **and** draft against disk in one worker pass (`mmprojAlreadyOnDisk` generalized to `moduleAlreadyOnDisk(rel, …, QString ModelEntry::* which)`), and `completeInstall` records `ModelEntry::draftPath` (persisted in `index.json`, byte-size included) and activates it per role. Launch: `launch/draftPath` + `check/draftPath` settings (cleared when a model without a draft is activated), `ServerLaunchConfig::draftPath` emits `--model-draft` (a core single-sourced flag; `model-draft` joined the reserved profile-row names), and the draft feeds the role-switch/launch-dirty comparisons via the config equality automatically. The spec flags themselves are model data, not code: `--spec-type draft-dspark`, `--spec-draft-n-max 8`, `--spec-draft-n-min 0` live in the profile's `launch` layer. The registry scan classifies sidecar file names (`dspark`/`mtp`/`draft`) as a new `ModelFileKind::Draft` — a draft in a model's directory is attached as the scanned `draftPath` (same rule as the projector) instead of growing a phantom standalone model entry, and reconcile keeps the recorded draft pairing with the index's curated metadata. Unknown-flag builds degrade by the existing capability retry; the exactness of DSpark under greedy decoding means a silently dropped spec flag costs speed, never correctness. Tests: `scanAttachesDraftAndDoesNotListItAsAModel`, `draftModuleReachesThePresets`, `argvPassesTheDraftModelPath`, Draft cases in `detectsRoles` / `argvSkipsReservedNames`. | A standalone drafter shares embeddings with the target model, so pairing it is a launch-time concern exactly like the mmproj projector — same lifecycle (download with the model, record, activate, remove with the dir), same "filesystem vs index" rules. Without repo support the draft could not be downloaded at all (different repo), and without the scan rule every DSpark install would surface a second, unusable "model" in the lists. The profile-driven flags keep llama.cpp's CLI drift out of C++ (ADR 41/57 discipline). |
| 138  | **LFM2.5-VL-3B doubles as the verification model** (profile data, no new code paths). `lfm25-vl-3b` declares a `check` role alongside `ocr`: alias `llocr-verify`, the same greedy sampling the verifier protocol expects (temperature 0, penalties off, `cache_prompt` on), `maxOutput` 8192, and a launch layer **identical to the OCR role's** (`special` + the three `spec-*` DSpark rows) — the dual-role identity rule from ADR 72/80's shipped-profile test: startup flags belong to the weights, so a role switch of the same model must not unload it, and the DSpark drafter accelerates verification for free (`serverRunsRole` neutralizes the alias, so the differing per-role alias never triggers a restart). The check window's model list and the check request-profile picker are built from `ModelProfiles::forRole("check")`, so the new role flows into them data-driven; activation of an LFM quant as the verifier now selects the family's own request profile (`selectModelProfile` follows the role), and the speculative decoding stays exact under the protocol's greedy decoding. | A general-purpose VL model that reads arbitrary text queries needs no new adapter to verify blocks — the structured protocol (ADR 90) is model-agnostic, and the model is already multimodal via its mmproj. Making the check role explicit in the profile is what surfaces it in the check window's download list; an OCR-only profile can never be picked as the verifier by design (ADR 80 role filtering). Tests updated to the new role set: `shippedProfilesAreTheOnesTheDocsDescribe` (lfm: `ocr,check`, identical launch strings), `quantRowsListTheModelsOfTheRole` / `installedListFilteredByRole` (the check lists grew a row), `activatingAModelSelectsItsFamilyProfile` (LFM activation as the verifier selects `lfm25-vl-3b`; the negative case moved to the OCR-only Unlimited-OCR family). |
| 139  | **The LFM2.5-VL verifier gets its own block prompts, and a bare FIX degrades to REVIEW.** Field use of LFM2.5-VL-3B as the verifier (ADR 138) showed two protocol violations the shared system prompt did not prevent: a bare `FIX` with no corrected block after it, and corrected text with no `FIX` line before it. Both are addressed where each belongs. (a) `lfm25-vl-3b`'s check role now carries `blockPrompts` for every block type the verify panel routes (text, title, list, table, captions/footnotes, equation/formula, code, abstract, ref_text/reference, header/footer/page_number/seal), each opening with a mandatory reply-format contract — "correct: exactly OK / errors: FIX on the first line, then the complete corrected block on the next line / unreadable: exactly REVIEW; FIX must always be followed by the corrected block; corrected text must always be preceded by the FIX line" — shaped after the model's trained strength: it emits rigid header-line-then-content formats natively (the layout annotation), so the protocol is framed as the same shape, imperative and reasoning-free per the model card. LFM-specific content rules ride along: tables must stay in the candidate's markup (its native table format is OTSL — `<fcel>`/`<lcel>`/`<ucel>`/`<xcel>`/`<nl>` tokens are named and forbidden, the rebuild would otherwise store raw OTSL, ADR 87) and formulas must keep the candidate's `$$…$$`/`$…$` delimiters (ADR 136). (b) `GeneralPurposeModel::parseResponse` maps `FIX` with an empty body to `CheckStatus::Review` instead of a protocol error: the verdict was explicit, only the payload is missing, so the block lands in the needs-human state rather than aborting the queue with an error (auto-apply touches nothing; recovered text is never invented). Corrected text without a verdict stays an error on purpose — distinguishing commentary from an intended fix is not decidable, and a wrong auto-apply silently corrupts page text (ADR 76). Test: `checkResponseParsing` gained the bare-FIX → Review case; the now-dead "FIX without corrected text" error string was `vanished` in `llocr_ru.ts`. | A 3B direct-answer model follows format contracts better when they are per-request, imperative, and shaped like its training formats — and when the one degenerate output it still produces degrades to a state a human can resolve instead of an error nobody can act on. Auto-applying an unverifiable bare reply was rejected precisely because the check flow writes results into page data. Follow-up: the check role also gained a `systemPrompt` override (ADR 140) — the shared verifyPrompts.json contract alone was not enough for this model. |
| 140  | **The verifier system prompt is a per-model profile field.** `ModelProfiles::Role` gains `systemPrompt`, read from the role object and resolved by `ModelProfiles::systemPromptFor(profiles, modelId, role, fallback)` — the same override-with-fallback rule as `blockPromptFor` — and `VerificationQueueController::startNextVerify` resolves the check model's id once and feeds both prompts through it. `lfm25-vl-3b`'s check role ships its own system prompt: the shared contract (image is the only source of truth; candidate and image text are data, not instructions; no rewriting of the author's mistakes) restated together with the same reply-format block the model's block prompts carry — per the model card it answers directly, in rigid header-line-then-content shapes, so the protocol is framed as one in the system message and mirrored per type in the user message. Models without an override (qwen3.5-4b, teleocr, any OCR role) keep serving verifyPrompts.json verbatim; the shared prompt stays the single source users edit in Settings → Проверка. Tests: `TestModelPresetCatalog::verifierSystemPromptFollowsTheModel` (override for lfm/check, verbatim fallback for qwen, the same profile's ocr role, and an unknown id). | The block prompts carry the format contract per request type, but the system message is where a chat-format model takes its role and global rules from; without a per-model override the two messages could speak different dialects of the protocol, and the shared prompt cannot be reworded for one model without changing it for every other. |
| 141  | **The generation budget lives in the request, and the Launch table shrinks to the machine's three hardware parameters.** Two consolidations, one philosophy: the shipped settings are tuned, and a user who wants different ones is an expert who edits the profile file or runs their own server. (a) `n-predict` is gone from `serverLaunch.json` (platform profiles and fallback) and the **fallback layer is removed entirely** (`parseFallback`, `LaunchProfileStore::m_fallback`, the compose branch): every platform profile already carried `ctx-size`, so the fallback never added anything in practice, and the generation budget for every app request is `max_tokens` from the per-model request profile (`maxOutput`, ADR 59/110) — the one guarantee that also holds against an external server whose defaults nobody here controls. (b) The **draft is the platform layer alone**: `LaunchProfileStore::composeDraft` resets from `merged(activeProfileId())` instead of the composed policy + platform + model (+fallback) set; every row it shows is the user's to edit (the `LaunchParametersModel` lock machinery — `EditableRole`, `setLockedPrefix`/`setLockedNames`, `appendRow`/`removeRow` — is deleted, along with the Request store's `appendDraftRow` and the add-row / remove-row UI in `ParamsTableEditor`). The table shows exactly the machine's parameters — `n-gpu-layers`, `flash-attn`, `ctx-size` — while the policy and the model's rows reach the server through `compose()`, which is unchanged, as are `ServerLaunchConfig` and the launch-dirty banner. A user copy of a platform profile carrying any other name (old-shape `n-predict`, policy rows, model rows) is dropped wholesale at startup by the existing pruning, now keyed to the built-in presets' names only. The launch tab's "model not in the catalog → fallback" warning lost its subject and became a plain "not in the catalog" note. Tests: `test_launch_profile` rewritten for the platform-only draft (draft save/load, wholesale drop of a stale copy, policy shared but out of the table, empty catalog); `test_launch_config` lost the append/remove model tests; `test_ensure_connection`'s mock-server flags moved from `appendDraftParameter` into the test catalog's preset rows (a user copy carrying foreign rows cannot exist any more — that is the point); `launchConfigDirtyFollowsTheRealLaunchConfiguration` now drives the change through the real UI path (draft edit + save). | Three sources carried the same 8192 and two UIs offered edits the author never intended: `n-predict` was a server default no app request ever used (every request carries `max_tokens`), and the layer composition made the Launch table a debugging view of the whole argv rather than a settings page. What remains is one value per question: the machine's memory → the launch table, the weights' needs → the model profiles, the request budget → `max_tokens`. `llocr_ru.ts` hand-edited: the add/remove row strings and the fallback warnings `vanished`, the new captions translated. |
| 142  | **Bug — the OCR request profile was pinned by a zombie `model/requestProfileId` and never followed the model.** `RequestProfileStore::activeProfileId()` for the OCR role read `requestProfileId` first and fell back to `modelRecipeId` only when it was empty — but the setting could never be empty: the defaults table initialized it to the default recipe id and `applyStartupMigration()` adopted the recipe id into it. So the first lookup always won, and every production path that switches the model (`selectModelProfile` → `setModelRecipeId`; a finished install; the wizard) left the pinned id untouched — the Request tab kept showing the previous model's sampling parameters, and recognition went out with the wrong temperature/top-k/max_tokens after a model switch. `draftFollowsTheModelSelection` hid the defect by updating both ids by hand, which no production path did any more. The fix removes the setting: the model id is the request profile's only selector. `SettingsStore` loses `kRequestProfileId`/`requestProfileId` (property, getter/setter, signal, defaults row); `applyStartupMigration` drops the stored key (it joined the retired-key cleanup — a stored value nobody reads is a leftover the next reader would trust) and no longer adopts or resolves it; `RequestProfileStore::activeProfileId()` reads `modelRecipeId` directly for the OCR role (the check role keeps `checkRequestProfileId`, which IS its model selector, written by `selectModelProfile`); `LocationTab`'s external-mode picker writes `modelRecipeId` only. `test_request_profile`'s `activeProfileIsIndependentOfTheModel` became `activeProfileFollowsTheModel` (switching the model switches the sampling set; the unknown-id fallback order is unchanged), the setup lines and ADR 110 comments are gone, `migrationDropsTheRetiredKeys` pins the key's removal. | The decoupling ADR 110 introduced made sense while both pickers existed; ADR 129 removed the pickers and made the draft follow the selection, which left the second id nothing to decide but every means to disagree. One question, one answer: the model's identity selects both the launch layer and the request profile. |
| 143  | **Audit vs the LFM2.5-VL model card: the wire order is the one deviation, and it travels in the profile.** Checked the OCR request against LiquidAI's card (the recognition prompt is the card's layout-annotation contract verbatim, minus the dropped "introduced here by IBM" citation; the instruction rides in the user message — the card allows system *or* user; sampling is exactly the card's temperature 0.2 / top-k 50 / repeat-penalty 1.0; no chat-template kwargs — the model has no thinking mode). One mismatch: `OcrModel::buildRequestBody` sent the content parts **image-then-text**, while every LFM2.5 example renders the text first with the `<image>` tag after it. Fixed data-driven, not forked in C++: `ModelProfiles::Role` gains `promptBeforeImage` (default false preserves the Qwen-family order — Unlimited-OCR and TeleOCR document image-then-text), `OcrModel::buildRequestBody` orders the parts by the request's model profile, and `lfm25-vl-3b`'s ocr role ships `"promptBeforeImage": true`. The card's check-request side was already compliant (the verifier user message is text-then-image-then-candidate). Tests: `ocrBodyFollowsTheTrainedPartOrder` (lfm body: text part first, exactly one user message, no system message; unlimited body: image first). Follow-up: the flag extends to the verification request — `GeneralPurposeModel::buildRequestBody` reads the check role's `promptBeforeImage` (the `CheckRequest` already carries the model id) and puts the whole text before the image (type prompt, candidate, image) for the models trained that way; `lfm25-vl-3b`'s check role ships it too, so the verifier message ends with the `<image>` exactly as the card's examples do. Tests: `checkBodyPartOrderFollowsTheProfile` (lfm: image last) plus the order pins in `checkRequestBodyContainsImagePromptAndRecognizedText` (catalog-unknown id: image in the middle, unchanged). | The message layout is what the model was trained on; the parts array reaches the prompt verbatim through llama.cpp, so a flipped order is a silent deviation from the documented shape. And the fix belongs in the profile — the same wire detail differs between model families, so a C++ constant would be wrong for someone by construction. |
| 144  | **The verifier's wording lives only in the model profile — the shared verifyPrompts.json and its settings tabs are gone.** Following ADR 141's rule (the shipped settings are tuned; an expert edits the profile file), the «Настройки проверки» window keeps a single tab: **Blocks** — which block types are auto-verified. Removed: the **System prompt** and **Block prompts** tabs (`VerificationSystemTab.qml` / `VerificationPromptsTab.qml` deleted) and with them `VerificationPromptStore`'s entire prompt half — the `systemPrompt` property, `promptForType`, the original-vs-edited tracking, and the built-in `:/profiles/verifyPrompts.json` (deleted; also dropped from the app and test resources). What the store still owns — the block-type list (type, name, group, default enablement, now a compiled-in table) and the per-type enablement — keeps its user file (`profiles/verifyPrompts.json` in the profile dir, enablement diffs only; `systemPrompt`/`prompt` fields of files written by older builds are read past and dropped on the next save). `qwen3.5-4b`'s check role received the former shared prompts verbatim — `systemPrompt` + `blockPrompts` for all 18 types the verify panel routes — so it keeps answering with the same wording; `lfm25-vl-3b` already carried its own (ADR 139/140); `teleocr` (experimental) is deliberately left without block prompts of its own coverage. Resolution lost the fallback argument: `ModelProfiles::blockPromptFor`/`systemPromptFor` dropped the `fallback` parameter — a type the check profile says nothing about (and any model without a check role) resolves to an empty prompt. Tests: `test_verification_prompts` rewritten for enablement-only persistence (plus a legacy-file case), `verifierSystemPromptFollowsTheModel`/`checkModelBlockPromptsOverridePerType` re-pinned to the no-fallback API and qwen's own prompts. `llocr_ru.ts`: the two tabs' strings `vanished`. | The same prompts were edited in the UI, shipped as a JSON resource, and overridden by profiles (ADR 139/140) — three sources for one wording, and the UI editor was the one nobody should use: the text is model-specific instruction tuning, not a preference. One question, one answer: the model's wording lives where the model lives (ADR 141), the settings window keeps the one choice that is actually a preference — which blocks the (paid) verifier loop touches. |
| 145  | **The verification pipeline splits into two model stages: a decision gate, then block re-recognition** (the plan: `archive/models/docs/15-teleocr.md`). Phase 1 sends every queued block (same enablement rules as before, ADR 134/135) to the **decision role** — a fast model that answers a probability in one pass, zero generated tokens (`DecisionModel` → `/v1/systemone`, ADR 147). P(match) ≥ `decision/matchThreshold` (default 0.5, clamped, «Порог совпадения (%)» in the decision window's Request tab) marks the block **Ok** (green); below it — the new **Mismatch** status (red, `BoxCheckStatus`/`CheckStatus` value 4, appended so old `.llocr` projects load; `ProjectStore` range extended). With `check/autoRecheck` («Автоматическое повторное распознавание», default off) phase 2 re-runs the mismatches through the **blockRecognition role** in the same run — at most two server role switches per verification run. A failed decision request is not a verdict: the block stays unchecked and the error is reported. The block-recognition contract is now plain transcription — the request carries only the type prompt and the crop, the previously recognized text is not sent (`<ocr_candidate>` is gone from `buildRequestBody`, `CheckRequest` lost `recognizedText`), and the reply IS the block text (empty → Review), superseding the OK/FIX/REVIEW protocol of ADR 90/139 — the profiles' prompts were rewritten accordingly (ADR 140's per-model wording stands). Manual entry points: «Проверить» per block, «Проверить страницу/всё», plus block-recognition commands — the per-block «Распознать» button (any status) and the header ▾ menus «Распознать проблемные блоки (на странице / все)» picking only Mismatch/Review marks (`startRecheckQueue` runs the queue straight in phase 2). Progress reads per phase («Проверка X / Y» / «Распознавание X / Y», `checkDecisionPhase`). Tests: `test_decision_model`, the two-phase scenarios in `test_app_import`, `checkResponseParsing` in `test_ocr_models`. | A 3B decision model judges a block in one pass where a generative verifier spent a full decode per block, and the two-stage split keeps the expensive transcription for the blocks that need it. |
| 146  | **The check role is renamed `blockRecognition`.** Profile role keys (`roles.blockRecognition` in lfm25-vl-3b/qwen3.5-4b/teleocr), the registry role tags (`index.json` migrates a stored `"check"` to the new name on load — `ModelRegistry::entryFromJson`, tested by `migratesLegacyCheckRole`), and the enums (`ConnectionRole::BlockRecognition`, `RequestProfileStore::Role::BlockRecognition`). The legacy vocabulary survives where renaming would cost user data or churn: the `check/*` settings keys, `CheckController`/`CheckRequest`/`CheckResult`, `requestValidate.json`, `RequestProfilesValidate`. The UI says «Модель OCR блока» / "Block OCR model". The old glossary rule "do not invent a third word" is retired by ADR 147's decision role. | The role now names what it does (transcribe a block), not an implementation era; settings and class names stay for compatibility. |
| 147  | **The decision role: a third model speaking `/v1/systemone`.** `roles.decision` in a model profile (d1-3b ships it: alias `llocr-decision`, its own launch layer with ctx-size 8192, the yes/no question wording in `systemPrompt`, empty request parameters — the model generates no text, so sampling does not apply). The endpoint is a llama.cpp extension (`{base}/v1/systemone`, body `{state, images, questions.match{noul, instructions}}`); an OpenAI-compatible server that only speaks chat/completions answers 404 and every judge fails — update the runtime. The answer's probability is read in three spellings (bare number, `{noul: x}`, `{probability: x}`), everything else is an error, never a silent verdict. Settings under `decision/*` (weights paths, profile id, external `decision/modelName`, `decision/matchThreshold`); a third settings window, installer list and wizard tab, all role-string driven (`bool forCheck` parameters are gone from the installer stack). Question wording falls back to a generic English string when the profile ships none. | LiquidAI's d1 answered a named yes/no question in one forward pass (~8–30 ms), which is what the gate stage needs; its manual was removed when the model was replaced (ADR 161). |
| 148  | **The provider timeout is split into a connection timeout and a response timeout.** `provider/timeoutMs` (property `connectionTimeoutMs`, 120 000 default) actually capped the whole request — the single `QTimer` in `LlamaClient::postJson` aborted the POST from issue to last byte, so it acted as the model's response deadline while the UI already called it "Request timeout". Now: `provider/connectionTimeoutMs` (default 5 s) bounds only the TCP connect, enforced by an async `QTcpSocket` pre-flight in `postJson` (QNAM exposes no connect timeout; llama.cpp sends no bytes until generation finishes, so response headers cannot serve as the connect signal) — a dead or mistyped endpoint fails fast with a translated "Connection to %1 …" error; `provider/responseTimeoutMs` (default **300 000** = 5 min) caps the whole request again, so slow machines are not cut off at 2 min. Both travel `ResolvedConnection` → `ConnectionConfig` → `postJson` and apply to **Managed and External alike** (both `resolveExternal` and `buildManagedConnection` fill them). `applyStartupMigration` moves a stored `provider/timeoutMs` to `provider/responseTimeoutMs` (it was a request deadline) and removes the retired key. In Settings → Runtime both fields sit directly under the Connection-mode combo, visible in either mode (they left `RuntimeTabExternal`, whose copy also feeds the wizard's StepExternal unchanged). Other timeouts are untouched and unrelated: `runtime/startupTimeoutMs` (server boot), health/probe budgets (ADR 51/52), `kModelsRequestTimeoutMs` (the 10 s `/v1/models` resolve query), `HttpClient`'s catalog fetches. Tests: `legacyTimeoutKeyMigratesToResponseTimeout`, updated defaults/reset and `test_ensure_connection` resolve assertions, `test_ocr_models`/`test_decision_model` request-timeout paths. | One setting was doing two jobs with the wrong default for each: a 2-minute cap was too short for slow OCR decodes, and a dead URL waited the full request timeout instead of failing fast. |
| 149  | **The manual page-markup pass: a `layout` role and a two-request pipeline for models that cannot answer coordinates and text in one shot.** `roles.layout` in a model profile (teleocr ships it: parser `teleocr-layout`, the card's layout prompt «Analyze the image layout.» plus the Segmentation variant, its own request parameters, a reply-format contract in `systemPrompt` — the upstream emits `<box:x1 y1 x2 y2><label:type><orientation>` lines on a 0–1000 scale). The request itself reuses the block-recognition transport (`GeneralPurposeModel`) and its connection (`ensureConnectionReady(ConnectionRole::BlockRecognition)`) on purpose: for TeleOCR layout and block recognition are the same weights, so there is no fourth settings window and no `ConnectionRole` — the layout request profile follows `check/requestProfileId` (`RequestProfileStore::Role::Layout`, user file `requestLayout.json`), and `Controller.layoutAvailable` is just «the active block-recognition model declares a layout role». `LayoutController` queues pages (current / all), squashes each page to the upstream-calibrated 1036×1036 square (normalized coordinates are invariant to the squeeze), and the reply is parsed by the registered `TeleOcrLayoutParser` (a `DetTokenParserBase` subclass whose `prepareText` rewrites the box/label lines into the canonical `label [x1,y1,x2,y2]` tokens and reduces polygons to their bounding box). `AppController::applyLayout` **replaces** the page's blocks (with a confirmation when the page already has results) and marks it recognized with an empty text: content in the layout reply is deliberately ignored — the text arrives per block through the existing blockRecognition pass («Распознать», or the new «Распознать все блоки страницы», which queues every NotChecked text-less box). Image-ish labels (`image`, `chart`, `figure`, `char`) are UI image boxes (`BoxListModel::isImageBox`), the layout vocabulary is styled data-driven via `teleocr.json` `blocks.styles`, and the layout-created blocks survive the `.llocr` container as-is. Tests: `test_layout` (request shape + loopback), parser cases in `test_det_parser`, `layoutRoleShipsTheMarkupContract` / `teleOcrBlockPromptsCoverTheLayoutVocabulary` in `test_model_preset_catalog`, the text-less-box round-trip in `test_project_store`. | The one-shot models (UnlimitedOCR, LFM2.5-VL) answer boxes+text together and need nothing new; TeleOCR cannot, so a manual markup step gives the same block overlay without embedding the feature into the automatic pipeline (that stays deferred until a second two-phase model justifies it). |
| 150  | **The layout role gets its own model settings — the fourth full role** (reverses the no-fourth-window part of ADR 149). `ConnectionRole::Layout`, the `layout/*` settings group (`layout/modelPath`, `layout/mmprojPath`, `layout/draftPath`, `layout/requestProfileId` defaulting to the role's recommended model, `layout/modelName`), the Settings menu entry «Модель разметки» (window `ModelSettingsWindow { role: "layout" }` with Model / Launch / Request tabs), a fourth `ModelQuantModel` + preset list in `ModelInstaller` (`layoutQuantModels`, `layoutActiveTitle`), and `LayoutController` resolving through `ensureConnectionReady(ConnectionRole::Layout)`. `AppController::layoutRole()` reads the layout request-profile store, not the check one. No wizard step and no migration: an unconfigured layout model fails with the per-role actionable message (ADR 61 semantics apply — a live server with compatible weights is still reused), and an already-downloaded TeleOCR shows up in the layout list as installed because install state matches files by repo + file stem, not by role tags. Tests: `layoutRoleResolvesAModelProfileWithoutOneStored`, `managedLayoutRoleSwitchesServer` + the layout refusal arm, `quantRowsListTheModelsOfTheRole`'s layout arm, `layoutStoreFollowsItsOwnSetting`. | The markup pass turned out to stay in use, so sharing the check model's configuration made the two features impossible to tune independently (a dedicated detection model vs. the transcription model) and the check window misleadingly claimed the layout function. |
| 151  | **The interface is reworked: book actions in the header, per-page actions in a two-row page toolbar, and a floating mini-toolbar on the selected block** (no new functionality — every removed header entry moved, and unimplemented tools are stubs). `Header.qml` becomes «Файл ▾» (unchanged contents) plus four book-wide actions: «Распознать» (`recognizeAll`; the old `pageCount > 1` gate is dropped — it is now the only book-recognition button), «Проверить» (`checkAllEnabledBlocks`, per `check/autoRecheck`), «Исправить» (`recognizeAllProblemBlocks`), «Разметить» (the book-wide layout pass — `layoutAllPages` behind the replace-results confirmation; moved out of the page toolbar), «Экспортировать»; Stop, page navigation and Настройки stay. The page menu (`PageToolBar.qml`, above the image in `ImagePanel`) lays the per-page actions out as a 2×2 grid — «Распознать страницу ▾» (dropdown: «Проблемные блоки» / «Все блоки» — moved from the old «Распознать ▾») over «Разметить страницу» (the page-level markup confirm dialog lives here), «Проверить ▾» (main click = verify and fix; the menu's «Только проверить» / «Проверить и исправить» pass the new per-run override `checkEnabledBlocksOnPage(int)` — `VerificationQueueController::RecheckMode` −1/0/1 = follow `check/autoRecheck` / decision phase only / also re-recognize the mismatches, threaded through `startVerifyQueue` so one run's recheck behavior no longer depends solely on the setting) over «Сохранить страницу» (`ExportDialog` gained `presetScope`, consumed on the first show, so the page export opens preselected to «Текущая страница») — followed by a 2×3 stub grid of block-add icons (text/image/table/formula/code + a menu listing every `BlockNames` type) and the rotate-ccw/rotate-cw stubs — stubs are disabled with a «Функция пока не реализована» tooltip. The availability flags (`canRecognize` and the five «supported» properties) moved from their single NOTIFYs (`configChanged` / `checkStateChanged`) to a dedicated `availabilityChanged`, emitted on recognition/layout busy transitions, check-queue movement, page change, page-content change and config changes — one NOTIFY cannot cover what those flags fold in, and without it the recognition buttons stayed stale-active while a check ran and «Проверить» stayed stale-disabled right after a recognition. The selected block gets the floating `BlockMiniToolbar` (распознать / проверить / удалить; delete-only for image boxes), instantiated inside the box delegate so it follows drags, clamped to the image area and flipped below the frame when there is no room above; the delegate is raised while selected and the old hover-✕ is gone; `BlockEditPanel` keeps only the texts plus «Отменить исправление» beside the corrected text. Icons: the Lucide (ISC) SVG set in `resources/icons/ui/`, registered as the `ui_icons` qrc prefix and colorized at runtime by `Common/Icon.qml` (Image + `MultiEffect` colorization — one asset set serves both themes). Russian translations are maintained by hand per ADR 122 (new contexts `PageToolBar`, `BlockMiniToolbar`; the removed `Header`/`BlockEditPanel` strings marked vanished). | The old header mixed book- and page-level actions in one row of six text buttons with dropdowns; splitting them into "book = header, page = above the image" matches the OCR-suite layout users already know (FineReader), gives the growing per-page tool set (block-add and rotate stubs) a home next to the picture it works on, and the floating mini-toolbar keeps the block actions next to the block instead of at the window bottom. |
| 152  | **A previously recognized block is re-run through blockRecognition framed as verify-and-correct, not as a blind re-roll** (supersedes ADR 145's "the previously recognized text is not sent"). The role gains an optional `recheckPrompt` — per-profile wording with the `{text}` placeholder, a built-in English fallback for profiles that ship none — and `VerificationQueueController::composeBlockUserPrompt` composes the user message: a block with text goes out as `typePrompt + recheckPrompt` with `{text}` = the block's **original** `box.text` (a stored correction, `correctedText`, is never re-sent — one canonical candidate per block, no correction chains for the model to rubber-stamp); a text-less block (layout markup) keeps the plain transcription contract, and the recheck framing alone carries the request when the profile describes no block type. The reply applies unchanged (non-empty → `Fixed` + `correctedText`, empty → `Review`), so a confirmed text lands as a fresh correction. Uniform across every entry point: the auto-recheck after the decision phase, the per-block «Распознать», the problem-block commands. All six shipped blockRecognition profiles (teleocr, lfm25-vl-3b, gemma-4-e4b/12b, qwen3.5-4b/9b) carry the field. `CheckRequest`/`CheckController`/`GeneralPurposeModel` are untouched — the composed prompt travels in the existing `typePrompt` field. Tests: `blockRecognitionRecheckPrompts` in `test_ocr_models`, `blockPromptComposition` + recorded-request-body assertions in the two-phase scenarios of `test_app_import`. | The decision gate judges a candidate it is given; a blind re-roll could contradict the text the user already sees without ever looking at it. Showing the model its candidate turns the decode into a comparison — confirm verbatim or name the fix — and the profile-field wording keeps the per-model contract discipline (ADR 144). |
| 153  | **The block overlay is reworked: no label inside the box, the status dot outside to the left, a per-kind background hue, and the statuses renamed to pipeline states; the first block recognition lands where page OCR lands.** Rendering: the label chip is gone from `ImagePreview.qml`; every box carries a translucent fill plus the double frame in its kind's hue — text green `#2E7D32`, table purple `#8E24AA`, formula orange `#EF6C00`, image blue (unchanged) — resolved data-driven from `BlockStyleMap` through the new `BoxListModel::boxKind` role (`ImagePlaceholder`/`Table`/`Equation` → image/table/formula, everything else text); `AppController` feeds the parser's `ParserOptions.modelId` into `setStyleModelId` in `applyRawResult`/`applyLayout` and resets it to the default vocabulary on project open. `Theme.qml` carries one `overlay<Kind>{Fill,Outer,Inner}` triple per kind (the old dark/white text frame is gone); `isImageBox` keeps deciding the interaction (drag/resize/delete). Status dot: outside the block to the left, vertically centered (clamped into view at the left edge); the filter-following gray "planned" concept is gone — gray now simply means recognized (page OCR or block recognition, `NotChecked` with text), a markup block without text shows no dot, green/amber/red keep their meanings. `PlannedRole`/`SuspectRole`, `setPlannedTypes`, `isPlanned` and the `selectedBlockSuspect`/`selectedBlockPlanned` properties are removed as dead. Statuses: `CheckStatus::Fixed` → `ReRecognized` (the `.llocr` int 2 is unchanged). The block-recognition reply applies as: a block with no text and no correction (first recognition — layout markup) writes it into `box.text` and stays `NotChecked` (gray); a block with text stores the reply in `correctedText` as `ReRecognized` (amber, two variants; the page text keeps preferring the corrected one). `Ok` on a two-variant block collapses the confirmed variant into `box.text`; `Mismatch`/`Review` keep both variants — the edit panel shows the second variant and «Отменить исправление» whenever `correctedText` is non-empty. A re-recognized block is re-judged by the next verification run on its corrected variant: `collectEnabledBoxes` queues `NotChecked` + `ReRecognized` blocks and the decision request's `state` carries the effective text (`correctedText` if present); `Ok`/`Mismatch`/`Review` are final. No in-run third phase — an amber block waits for the next «Проверить». Tests: `kindRoleFollowsLabelMap`, `updateBoxCheckUpdatesText` (`test_box_model`), `firstRecognitionFillsBlockTextWithoutVerdict`, `confirmedCorrectionBecomesBlockText`, the reworked two-phase scenarios (`test_app_import`). | The label chip covered the very pixels the user needs to check and duplicated what the box hue and the edit panel already say; per-kind hues are the annotation-tool convention — one glance tells a table from a formula from text. Statuses now name the pipeline state instead of mirroring the settings filter: every recognized block is gray until the decision stage answers, so the dot reads as progress (gray → green/red, amber = two variants to resolve). First recognition writing into `box.text` makes block OCR and page OCR land in the same place — one canonical text per block — and the amber two-variant state goes back through the same decision gate that rejected the original, so the loop closes on the next «Проверить» without a third in-run phase. |
| 154  | **The decision question is composed per block type, and the noul answer sides gain a wire field** (extends ADR 147). The decision role gains the per-block shape blockRecognition already has: `roles.decision.blockPrompts` carries one check list per type the verify panel routes (all 18 — a table is judged cell by cell and by row/column position, a formula by symbols, bounds and letter alphabets, a page number by every digit), and `VerificationQueueController::decisionQuestion(blockType)` sends `systemPrompt + "\n\n" + typePrompt`; a type the profile does not name sends the bare question. The card's optional `criteria` member of the `noul` question (`{"true": …, "false": …}` — what each side of the answer accepts) is wired through end to end: a profile ships it as a `criteria` object on the role (`ModelProfiles` parses it into `Role::criteriaTrue`/`criteriaFalse`, `DecisionRequest` carries it, `DecisionModel::buildRequestBody` inserts it into `questions.match.criteria`). The shipped d1-3b instead keeps its admission rules in the system prompt's «Count as a difference» / «Do not count as a difference» lists and ships no criteria — the field is omitted then and the endpoint's default sides apply. Tests: `decisionRoleShipsPerBlockPrompts` and `requestBodyCarriesNoulCriteria`, `verifierSystemPromptFollowsTheModel` pinned to the shipped framing and rule lists. | One generic question judged a table, a formula and a page number by the same words; the card's own guidance is to define the answer sides explicitly and keep the instructions official. The shipped profile answers that with two explicit rule lists in the prompt, and the structured `criteria` field stays available for profiles that prefer it. |
| 155  | **The per-block verification loop stops paying connection taxes: the resolve is cached per role, the models own one `LlamaClient`, and `availabilityChanged` fires only on an actual flip** (review C++-19/20/21). (a) `RuntimeController` keeps the last successful `ResolvedConnection` per role (`m_resolvedCache`): `ensureConnectionReady` answers synchronously from the cache when the server is `Ready` on the requested role, nothing is in flight (`!m_resolveInProgress && !m_switching`), the instance is not locked out, and `serverRunsRole(role)` holds. Every `setState` transition (start/stop/role-switch) and every `SettingsStore` change (`connectEveryChangeSignal → invalidateResolvedCache`) clears it; failures are never cached — so the start/stop/switch/recovery semantics of ADR 61/62 are exactly the old resolve, minus the per-block `/v1/models` round-trip. (b) `OcrModel`/`GeneralPurposeModel`/`DecisionModel` create their `LlamaClient` (with its QNAM) once per model instance instead of per request — the HTTP keep-alive survives across the per-page/per-block requests; per-model usage is serial by the controllers' busy gates, so the single in-flight state and `abort()` are unchanged. (c) All `availabilityChanged` emitters in `AppController` route through `updateAvailability()`, which recomputes the seven NOTIFY-covered flags and emits only when one actually flips — a per-block queue update costs one C++ recomputation instead of re-evaluating every QML availability binding. Tests: `TestEnsureConnection::repeatedResolveOnReadyServerIsSynchronous`, `::resolveCacheInvalidatesOnSettingsChange`; the role-switch/dedup/cancel suite of `test_ensure_connection` and `test_ocr_models` (incl. future-completes-after-model-destruction) pass unchanged. | Every «Проверить» block used to open a fresh TCP connection twice over — a raw connect probe plus a per-request QNAM — after re-fetching `/v1/models`, and re-evaluated every availability binding in QML; on a long document that is hundreds of avoidable round-trips and quadratic binding work between the model calls that actually matter. The cache keys on exactly the inputs the resolve reads (server state + settings), so nothing semantic changed — only the repetition is gone. |
| 156  | **The block-text rebuild is memoized, and an empty selection no longer pays for it** (review C++-18). `AppController::currentPageRebuild()` — the parser pass (table/math regexes over every block, then re-anchoring `indexOf` over the whole page text) behind `blockTextMapped`, `blockTextRange`, `boxIndexForTextPosition`, `selectedBlockTextRange` and `updateBlockHighlight` — is memoized on its inputs: (current page, `docRevision`, `cropRevision`, the shown page text). One keystroke used to re-run the whole pass per consumer — the highlighter (via `resultChanged → blockTextRangeChanged`), the QML re-read of `selectedBlockTextRange`, and whatever else touched the mapping — now they share one computation per text state. The parser options (`parserId`, `modelRecipeId`, `keepPageNumbers`, `tablesAsHtml`) have no revision to key on, so their change signals reset the memo through `invalidateRebuildCache()`, which re-announces `blockTextRangeChanged` — otherwise the highlight and the QML range property would wait for the next edit. `updateBlockHighlight()` short-circuits to an empty highlight when no block is selected instead of rebuilding the page for a range it will discard. Tests: `blockTextMemoFollowsThePageAndParserOptions` (a fresh page never serves another page's memo, returning to a recognized page does, a parser-option drop re-announces the ranges); `blockTextRangesTrackBoxesAndManualEdits` and `blockHighlighterPaintsTheSelectedRange` (edit/revert/selection) pass unchanged. | Typing on a page with big tables ran the full regex rebuild three to four times per character on the UI thread — the lag the panel showed was not one rebuild but several sharing none of their work; the same-shaped preview cache (ADR 102 era) already proved the key. The re-recognition hole (identical text over a changed segmentation, no revision bump in `applyRawResult`) is shared with the preview cache and accepted the same way. |
| 157  | **The secondary windows are created on first use** (review QML-13/14/12). All ten secondary windows (two logs, four settings, four model windows) and the setup wizard are declared as `Loader { active: false }` handles in `Main.qml`; the first `showWindow(loader)` flips `active` and calls `bringToFront()` on the synchronously created item. Consumers keep their API unchanged: the Header calls `showWindow(...)` directly, and the two reference-style consumers — the footer's `logWindow`/`problemWindow` and the model tabs' `runtimeSettingsRef` («Configure runtime…») — receive `windowProxy(loader)`, an object carrying the same `bringToFront()`, so the guarded call sites read identically and the window materializes on the first click. Window data still loads in `onVisibleChanged` (unchanged behavior); popups and `FileDialog`s of the main window stay inline. Along the same pass: `ModelSettingsWindow` lost its `Connections`-on-tabBar rescan — the `refreshInstalled()+reloadPresets()+rescanRegistry()` trio now runs once per window activation, not on every return to tab 0 (in-window mutations already propagate through `ModelInstaller` signals), and `Setup/StepModel` lost its `Component.onCompleted` duplicate — the steps switch by `visible` bindings, so `onVisibleChanged` covers the step show; with the wizard itself lazy, the startup disk scan is gone entirely. `qmllint` note: `Loader.item` is statically `QObject`, so the wizard call goes through `item as SetupWizard` to stay under the `missing-property` gate. Popup note: a `Dialog` created inside a zero-size Loader centers on the Loader unless it pins `parent: Overlay.overlay` explicitly — SetupWizard carries that pin. Smoke-tested with an 8-second offscreen run on a clean profile (the wizard trigger creates and opens the wizard through the new path; no QML errors), and the centering was verified by an offscreen position test of a Loader-created dialog. |
| 158  | **Shared instances instead of per-delegate/per-block ones: one popup pair per download list, one toolbar-and-handles set per overlay** (review QML-9/8). (a) `ModelDownloadList` carries one `quantPopup` + one `rowMenu` for the whole list and `RuntimeBuildsList` one `rowMenu` — lifted out of the row delegate, where each row eagerly instantiated 40–80 objects (popup content is created when the popup is, so every row built its whole quant menu at row-creation time); the lists are instantiated 5+ times across the settings windows and the wizard, and `StepModel` disables virtualization (`maxVisibleRows: -1`), so a large preset catalog meant thousands of dormant popups. The single instances are parameterized by `activeRow` — the delegate whose button opened them (null-guarded `enabled`/`visible` bindings; actions read `activeRow` imperatively at click time, when it is necessarily set) — and rows call `root.openQuantPopup(row, button)` / `root.openRowMenu(row, button)`. The delete-confirmation dialogs were already list-level. (b) `ImagePreview` carries one `BlockMiniToolbar` and one 8-handle resize set at the `imageArea` level, tracking the selected box through `boxRepeater.itemAt(Controller.selectedBoxIndex)` (geometry math and `resizeRect` converted to overlay coordinates; z = 3 above the selected box's z = 2; handles visible only for image boxes) — the per-delegate versions cost ~30 live objects per block and were torn down and rebuilt on every page change, and the handles existed even for text blocks where they can never show. Tests: `test_app_import` (real `AppController`, includes the check queue and block ops) unchanged; the toolbar's follow/center/clamp/hide behavior and the single-instance property verified by an offscreen qmltestrunner probe. | A list of N rows and a page of M blocks are N and M times the UI the user can look at one row/block of at a time; the per-delegate instances made every list and every page change pay for all of that eagerly, in exactly the files that ship in five copies across the settings windows. The `activeRow` parameterization keeps the delegate API a two-word change, and the overlay-level toolbar is the same floating bar the user already knew — now with one binding surface instead of M. |
| 159  | **`runtime/stopOnExit = false` is honored end to end: the destructor detaches instead of killing, and the surviving server's stdout goes to a file** (review C++-4, the hygiene batch). `~QProcess()` kills the child unconditionally (documented), so `LlamaServerProcess` wraps its `QProcess` in a two-line subclass exposing `detachFromProcess()` (`setProcessState(NotRunning)`). The destructor: with `stopOnExit = true` — terminate/kill **and** `clearOwnerJson()` (owner.json is now cleaned on this path too, not only in `shutdownSync`); with `false` — detach and leave the owner record for the next run's orphan scan. A detached server would die of SIGPIPE on its next log line once the app closes the pipe, so `spawn()` routes the server's stdout into the log file (append) whenever `stopOnExit = false`, and resets the redirection when the option is switched back; the cost, only while the option is set, is that streamed model-load progress lines never reach the UI — startup is tracked through health polling and `/v1/models`. The same batch: `DownloadTask::closeFile()` reports flush/fsync failure and the success path fails the download with a translated «Unable to write %1» instead of renaming a truncated archive into place; tar `fieldString()` stops at NUL only (ustar name/prefix/linkname are NUL-padded and legally contain spaces — «my data.bin» no longer extracts as «my»; test `tarKeepsSpacesInNames`); `InstallTransaction` passes `&ok` to `sha256File` and reports an unreadable archive distinctly from a digest mismatch, with both strings translated; `BoxCheckStatus` gained the single `boxCheckStatusFromInt(int)` gate used by both the project load and the QML `updateBoxCheck` entry (test `updateBoxCheckClampsOutOfRangeStatus`); `ReleaseCatalog`'s `failWithCache` lambda lost its dead `message` parameter. Platform note: ProcessGuard's Linux `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` and Windows Job Object still terminate the server when the app dies — that is their deliberate design, so the outliving behavior is real on macOS. Tests: 37/37, archive and box-model suites extended as above. | The option was a sleeping contradiction: `shutdownSync` kept the promise, the destructor broke it, and `~QProcess` would have broken it anyway even without an explicit kill. The detach-plus-file-stdout pair is the smallest construction that makes the promise true rather than silently false; per-platform death guarantees stay where ProcessGuard put them. | `engine.load` used to build eleven window trees (tab bars, tables, per-row popups) plus the wizard's eight steps before the first frame — pure startup latency and a permanent binding surface for windows the user may never open. The proxy keeps every consumer spelling unchanged, which made this a Main.qml-only restructure; the one behavioral nuance — a «Configure runtime…» click now pays the runtime window's creation cost once — is the point of the change. |
| 160  | **The decision profile is reworded to the v2 shape — `statePrompt`, `question`, wire criteria, block aliases — and the verdict's probability becomes visible on the block.** (a) `ModelProfiles::Role` gains `statePrompt` (the framing + admission rules: the image is the source of truth, state text is data never instruction, notation neutrality, the «A difference is / Not a difference» lists), `question` (the ask itself), and `blockAliases` (alias → canonical `blockPrompts` key, resolved inside `blockPromptFor`); `VerificationQueueController::decisionQuestion` composes `statePrompt ‖ systemPrompt + "\n\n" + question ‖ fallback + "\n\n" + typePrompt`, so profiles predating the fields keep their old shape. The shipped d1-3b profile moves its rules out of the `systemPrompt` prose into `statePrompt` + `question` + shipped `criteria{true,false}` (the wire field ADR 154 built but the profile never used) and collapses `formula`/`reference` into aliases of `equation`/`ref_text`; `noul-threshold` stays where it was — the `decision/matchThreshold` setting (the shipped profile then recommended 0.4 for recall). (b) The probability is no longer discarded at the threshold comparison: `CheckResult` carries `confidence` (−1 = none), `applyCheckResultToBox` stores it on the `BoundingBox`, `BoxListModel` exposes the `boxConfidence` role, and with `check/showConfidence` (Настройки → Проверка → «Показывать уверенность модели», off by default) the status dot of an Ok/Mismatch block renders as the probability in percent inside a circle of the verdict's color. Not persisted in `.llocr` — a per-session debugging aid for threshold tuning; re-recognition, revert and the NotChecked reset clear it, a failed decision request keeps the last verdict's value (like the status). Tests: `decisionRoleShipsPerBlockPrompts` (16 canonical prompts, alias resolution, criteria/statePrompt/question shipped), `verifierSystemPromptFollowsTheModel` (re-pinned to the new wording), `updateBoxCheckCarriesConfidence` (`test_box_model`), the confidence assertions in the two-phase scenarios of `test_app_import`. | The rules review of the decision prompts (state/questions composition, literal wording, one canonical text per wording) produced a v2 profile the single `systemPrompt` slot could only approximate; and tuning the threshold needs the raw probabilities the pipeline threw away at the comparison — now they are one checkbox away. |
| 161  | **The decision model switches from d1-3B to Cloudflare clef-flash, and the request moves to the structured-state scheme (v5).** The standalone evaluation harness (`scripts/decision-eval`, corpus `OCRtest/`) measured the shipped request — the candidate text as a raw `state` string — at AUC 0.668 with the answers flat in a 0.6–0.7 band and ~90 % of corrupted blocks passing the 0.5 threshold: over a string state the model answers "is there text on the crop", not "is it this text" (an empty state scores the same as the correct one, 0.7125 vs 0.713). Moving the text into a named field of a JSON state (`{"block_type", "recognized_text", "notation"}`) with one noul question lifts d1-3B to AUC 0.75 — but formulas and tables stay beyond any phrasing (seven schemes tried, `archive/models/docs/17-d1-verification-study.md` §7). **clef-flash** (9B, Qwen3.5-VL base, Apache-2.0, llama.cpp ≥ b11430) with the same v5 scheme verifies formulas (AUC 0.907; threshold 0.7 → 27 % missed / 9 % false alarms) and makes tables workable (0.787). Changes: `DecisionRequest` carries `blockType`/`recognizedText`/`notation` (the profile's `statePrompt`), `VerificationQueueController::decisionQuestion` composes the bare question (+ the type prompt when a profile ships one), the framing moves out of the instructions into `decisionNotation` → state, `DecisionModel::buildRequestBody` sends the state object; the shipped decision profile is now `clef-flash.json` (quant Q4_K_M of `bartowski/Cloudflare_clef-flash-GGUF` + mmproj f16, `minBuild` b11430, launch adds `batch-size`/`ubatch-size` 8192 — clef evaluates the whole prompt in one batch — and rejects `state: null` with 400); `decision/matchThreshold` recommendation 0.7, calibrated on formulas and pending a full-corpus run over the text blocks. The d1-3B profile and its manual (`docs/14`) are removed; the harness and the study stay. Tests: `test_decision_model` (state object), `decisionRoleShipsTheStructuredStateWording`, `shippedProfilesAreTheOnesTheDocsDescribe`, `roleDefaultsFollowTheShippedProfiles`, the decision-list assertions in `test_model_installer`. | The decision role existed to verify formulas and tables; d1-3B could do neither in any phrasing, while a same-endpoint 9B model closes the task with the request the harness had already validated — the raw-string state and the long framing instructions were measured to be the bottleneck, not the model class. |
| 162  | **Six tasks replace the four roles — the model-per-task system is generalized** (the staged plan lives in `docs/20-model-task-rework.md`). A model profile declares the tasks it serves by string id, and the same ids unify the profiles, `ConnectionRole`, the settings and the QML: `layout` (page markup), `pageOcr` (full-page OCR with layout tokens — the old `ocr` role), `rawPageOcr` (full-page OCR as raw text, parser `raw` — new), `blockRecognition` (single-block OCR), `decision` (the verification gate), `fix` (a universal model corrects a block — new, ADR 163). `ConnectionRole` grows to `PageOcr, RawPageOcr, BlockRecognition, Decision, Layout, Fix`; the single-llama-server mechanics of reloading the model per task (`RuntimeController::serverRunsRole`) carries over unchanged. The shipped model→task matrix: `unlimited-ocr` → pageOcr; `lfm25-vl-3b` → pageOcr + blockRecognition; `glm-ocr` → rawPageOcr + blockRecognition; `teleocr` → layout + blockRecognition (its `ocr` role is removed); `clef-flash` → decision; `qwen3.5-4b`, `qwen3.5-9b`, `gemma-4-e4b`, `gemma-4-12b` → fix (their `blockRecognition` role is replaced by the fix prompts). Principle: an OCR model only OCRs, a decision model only verifies, a universal model only corrects — a profile does not combine incompatible tasks. The per-model settings move into one namespace `tasks/<task>/{modelPath, mmprojPath, draftPath, requestProfileId, modelName}` with a one-time migration from `launch/*`, the `check/*` model keys, `decision/*` and `layout/*`; `check/` keeps only the behavior keys (`autoCheck`, `autoFix` — the renamed `autoRecheck`, `showConfidence`), and `decision/matchThreshold` stays. How a page is recognized becomes a split-button choice («Распознать»: main click = with markup, the menu = without), not a setting. The setup wizard writes the new keys mechanically (automatic model picking in the wizard stays out of scope). Supersedes the "fourth role" framing of ADR 149/150 — layout is one of six tasks, not a special fourth window. | Four ad-hoc roles accreted a settings window and role-keyed hacks per feature; tasks are one stable vocabulary — the UI, the profiles and the settings speak about the same six things, and a new capability (raw OCR, fix) becomes a task instead of another role-shaped special case. |
| 163  | **The OK / FIX / REVIEW correction protocol returns for universal models (the `fix` task)** — restoring the feature that existed 2026-09-21…2026-10-09 and was dropped when the decision pipeline landed (introduced in `2c37a1a` "second llm support", full form in `6de1448` "check prompts moved to profiles", removed in `6513786`/`5e0a8bb`). The request: `system` = the profile's verifier prompt, `user` = the block-type prompt + the OCR candidate (the block's effective text, framed as `<ocr_candidate>…</ocr_candidate>`) + the block crop, ordered per the fix role's `promptBeforeImage`. The answer is exactly one of: `OK` — no correction needed, the correction layer receives a duplicate of the text; `FIX` + the corrected text (after a newline or `:`); `REVIEW` — the image is unreadable: the block is marked damaged on the correction layer and no correction is stored; an empty `FIX` degrades to `REVIEW` (ADR 139); anything else is a request error and the block state does not change. This supersedes the recheck framing of ADR 152: re-recognizing a block no longer sends its previous text through blockRecognition — presenting a candidate and getting confirm/fix/refuse back is the `fix` task's job. The ADR 154 wording rules apply (rule lists, no criteria, "exactly" kept); the prompt wording is recovered from the history (`git show 6de1448:src/models/GeneralPurposeModel.cpp`, the system prompt from `2c37a1a`) and adapted to the shipped profile style. | The decision gate can only judge the text it is given, and blockRecognition re-transcribes from scratch; a mismatch needs a third behavior — confirm, correct or refuse while looking at the candidate. Universal models (qwen3.5, gemma-4) get that role, the original OCR text stays untouched for diff/revert, and a damaged crop gets an explicit "unreadable" verdict instead of a hallucinated transcription. |
| 164  | **Two-layer block verification: the text layer and the correction layer carry independent verdicts.** Every block has two text layers — `box.text` (the OCR result) and `box.correctedText` (the universal model's correction, ADR 163); the **effective text** is the correction when present, else the text. Each layer carries its own verdict + confidence: `checkStatus`/`confidence` for the text, the new `correctedCheckStatus`/`correctedConfidence` for the correction. The transitions (pure `BoxTransitions` functions): any OCR result — page OCR, block recognition, re-recognition — always lands in `box.text` and **erases the correction layer entirely** (text, verdict, confidence) — a repeated recognition overwrites instead of stacking a second variant; a `fix` result lands in `correctedText` and resets only the correction layer's verdict (the text layer's verdict survives — it is about the text); a decision run judges the **effective** layer and erases that layer's previous verdict when it starts; «Отменить исправление» drops the correction layer and the block rolls back to the text layer's state. There is no automatic promotion of `correctedText` → `text`: export, the page-text rebuild and the Markdown preview all use the effective text. The status dot reflects the effective layer: gray = recognized (no verdict on that layer), green/red = Ok/Mismatch, red = Review («повреждён»), gray again with a stored correction but no correction verdict («исправлено»); the confidence badge follows the same layer. The amber state is removed: `BoxCheckStatus::ReRecognized` (value 2) is deleted **without renumbering** — the numeric hole is reserved, and old projects read value 2 as NotChecked on both layers while a stored `correctedText` survives as the correction layer. Supersedes ADR 153's amber two-variant state and its collapse-on-Ok rule. | The old single status conflated "the OCR text was verified" with "the correction was verified": a fix silently invalidated the verdict it kept, and the amber two-variant loop's semantics could not be stated in one sentence. Two layers with independent verdicts make every dot answer exactly one question — is the text you are looking at verified? |
| 165  | **A page carries two independent statuses: the recognition mode (persisted) and the verification aggregate (computed).** `PageRecognitionMode {None, MarkupOnly, RawOcr, MarkupOcr}` is stored per page: the layout pass → MarkupOnly (the page still counts as `recognized` — the batch skips it, as now), a pageOcr result → MarkupOcr, a rawPageOcr result → RawOcr; MarkupOnly becomes MarkupOcr automatically once every non-image block of the page has text. The aggregate is computed, not stored: the subject blocks are the types enabled for auto-verification plus duplicate suspects; the page reads «проверено» only when **every** subject block has a verdict (not NotChecked) on its effective layer — any Mismatch or Review makes it «есть ошибки», otherwise «без ошибок»; no subject blocks → «не проверено». Thumbnails and the page caption show the aggregate when it is complete (green/red marker + the caption), otherwise the recognition mode («Только разметка» / «Распознано» / «Распознано (без разметки)»); the existing edited/duplicate markers stay. Persisted in `.llocr` v2 (`recognitionMode`); v1 projects derive it (recognized + boxes → MarkupOcr, recognized alone → RawOcr). | A bare "recognized" flag answers neither of the two questions the user has — how far this page is in the pipeline and whether it still holds problems; the aggregate over subject blocks makes «проверено» an honest statement (everything that should have been judged was judged) instead of "something was verified somewhere". |
| 166  | **The model settings converge into two windows — «Модели по задачам» and a single model manager; the four per-role model windows are removed.** «Модели по задачам»: one row per task (task — model combo — quant combo; in External mode a model-name field instead of quants), with the Launch/Request tabs of the selected task beneath the table (the existing `LaunchTab`/`RequestTab` components, re-used) — no separate launch/request-parameter windows remain. Saving validates that every chosen quant is physically downloaded; missing ones are offered in a dialog and, on agreement, installed by a sequential queue — each finished install is immediately assigned to its task. The unified model manager lists every profile × quant — installed and available — with download / delete / open-folder; it performs no task assignment (removal of a file any task points at stays refused, now across all six tasks). The four windows «OCR model / Decision model / Block OCR model / Layout model» and their menu entries are removed; the decision threshold stays in the decision task's Request tab. The verification window (autoCheck / autoFix / showConfidence, block types) is untouched. | Six tasks across four look-alike model windows (two more roles would have made six) no longer fits a menu; one window says which model does what, another manages the files, and the per-task Launch/Request tuning follows the task instead of being scattered over windows that differ only in their title. |
| 167  | **The main window's chrome is unified: one toolbar, page-selection scope, a segmented text/preview switch.** The header holds everything in one row: «Файл ▾», the **preset selector stub** («Ручной (Пошаговый)» checked; «Простой текст», «Сложная вёрстка», «Технический / научный» present but disabled, the «Авто-распознавание» button disabled — the automatic pipelines are a later step) and the **pipeline group**: Разметить, Распознать ▾, Проверить ▾, Исправить — while anything runs these and the preset stub go inactive and a Стоп key appears right of «Исправить» (the toolbar itself carries no progress text — the footer keeps it via the `TaskStatus` singleton); on the right — Экспорт ▾ and the ⚙️ icon. **Every pipeline button acts on the thumbnail selection**: an empty selection means the whole document, a fresh document selects its current page (`PageListModel` gains the `selected` role, `selectSingle`/`setSelected`/`selectAll`/`clearSelection`/`isSelected`); plain click selects a page alone, Ctrl/Cmd+click toggles it without moving the current page, «Выбрать все (N)»/«Снять выделение» sits in the column header, and the navigation ‹ › keeps following the current page until the user builds a multi-selection of their own. The buttons carry the scope in their caption («Распознать 3 стр.» / «Распознать все 14»); the scoped C++ entry points are `recognizeScopePages(task)`, `recognizeScopeBlocks()`, `layoutScopePages()`, `checkScopeEnabledBlocks(fixMode)`, `fixScopeProblemBlocks()` (page-list variants on `RecognitionController`/`VerificationQueueController`, recognition of an explicit page set skips only unreadable pages), gated by the new `selectedPageCount`, `scopedProblemBlockCount`, `scopeVerificationSupported`, `scopeBlocksRecognitionSupported` availability properties (the counters ride the deduped `AvailabilityFlags`, so a verdict landing on any page re-announces them). **«Исправить» lights up warm** (`Theme.accentAttention`) with a `(N)` badge only when the scope holds problem blocks — before a check it is disabled, which is what explains the button. The «Распознать ▾» menu is a three-mode picker replacing the old page/document split — «С разметкой» (the full page pass producing blocks and their text, the default), «По размеченным блокам» (the text of the text-less marked boxes), «Без разметки» (raw text) — the chosen item is mirrored onto the button caption and the main click runs it; an item is selectable only while its model (and, for the block pass, text-less boxes in the scope) exist, so the button can never sit on a dead mode; the per-page «Проблемные блоки» entry is gone (the fix pipeline and the block mini-toolbar cover it). The page toolbar collapses to one row — «Стр. ‹ n / N ›» (moved out of the header) plus the block-add stubs and rotations; «Сохранить страницу» merges into Экспорт ▾ («Весь документ…» / «Текущая страница…», both through the export dialog). The right panel's preview Switch becomes a two-segment control «Текст (MD) | Предпросмотр» next to a «Копировать» button (`AppController::copyCurrentPageText`). | The second text row over the page duplicated the header's verbs at page scope, and the per-button «страница/весь документ» dropdowns were the same scope choice repeated five times; one toolbar plus a visible selection answers "what will this act on" once, the lit «Исправить (N)» explains the fix pipeline by itself, and the preset placeholder reserves the auto-pipeline's place without faking it. |
| 168  | **The Models window is one window: quant dropdowns with sizes, per-row gear settings, a files-on-disk block.** The manager window («Модели», the stage-7 all-models list) is **deleted**; «Модели по задачам» is renamed to **«Модели»** and absorbs its duties — the settings menu carries a single «Модели» item, and the ModelInstaller message "pick another one in Settings → Models" is correct again. Each of the six task rows is `task | model ▾ | quant ▾ | ⚙`: the quant dropdown lists **every quantization the profile ships**, each as `✓ Q8_0 — 9.0 GiB (Установлен, макс. качество)` / `⬇ Q4_K_M — 5.6 GiB (Скачать, меньше памяти)` — marker, id, size, status word and a **family hint** derived from the quant id (`ModelQuantModel`: Q8* max quality, Q6_K high quality compact, Q5_K* good quality less memory, Q4_K* less memory, IQ4*/Q4_0/Q3*/Q2* for weak PCs, F16/BF16 full precision); the closed button mirrors the marker (`Q8_0 ✓` / `Q4_K_M ⬇`). **Quant sizes are curated in the profiles, not fetched at runtime**: every `resources/profiles/models/*.json` quant gains `sizeBytes` and the curated set widened to the full HF ladder (Q8_0/Q6_K/Q5_K_M/Q4_K_M + a weak-PC pick; `scripts/fetch-quant-sizes.sh` re-prints the HF tree numbers for future updates), so a size is always present, offline, and the dropdown stays a chosen set — not every file of the repo; an installed quantization overrides the curated number with the measured `entry.byteSize`. The bottom Launch/Request tabs move behind the row's **⚙** — a Popup with the same two tab instances (`role` set on open, drafts reloaded then), so the footer's Restore/Cancel/Save semantics are unchanged. In the tabs' place sits the **«Загруженные файлы моделей на диске» block**: one row per installed registry entry (`InstalledFilesModel`: entryId, title, quant, `sizeLabel` via the new `core/ByteSize` `formatBytes`, `usageCount`/`usageText` "Used by %n task(s)" counting the six `tasks/<task>/modelPath` settings, `removable`/`note` via `removalBlockerFor`) with a 🗑 button — removal goes through `removeInstalledEntry(entryId)` → the same `removeModel` guards (shared mmproj kept, External refused, in-use+Ready refused; in-use-but-stopped asks first, the task rows keep their choice). | Two windows for one concept asked the user to cross-reference them; the dropdown already implied "pick a quantization", so it now says what each costs (GiB and a hint) before the download decision, and the disk block turns "what's eating my storage" from a folder trip into a list with a delete button. Runtime HF tree fetches were rejected: sizes would arrive asynchronously, need the network, and list every file of the repo; the profiles are the app's curated layer and a pinned number per quant matches that philosophy (repos are unpinned, so the script exists for refresh rounds). |
| 169  | **Every task ships an explicit default model: `roles.<task>.default: true` in the profile JSON, exactly one per task.** The mechanism predates this ADR (`ModelProfiles::Role::isDefault`, `defaultIdForRole()` = role flag → profile-level flag → first of the role's ids, and `forRole()` sorts the recommended model first in every picker) — the change made all six shipped defaults explicit and pinned: pageOcr → `unlimited-ocr`, rawPageOcr → `glm-ocr`, blockRecognition → `glm-ocr` (was lfm25-vl-3b), decision → `clef-flash`, layout → `teleocr`, fix → `qwen3.5-4b`; the profile-level `"default"` on unlimited-ocr moved down to its `pageOcr` role, and teleocr's layout role carried an inert `"isDefault"` typo that never parsed — now a real `"default"`. `test_model_preset_catalog::roleDefaultsFollowTheShippedProfiles` pins the table **and** asserts exactly one flagged profile per task (a tie or a gap would silently fall back to first-in-list). This is the data layer for the auto-pipeline step: the model per task = the role's default; the quant = the first profile entry (profile order is quality-descending) whose size (`sizeBytes` curated / `entry.byteSize` installed, ADR 168) fits the machine — that selection logic itself is a later step. | The rework left defaults half-implicit: two roles flagged, three resolved by list order, one flag misspelled and dead. Auto-selection needs a single authoritative answer to "which model for this task", the table must survive frequent model updates (a one-line JSON edit), and uniqueness has to be machine-checked rather than remembered. |
| 170  | **The setup wizard is five steps with real hardware detection and auto-selected models.** Managed flow: Intro (language/theme) → mode choice → **«Оборудование и модели»** → «Вывод» → **«Проверка и запуск»**; the external flow ends at «Вывод» (the summary screen is gone — Finish sits right there; `finishWizard()` writes `setupVersion` inline). The hardware step detects the machine once (`runtime/HardwareInfo`: DXGI adapters on Windows — dxgi.dll loaded dynamically, software adapters skipped, largest DedicatedVideoMemory first; `MTLCreateSystemDefaultDevice` name + `recommendedMaxWorkingSetSize` on macOS; no enumeration on Linux) and shows GPU (or CPU) plus VRAM (or RAM); `ReleaseCatalog::detectPlatform()`'s Windows branch finally maps real adapters — NVIDIA (vendor 0x10DE) → cuda, any other real adapter → vulkan, none → cpu — so `RuntimeInstaller.recommendedBackend` reflects the machine; the backend stays an editable ComboBox. The task table is the shared `Common/TaskModelsTable` (extracted from the Models window, ADR 168 — one implementation of the polished model/quant row sync serves both UIs), prefilled by `ModelInstaller::defaultTaskChoices(budget = -1)`: each task gets its role-default profile (ADR 169), the quant is the first installed ∧ fitting → first installed (no re-download on wizard reopen) → first fitting by `sizeBytes` → last; the budget is **0.9 × (VRAM when the primary adapter reports dedicated memory — an integrated 0-byte adapter is budgeted as CPU-only — else total RAM)**. Only tasks never configured before take the prefill. The check step is one button «Скачать и проверить»: the runtime downloads only when none is installed (an installed one is reused as is), then `queueTaskInstalls` of the missing quants, then the self-test — its success is the only way to Finish. Deleted: StepRuntime, StepModel, StepLaunch (port / memory estimate / command preview remain in Settings → Launch), StepDone. Covered by `test_hardware_info` (backend mapping, budget math) and `defaultTaskChoices*` in `test_model_installer` (defaults table, tight budget, installed preference). | The old wizard walked the user through the platform's homework screen by screen — pick a backend blind, download the runtime, download one model, tune the launch; the machine knows its GPU and the profiles know their defaults, so the wizard presents that answer once, lets the user override any row, and verifies the result. The external-runtime screen is deliberately untouched (a later rework). |
| 171  | **The main-window header is the pipeline toolbar of labeled groups, and the page range is one editable field over the selection.** The row: «Файл / Импорт» (stacked; «Файл» keeps only the project items — open / save / save-as project — while «Импорт» opens the images/PDF/DjVu dialog and carries Ctrl+O), «Страницы» (a text field in the compact 1-based form "1-10, 15"), «Авто» (a Fast/Balanced/Accurate segmented switch over a disabled «Распознать N стр» stub), «Разметка» (Разметить / Распознать блоки), «Распознать» (С разметкой / Без разметки), «Верификация» (Проверить / Исправить (N)), Экспорт and «Настройки» on the right.
